# S7, the reaper: status

The reaper (`middle_end/flambda2/reaper/`) is the whole-unit pass that
removes what is not used and unboxes what can be: a traversal builds a
flow graph of the unit (`traverse.ml`, `traverse_acc.ml`), Datalog rules
are solved over it (`points_to_analysis.ml`, `unboxing_analysis.ml`), and
the unit is rebuilt from the solution (`rebuild.ml`), with the types of
what the unit exports rewritten (`types_rewriter.ml`).

`PARALLEL-PLAN.md` kept S7 for later, with the idea of checking the
reaper's output with `validate_unit`, the analysis left unverified. This
stream does something else, on the human's request, in the way the
semantics treats Simplify: the parts of the reaper are transcribed in
Rocq, what they guarantee is stated in Spec files and proved, the
transcriptions are extracted, and a test tool runs them beside the
compiler's reaper on the same units and compares what the two compute.

- The traversal is a function from a unit to a finite graph
  (`FlambdaReaperTraversal.v`), a transcription of `traverse.ml`. The
  tool compares its graph with the one the compiler builds.
- The solution is an input, read off the compiler's Datalog database,
  as a decision of Simplify is read off the term after. Nothing is
  believed of it: an extracted check (`FlambdaReaperSolution.v`) tests
  that it is closed under the rules, and is proved to imply `closed`
  (`FlambdaReaperGraphSpec.v`). The least solution, which the engine
  computes, passes.
- The rebuild is a function of the unit and the solution
  (`FlambdaReaperRebuild.v`), a transcription of `rebuild.ml`. The tool
  compares its term with the one the compiler's reaper gives, up to
  bound names (`FlambdaCompareSpec.expr_eqb`), and runs both on the
  machine.
- The statement is that a unit rebuilt from a solution that passes the
  check refines the unit, run on its own (`program_refines`, or a
  relation of exits up to what can be observed, if the removal of
  unused value slots of escaping closures needs it).

A model of the same reaper on a language of its own was written before
this stream, outside this tree, with the proofs of the same statements
for that language and the counterexamples that it found. This stream
carries it over to the semantics of this tree, one part at a time.

## The plan

1. The flow graph and the rules (`FlambdaReaperGraphSpec.v`): the
   nodes, the fields, the relations of the graph, the tables of the
   solution, what it is for the tables to be closed under the rules of
   `points_to_analysis.ml`, and the finite forms of a graph and of a
   solution that the transcriptions and the tool exchange.
2. The traversal (`FlambdaReaperTraversal.v`), extracted.
3. The check of a solution (`FlambdaReaperSolution.v`, its Spec and its
   proofs), extracted.
4. The rebuild without changes of calling convention
   (`FlambdaReaperRebuild.v`): unused `Let`s removed, unused fields of
   blocks and unused boxed numbers made poison, unused value slots
   removed, unused parameters of continuations removed. Extracted.
5. The test tool and its examples: the tool runs Simplify and then the
   compiler's reaper on each example, converts the unit, the graph, the
   solution and the result to the model, runs the three transcriptions
   and compares.
6. The meaning of a solution (`FlambdaReaperFlowSpec.v` and its proofs):
   a relation between the values of the machine
   (`FlambdaSemanticsSpec.step`) and the nodes, kept by every step of a
   unit whose solution passes the check. The machine has no identity
   of allocations, so the relation says which allocation a value may
   come from rather than reading it off the value. Then the statement
   of the rebuild, and its proof.
7. Then, one at a time: changes of calling convention, unboxing of
   blocks and of closures, tupled code (`traverse_acc.ml`,
   `create_unknown_arity_tupled_call_witnesses`, with the edge that
   keeps a tuple alive, and an example that shows the edge is needed),
   exceptions, partial and over-applications, and the rewriting of the
   types.

## Done

- Step 1: `FlambdaReaperGraphSpec.v`.
- Step 2: `FlambdaReaperTraversal.v` (`traverse_unit`), with five
  examples; its header lists where it differs from `traverse.ml`.
- Step 3: `FlambdaReaperSolution.v` (`check_closed`,
  `check_field_used_exact`), its Spec and its proofs: what passes the
  check is closed, and meets `field_used_exact`; an example that is a
  least solution, and two that are not, show that neither statement
  holds for nothing.
- Step 4: `FlambdaReaperRebuild.v` (`rebuild_unit`,
  `rebuild_unit_from_tables`), the rebuild with the flag
  `-no-reaper-change-calling-conventions`, with three examples; its
  header lists where it differs from `rebuild.ml`. One difference is for
  the proof to settle: where the compiler drops the callee of a call
  that has no use (`rewrite_simple_opt`), the model, which has no call
  without a callee, keeps it, and its binding may have been removed.
  That happens only in code whose code id has no `any_usage`, which
  the proof is to show is never run. In the compiler the same case is
  what makes it stop with "Missing callee" on unused code (the model
  of the earlier work found the cause: every code is rebuilt, used or
  not).
- Step 6, first slice (`FlambdaReaperFlowSpec.v` and its proofs): the
  meaning of a solution, for units with no call, no closure and no
  code (`in_flow_fragment`). A value may be at a node ([flows]) if one
  of the allocations it may come from, existential since the machine
  has no identity of allocations, holds in each field what may be at
  the node of that field; every state that a unit reaches from empty
  environments, under a solution that passes `check_closed`, has each
  variable's value at the variable's node, and an exit's values at the
  nodes of the unit's continuations (`flow_sound`). What the rebuild
  will need follows: a bound variable has a source, and a read with a
  use makes the field read used in every allocation it may come from.
- Step 6, second slice: closures (`Closure_of_set`, the projections of
  value and function slots, static sets of closures) and direct calls,
  under the hypotheses that direct calls are full, of code that is not
  tupled, and with no extra argument for the exception continuation. A
  closure may be at a node through allocations of its set, one per
  function slot, each with its call witness; a call reads the witness
  through the closure, which makes the code it calls used, so the facts
  made conditional on that code hold. Local fields are covered only for
  units with no projection: with one, the statement is false, and
  `local_slot_counterexample` shows it on the machine. A closure stored
  in an immutable array and loaded back reaches its loaded variable by
  a use, not an alias (the default case of `traverse_prim`); a value
  slot then read from it through a local field gets no source, and the
  rebuild makes code that runs `Invalid`. The compiler does the same:
  the earlier model found it, and it was reproduced at `-O4` with a
  closure put in `[: clo :]` and read back with `Iarray.get`. Next
  slice: calls of unknown arity, partial and over-applications, and
  exceptions.
- Step 6, third slice: calls of unknown arity (`Indirect`), partial
  applications as values, over-applications, and exception
  continuations with extra arguments. A partial application may be at
  a node through a chain of unknown-arity witnesses, one per group of
  arguments given, matched group by group against the code's chain,
  each argument at the parameter at its place. The proof needs one
  fact of the machine that the graph cannot say: a partial application
  has fewer groups of arguments than its code has parameters (only
  `shape_of_code_call` makes one); without it a saturated partial
  application would route results and exceptions to the wrong call.
  Code with no parameter is out of the fragment: the traversal gives
  it one witness where the compiler stops with a fatal error, and the
  machine runs a call of it with arguments as an over-application,
  which the graph does not follow.
- Step 6, indirect calls of tupled code: the tuple that every call
  gives tupled code reaches the variable standing for it through the
  call's witness, and each field the code's parameter it is read into
  (`create_unknown_arity_tupled_call_witnesses`; the machine's
  `tuple_fields`). The two facts of the fix of #7072 only add uses, and
  the proof does not read them: `flow_sound_without_keep_alive` is the
  same statement for the graph without them, and
  `tupled_reverted_analysis_sound` says that, on the unit of
  `FlambdaReaperTupledSpec.v`, the solution whose rebuild goes wrong is
  a sound analysis. So what was unsound before the fix is the rebuild,
  which gives the code poison for a tuple whose fields it reads, not
  the analysis. Out of the fragment: direct calls of tupled code (no
  call kind of the model gives code the fields), and tupled code whose
  parameters are unboxed products.
- The statement of the rebuild, for the first fragment
  (`FlambdaReaperRebuildSpec.v` and its proofs): for a unit with no
  closure, no call and no code (`closure_free`), a solution that passes
  `check_closed` and `check_field_used_exact`, a unit whose names are in
  scope (`well_formed_unit`) and which gives its two continuations one
  value each (`exits_one_value`), the rebuilt unit refines the unit
  (`rebuild_refines`), exits compared exactly: a block that reaches an
  exit has all its fields used, and a symbol is compared as a symbol.
  Each of the two premises that are not about the solution's closure is
  shown needed: a unit that exits with two values has its second made
  poison (`exits_one_value_needed`), and a field said used though no
  rule gives it makes the rebuild box poison, which goes wrong
  (`field_used_exact_needed`). The first is for `C0-well-formed.md`:
  `well_formed_unit` does not count the values given to the unit's
  continuations, and where the compiler's traversal would stop
  (`List.iter2` in `apply_cont_deps`) the model's drops what is beyond.
- Why the statement of the rebuild does not yet go beyond the first
  fragment, with two counterexamples in `FlambdaReaperRebuildSpec.v`,
  each a unit that meets every premise of `rebuild_refines` but
  `closure_free`, whose rebuild does not refine it:
  - `closure_exit_counterexample`: a closure reaches an exit with a
    value slot that no projection names; the rebuild removes the slot,
    and the exit, which `refines` compares exactly, differs. What an
    exit is to be compared up to is a choice to make: the outside
    world cannot read a value slot (with local fields), so the
    statement for closures is to compare exits up to value slots, or
    to ask that no closure reach an exit, which a unit that raises a
    closure in an exception does not meet.
  - `wrapper_kinds_counterexample`: code whose body returns a value of
    another kind than its result arity says; the machine tests the
    arities of the call against those of the code, not the value
    returned, and the wrapper continuation of the rebuild tests the
    value against the call's return arity, so the rebuilt unit goes
    wrong. The premise needed, that what code returns has the kinds of
    its result arity, is one that `well_kinded_unit` gives and that no
    statement may take; a check of the term for it is for
    `C0-well-formed.md`.
- The statement for closures, chosen and stated
  (`FlambdaReaperRebuildSpec.v`): exits are compared up to what the
  outside world observes (`observed_equal`: closures without their
  value slots, through blocks, arrays, boxed numbers and partial
  applications; `program_refines_observably`), and a check of the term
  asks that what code returns have the kinds of its result arity, and
  the continuation a call returns to the kinds of the call's return
  arity (`results_kinded`). `rebuild_refines_observably` is proved for
  the first fragment, from `rebuild_refines`; the closure of
  `closure_exit_counterexample` refines up to observation, and the
  unit of `wrapper_kinds_counterexample` fails `results_kinded`. To
  prove it of closures, projections, static sets, code and direct
  calls is what is next; the Spec header lists what that needs.
  Comparing exits up to value slots is for review: it is the reaper's
  own reading of what a unit gives the outside world, and it is not
  `refines`.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with closures
  (`no_code_no_slots`): sets of closures of several functions with no
  value slot, projections of function slots, static sets of closures,
  deleted code; with an example of each, rebuilt. A set whose closure
  has a source never becomes `Invalid` in a run, and a static set is
  kept whole where one of its symbols has a use.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with value slots (`no_code`):
  sets of closures and static sets with value slots, and projections of
  value slots. The closures of the two runs are related as they are
  observed: the rebuilt closure has the slots the rebuild keeps, with
  related values; the example (`value_slots_example`) exits with a
  closure that has lost an unread slot, observed equal but not equal.
  A projection of a value slot into a variable of kind `Region` or
  `Rec_info` is left out: the rebuild keeps such a variable whether or
  not it is used, and not the slot it reads. The compiler makes no
  value slot of those kinds: closure conversion captures neither, and
  the backend stops on one (`to_cmm_set_of_closures.ml`).
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units that bind code without calling
  it (`no_call`): the rebuild keeps the code of a code id with a use,
  rebuilt, and deletes the others (`code_example`).
- `exits_one_value` now checks calls too: a call to one of the unit's
  continuations returns one value, and passes no extra argument to its
  exception continuation. A call of code that returns two values to
  the unit's return continuation has its second result made poison
  (`call_exits_one_value_needed`). The compiler's units meet it, as a
  call returns what its continuation takes; a row for
  `C0-well-formed.md`, with the one for `Apply_cont`.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with direct tail calls
  (`direct_tail_calls`): a direct call whose continuations lead out of
  the unit and which returns one value. The rebuild passes poison for
  a parameter with no use, and every argument it keeps has a use, so
  its variable is still bound: an escaping closure, which blocks the
  usage of the parameters through its witness, still has its arguments
  aliased to the parameters by a rule with no guard
  (`tail_call_example`).
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with direct calls that
  return to a handler of the unit (`direct_calls`), through the wrapper
  that the rebuild gives a call whose handler drops results
  (`wrapper_example`). Of `results_kinded` the proof uses only that the
  return arity of a call is that of its handler: the rebuild keeps the
  kinds of values, so the handler's own test of kinds gives the
  wrapper's.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units whose calls are all direct
  (`only_direct_calls`), whatever they return or raise to: a call may
  raise to a handler of the unit with extra arguments, of which the
  rebuild removes those the handler drops, from the call and from the
  handler, and keeps the exception (`raise_example`). An exception
  reaches its handler whole, and leaves the unit only through the
  unit's exception continuation, where it is compared as any exit. The
  machine raises only where the term does: an exception that a call
  raises on its own, such as `Sys.Break`, is not in the model, and the
  statement says nothing of it.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units whose indirect calls are full
  calls of code that is not tupled (`full_calls`): the rebuild passes
  poison for an argument whose parameter no code the callee may be
  uses, following the chains of call witnesses from what the callee
  may come from (`indirect_example`).
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with partial applications
  and over-applications of code that is not tupled (`generic_calls`):
  one proof for the shapes of a call of a closure, full, over and
  partial, with the arguments that a partial application holds
  (`partial_call_example`). `observed_equal` now compares the
  arguments of a partial application by kind only, as it compares
  closures without their value slots: a unit that exits with a partial
  application of code that does not use the argument it holds exits,
  rebuilt, with poison in its place, which only that code can read
  (`partial_exit_counterexample`; the former relation is
  `observed_equal_with_arguments`). A choice of what is observable, for
  the human to confirm.
- `rebuild_refines_observably` for units with indirect calls of tupled
  code, full or over-applied (`tupled_calls`, `tupled_call_example`):
  the two facts of #7072 give the tuple a use, so the rebuild keeps it,
  with poison in the fields that the code does not use; without them
  the rebuild of the example goes wrong. The machine reads every field
  of a tuple, used or not, so the relation of the two runs says that an
  unused field of a block is poison in the rebuilt run. That ends the
  calls. What the statement still asks that is not about the solution:
  `in_flow_fragment`, which leaves out the older forms of sets of
  closures and direct calls of tupled code (the compiler's, which give
  the fields, are not in the model); `tupled_calls`, which also leaves
  out static boxed numbers, strings and immutable arrays, which the
  compiler's units have, and arguments and projections of kind `Region`
  or `Rec_info`; and, met by the compiler's units, `well_formed_unit`,
  `exits_one_value` and `results_kinded`; and no projection where
  fields are local (`local_slot_counterexample`).
- Static boxed numbers, strings and immutable arrays of values
  (`static_constants`, `static_constants_example`), with the flow
  invariant widened to them. A static boxed number that holds a
  variable is left out, and must be: the traversal aliases its symbol
  to the constants and gives the variable no use, so the rebuild boxes
  zero in its place (`static_boxed_variable_needed`). The compiler does
  the same: in classic mode, closure conversion makes a static float
  block of a top-level record of floats with variables as its fields,
  and with `-Oclassic -flambda2-reaper -X reaper-oclassic=1` a program
  that prints `2.500000 1.000000` prints `0.000000 0.000000`
  (`traverse.ml` gives such fields no use; `rebuild.ml` writes zero).
  The commit "reaper: fix a few bugs", ported here, fixes it: the
  traversal makes the variables of static constants uses. The model's
  traversal follows (a boxed number of a constant has its field from
  the constants; one of a variable, whose kind the model does not
  record, is aliased to the constants and uses the variable), and the
  rebuilt unit of the example now exits with 2.5
  (`static_boxed_variable_example`), and `static_constants` admits
  every static boxed number, of a constant or of a variable.
- Toward the change of calling conventions: the simulation of the
  rebuild now runs on `rebuild_unit_cc`, under the hypothesis, for now,
  that no code changes, which the plain statements meet; three examples
  (`two_calls_example`, `poison_result_example`,
  `masked_return_example`) rebuild units where code loses parameters
  and results, called directly, through the wrapper, and in tail
  position, and their runs exit as the units do. Next: drop the
  hypothesis for code that makes no call in tail position, then for
  all code.
- Tupled code and the fix of #7072 (`FlambdaReaperTupledSpec.v`, its
  proofs, and the example `tests/semantics/reaper/
  tupled_unused_components.fl`): on a unit that gives tupled code a
  pair whose components it does not use, at unknown arity, the
  rebuild with the two facts that keep the tuple alive refines the
  unit (`program_refines`), and without them, the traversal as it was
  before the fix, the least solution passes the check and the rebuilt
  unit goes wrong, as the machine reads the fields of a tuple that has
  become poison: the model tells that reverting the fix is unsound.

- The change of calling conventions, with unboxing off
  (`FlambdaReaperCallingConventions.v`, its Spec and its proofs),
  extracted: the rules of `calling_convention_changes_rules` as a check
  (`check_cc_closed`, proved to give the closure of the rules) of the
  table `cannot_change_calling_convention`, which the compiler does not
  export and the tool is to compute from the decisions it does export;
  the least such table (`derive_cannot_change`); the decisions of each
  code, Keep or Delete for its parameters and its results
  (`compute_code_changes`); and `rebuild_unit_cc`, the rebuild with
  them, which is `rebuild_unit` where no code may change
  (`rebuild_unit_cc_unchanged`).
- The test tool in that mode: each example is run a second time with
  the change of calling conventions on. The model's table passes
  `check_cc_closed`, and so does the compiler's, read off
  `Unboxing_analysis` with its graph; the tables, the decisions, the
  rebuilt terms and the runs agree on every example, except
  `cc_known_arity.fl`, where the model keeps the calling convention of
  code the compiler changes after a call of known arity (see the call
  kinds below). Four examples exercise the change (`cc_*.fl`).
- The tool on the whole corpus (`tests/semantics/{*,ocaml/*,reaper/*}.fl`,
  both modes) and on units compiled from OCaml: the compiler's solution
  and table pass the checks everywhere; the model and the compiler
  differ only on calls of known arity, and the runs only on units that
  re-simplify code with `newer_version_of` read from text
  (`value_slot_newer_version.fl`): the reader gives code empty free
  names (`fexpr_to_flambda.ml`), Simplify takes such code as simplified
  and does not register its projections, and the reaper drops the slot
  they read. From OCaml source the slot is kept. The rebuild's sources
  of a field (`get_field_sources`) are predicates built on the previous
  level's, so a call of unknown arity with k groups of arguments costs
  a power of the number of facts; the tool skips the model's rebuild
  over a budget (`FLREAPER_REBUILD_BUDGET`). Next: compute each level
  as a list, once.
- The unboxing of blocks and boxed numbers
  (`FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v`, its Spec and its proofs), extracted: the
  names dominated by one allocation point and the allocations to unbox
  as checks of tables (`check_dominated`, `check_to_unbox`, each proved
  to agree with the rules of `unboxing_analysis.ml` both ways), the
  unboxed fields of each name, the decisions with `Unbox`, and
  `rebuild_unit_unbox`. The case `Keep _, Unbox _` of
  `make_apply_wrapper` is transcribed as the compiler has it, an
  `Invalid` handler; an example shows the rebuilt unit undefined where
  the unit exits.
- The unboxing of closures, in the same file: the sets of closures
  whose representation changes, as a check of a table
  (`check_to_change_representation`, proved to agree with the rules
  both ways, and `check_sets_whole`: the closures of a set with a use
  are unboxed alike), the new representation of each, the decision on
  `my_closure` of each code, and `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr`, which
  rebuilds sets with new slots, unboxed sets and code that takes the
  fields of its closure as parameters. The model picks its own names
  for the new slots. The test tool relates the compiler's new slots to
  the model's, through the two representations, and compares the sets
  whose representation changes, the representations, the decisions on
  `my_closure`, the terms and the runs: the units that unbox closures
  with lambda lifting agree on all of them, but for the runs, undefined
  on both sides as the machine calls the closure that the callee is,
  and the rebuild makes the callee of code that takes the fields of its
  closure poison; compiled from OCaml they print what they print
  without the reaper. A unit that gives a `Block_representation` to one
  of its names (the closure of a partial application) is out of the
  model.
- The test tool in the mode with unboxing: the model and the compiler
  agree on the dominated names, the allocations to unbox, the unboxed
  fields, the decisions, the terms and the runs on every example, but
  for the known differences (calls of known arity, the text artefact).
  The compiler renames the slots of most closures; the tool undoes it.
  Five examples unbox blocks (`unbox_*.fl`). One, `unbox_kept_result.fl`,
  is the case `Keep _, Unbox _` of `make_apply_wrapper`: the compiler's
  reaper makes a call that returns into one that never does, and the
  rebuilt unit is undefined where the unit returns 10. It is a
  miscompile from OCaml too, with the default flags of the reaper: a
  closure parameter given, on one path, a pair whose field another path
  reads (`make 9`, cast with a `%identity` external), and called on
  none, makes `-flambda2-reaper` print nothing where the program prints
  10 without it or with `-no-reaper-unbox`. In Rocq,
  `kept_result_unboxed`: the tables pass every check of the model, the
  unit exits with 10 and its rebuild goes wrong. The fix proposed is a
  seed of `cannot_unbox0`, beside the rule for results of code: an
  allocation that code returns is unboxed only if every name that a
  call reads it into is unboxed with it (`check_result_receivers`,
  which rejects these tables and accepts those of the sound examples;
  the tables without that allocation rebuild into a unit that exits
  with 10).
- The corpus in the three modes, with and without local fields and
  lambda lifting, finds no other miscompile; nor do the 493 OCaml
  sources of `tests/semantics/ocaml/`, compiled with and without the
  reaper and run. The model and the compiler differ, besides calls of
  known arity, on: empty arrays (the compiler aliases `Empty_array` to
  the constants, the model gave the symbol constructors), closures
  other than the first of a set (which the compiler finds dominated by
  their allocation, and the model did not, as it made a node for them).
  The traversal of the model now does as the compiler on both; what is
  left is one accessor fact for each closure that a projection binds,
  which a term that binds every closure of a set at once, as the
  compiler's does, would remove (a request to the core). The
  conversion reads the end of a region as the unit value, which the
  model's traversal takes for a constant and the compiler's for a
  primitive with effects; the tool gives the model such a binding as
  `Opaque_identity`, which the model treats as the compiler does, and
  compares sets of closures up to the order of their slots. Eight units
  still differ in their terms, each for a reason above. Run again after
  the fixes, in four configurations of flags (659 units), the tables of
  the unboxing agree on every unit; graphs differ only by the accessor
  facts, the text artefact and calls of known arity, and the runs only
  for the text artefact, `unbox_kept_result`, and units whose rebuild
  calls with no callee, which the machine does not run.
- Random OCaml programs (about 1,650: closures, mutual recursion,
  tupled code, partial and over-applications, blocks, boxed numbers,
  exceptions), compiled in six configurations of the reaper and run,
  print what they print without it; and so do 1,700 more, made so that
  the reaper unboxes and changes calling conventions in nearly all,
  with local allocations, unboxed products and records, mixed blocks,
  first-class modules, GADTs, objects, lazy values and recursive
  values (their digests of floats, at first, kept only the low bits,
  which round floats lack; 431 more, in classic mode and at `-O2` and
  `-O3`, with a full digest, find only the bug of static float blocks
  below). 26 of the first make the compiler stop (and 124 of the
  others, for the same cause), all with
  lambda lifting (`-X reaper-lambda-lifting=1`) and all for one cause:
  where inlining makes two sets of closures of one code, each closure
  is unboxed to no field, but the code's `my_closure`, dead and with
  two sources, is not; the call's decision is then `Keep_my_closure`,
  and `rebuild_apply` keeps the callee, whose binding the unboxing
  removed ("Incorrect free names", or a variable not found in
  `To_cmm`). Five lines of OCaml show it: a function, inlined at its one
  call, that calls a local function `[@inline never][@local never]`
  closing over its parameter and drops the result. The model shared
  the bug (`lambda_lifting_dead_callee.fl`, run with the flags its
  first line gives). The commit "reaper: fix a few bugs", ported here,
  fixes it: `rewrite_simple_opt` drops a callee that has unboxed
  fields. The transcription follows it, and in Rocq
  (`lambda_lifting_dead_callee`), on a unit that makes two closures of
  one code and calls through each, the rebuild with lambda lifting has
  no free variable left and exits as the unit does once its calls are
  given a closure of the code; the machine itself finds a call with no
  callee undefined (see below). On the fixed compiler, 700 programs
  agree in every configuration, lambda lifting included, and the
  reproducers of both bugs that the commit fixes run as without the
  reaper.
- The unboxing refines, for local unboxing
  (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesSpec.v` and its proofs): on a unit
  without closures whose unboxed blocks and boxed numbers are only
  read where they are bound (`local_unboxing`),
  `rebuild_unit_unbox` refines the plain rebuild exactly
  (`rebuild_unbox_refines_rebuild`), by a simulation between the two
  rebuilt terms that knows nothing of the reaper, and so the unit
  observably (`rebuild_unbox_refines_observably`, with
  `local_example`). Of the solution, it asks besides `check_closed`
  that a read the rebuild keeps find its field among the unboxed ones,
  which is the absence of the compiler's fatal error in
  `rewrite_field_access`, and which a closed solution that is not
  least may break, in code that never runs (`unboxed_reads_example`):
  as a check of the tables (`check_unboxed_reads`), which the tables of
  every example pass, proved to give what the proof needs. The
  fragment now also has unboxed names given to
  continuations whose parameters are unboxed alike, loops among them,
  aliases, and a block unboxed in a block (`continuation_example`,
  `loop_example`, `alias_example`, `nested_example`). That an unboxed
  name is never given to a kept parameter that is not unboxed does not
  follow from the checks: a closed solution with a spurious use of a
  parameter passes them, and the rebuild gives it poison
  (`spurious_keep_example`), harmlessly, as a real use would forbid the
  unboxing; the fragment asks it. Unboxed fields nest to any depth
  (`deep_example`, `nested_param_example`, `nested_alias_example`).
  Toward calls: where nothing is unboxed, the rebuild with unboxing is
  the rebuild with the change of calling conventions, term for term
  (`rebuild_unbox_without_unboxing`), and so refines observably;
  `unboxed_call_example` and `unboxed_wrapper_example` rebuild code
  whose parameter and result are unboxed, directly and through the
  wrapper, into units that exit as the unit does.
- The unboxing across calls, in the mode of the fix and in that of its
  variant (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCalls*.v`): the unit rebuilt
  with unboxing in the mode of the fix refines the unit rebuilt with the
  change of calling conventions alone from the same table
  (`rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc`), and so the unit up to observation
  (`rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_observably`). The variant that passes a
  kept result through is now a mode of the model (`tail`,
  `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed_tail`), and the same two statements
  hold of it (`rebuild_unbox_fixed_tail_refines_cc`,
  `rebuild_unbox_fixed_tail_refines_observably`). The fragment
  (`calls_unboxing`) has code with any number of groups of parameters
  and of results that changes its calling convention, unboxed as its
  code dep says, or keeps it (not tupled, no result unboxed, its return
  continuation of fixed arity, kept with or without a use), and direct
  calls of it, in the unit and in code, that return to a handler or, in
  tail position, to the return continuation of the code they are in.
  Code may raise, by an `Apply_cont` or a call: its exception
  continuation, which the first rebuild keeps with no mask, has the
  decision of a continuation of fixed arity (`code_exn_decision`: kept,
  with a use or with none, so that the code raises poison where the
  exception has no use; not unboxed). The exception continuation of a
  call (`exn_cont_ok`) is a way out of the unit or that of the code the
  call is in, with no extra argument, or a handler of the unit, with an
  extra argument for each of its parameters after the first, given as
  the arguments of an `Apply_cont` are (`get_extra_args`: kept, poison
  where kept with no use, its fields where unboxed, dropped where
  deleted, by the decisions of the handler, and by its mask after the
  first parameter in the first rebuild). The way out, the exception
  continuation of code and the first parameter of the handler take the
  exception of the callee as the parameter of the continuation of a call
  takes a result (`exn_param_ok`): the first rebuild keeps them, they
  are not unboxed, and one with a use takes only an exception raised
  with a use, so that a dead exception, poison in the second run, never
  leaves the unit (in the simulation, `cs_exn`, `exn_call_rel`, and
  `cont_rel_extra` for a handler with extra arguments). In the model of
  the traversal the first parameter of an exception handler has a use
  wherever the code it is in has one (`exn_handler_deps`), so that with
  a closed table only dead code raises with no use. A call of code that
  keeps its calling convention gives the arguments that the callee uses
  as they are and the others as poison, in both rebuilds, which must
  read the same uses; an argument given as poison may be an unboxed
  name, which both rebuilds give as the poison of its kind
  (`kept_args_ok`, `masked_args_ok`, `args_rel2_mask`). Calls whose code
  is not known are in the fragment too (`ind_call_ok`): of unknown
  arity, or of known arity with no one code id that the solution gives,
  by a used variable, full calls, partial applications and
  over-applications, the callee a closure or a partial application.
  Both rebuilds give the arguments at the mask of the call
  (`call_mask`) and expect every result. As the simulation relates the
  two rebuilt units with no flow invariant, a call may reach any code of
  the unit, so every code must keep its calling convention, return a
  result of the arity of the call as the continuation takes it, and
  return a single result of kind `Value`, which an over-application
  calls, with a use (`ind_code_ok`); the exception continuation takes
  the exception with no use if some code raises with none
  (`ind_exn_decision`). In the simulation such a call is made at a shape
  that every code fits (`call_target`, `ind_targets`, `ind_compat`): the
  code keeps its calling convention, so that the two runs' code have the
  same arities (`cs_keeps`, a field of `code_shape`, and a conjunct of
  `code_frame`), and the call is in both a full call, an
  over-application or a partial application alike
  (`closure_call_answered`); what is left of an over-application is
  related (`cont_rel_over`) and called as such a call in turn. The
  arguments are related group by group (`call_args_groups`), and the
  wrappers of the two rebuilds result by result (`result_seg`,
  `results_seg`): where the change of calling conventions makes a
  wrapper, the rebuild with unboxing makes one too, as its wrapper
  passes each result as it is only where the first does. Through tail
  calls, the relation lets the return continuation of code be a handler
  under any number of wrappers of either run. The fix and its variant
  differ only where a call returns to a parameter kept with no use whose
  result the callee keeps: in the model, a call in tail position from
  code that keeps its calling convention and whose result has no use.
  The fix makes it return to a wrapper that gives poison, so that it is
  no longer in tail position, and the variant leaves it as the change of
  calling conventions does; both refine, the parameter having no use
  (`tail_example`: code 51 calls code 50 in tail position, with the
  least solution). The regression of the fix is one of space, the
  `Stack_overflow` of `fix_tail_call.fl`, which the model, whose
  continuations are not bounded, does not see. Code 50 of `exn_example`
  raises its parameter to a handler of the unit installed by a trap
  action, with two extra arguments, the second for a parameter with no
  use, which both rebuilds and the variant drop alike.
  `poison_arg_example`: the rules of the fix unbox a pair given to
  escaping code (which cannot change its calling convention) at a
  parameter with no use, the mask of the call keeps no argument, and the
  rebuilt unit, which gives the call poison, refines the unit (least
  solution). `escaping_argument_calls`: the unit of the compiler's fault
  `escaping_parameter_argument.fl` (an unboxed argument at a parameter
  with a use of code that cannot change its calling convention, a
  parameter that the code calls at unknown arity) is of the fragment
  with the tables of the rules of the fix, which unbox nothing, and its
  rebuild refines it; with those of the branch the argument is unboxed,
  the mask of the call keeps it, and the unit is not of the fragment:
  the premises exclude the fault and are met by the fixed tables. No
  miscompile of either mode found in the fragment. `FlambdaExtract.v` is
  to extract `FlambdaReaperUnboxing.rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed_tail`
  beside `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed`. Next: calls whose code is not
  known in units where some code changes its calling convention, which
  needs the flow invariant (that such a call reaches only code in
  `cannot_change`, by `check_cc_closed`) carried across the two rebuilt
  units, and over-applications of code whose result has no use.
- The unboxing of closures refines, for changes of representation
  (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosures*.v`): on a unit of the fragment
  `repr_unboxing`, the rebuild with unboxing and changes of
  representation, in the mode of the fix and in that of its variant
  (`rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed`, `_fixed_tail`, any table `tcr`),
  refines the unit rebuilt with the change of calling conventions alone
  (`rebuild_repr_fixed_refines_cc`, `rebuild_repr_fixed_tail_refines_cc`),
  and so the unit up to observation (`..._refines_observably`, composed
  with `rebuild_cc_refines_observably`). The relation of the simulation
  (`rr`, in the SimProofs file) has three classes of names: plain, the
  same value in both runs; unboxed, as in the local unboxing; and
  changed (`crel`): the closure of the first run, at the function slot of
  its representation, and the closure of the second, at the new function
  slot, with the declarations renamed and, at the new value slots of each
  field of the representation, the value of the old slot or, for a block
  unboxed in turn, the values at the paths of its fields. A read of a
  slot of a changed closure reads the new slot (`rr_proj`), or the new
  slots of the fields of an unboxed value (`rr_proj_unboxed`); a call
  through a changed closure binds the `my_closure` of the code, changed
  alike, to the closure of its run. The fragment: unboxed blocks and
  boxed numbers bound and read as in `local_unboxing`, sets that do not
  change with plain value slots, sets that change at the function slot
  of their representation, whose fields are value slots that the first
  rebuild keeps (a use in a closure of the set) holding a plain simple
  with a use or a name unboxed alike, whose new slots are distinct and
  whose new function slot finds the code of the old (`changed_set_ok`,
  `repr_field_ok`: checks of the tables, not derived from the rules);
  code that changes its calling convention with `Keep`/`Delete`
  decisions, keeps its `my_closure` and does not raise; direct full calls
  with plain arguments by a callee of the class of the `my_closure` of
  the code, returning where neither rebuild makes a wrapper; handlers
  with plain parameters and no `Keep_unused`. Left out: unboxed
  parameters, wrappers, raising, code that keeps its calling convention,
  projections of other closures of a set, static sets that change.
  `block_in_slot_example` (the unit of `FlambdaReaperUnboxing.Examples`)
  and `two_fields_example` (the unit of `unbox_block_in_value_slot.fl`,
  a block of two fields unboxed into two new slots, tables of the least
  solution, returns 47 before and after) are the statements applied.
  `static_set_example`: the static set of the examples, whose
  representation changes, rebuilt, returns 7 as the unit does (by runs;
  the statement for every unit would have static environments that
  differ by the changed symbols). The unboxing of closures
  (`Unbox_my_closure`): the machine runs a call by its callee, and the
  rebuild gives the call of code that takes the fields of its closure a
  poison callee, so that `unboxed_closure` returns 7 and its rebuild goes
  wrong, and no refinement holds on this machine
  (`unboxed_closure_goes_wrong`). On a machine with calls that have no
  callee, defined in the Spec file (`step_lifted`: a direct call of
  poison runs the code it names, its `my_closure` bound to the poison,
  as the compiler's call with no callee does), the rebuilt unit returns
  7 (`unboxed_closure_lifted`). The statement for every unit is left for
  when the core machine has calls with no callee (the request below): it
  would relate a closure of the first run to the variables of its fields
  in the second, as an unboxed block is, and the `my_closure` of code
  that unboxes it to the parameters of its fields.
- The change of calling conventions refines, for code whose calls
  return to handlers (`rebuild_cc_refines_observably`, on
  `cc_fragment`; `two_calls_refines`). A closed table is not enough: a
  solution with a source on the witness that a dead call reads off a
  closure makes `code_id_actually_directly_called` keep the old calling
  convention for a live call of code that changes, and the rebuilt unit
  goes wrong (`spurious_widget_counterexample`); the statement asks
  that the witnesses read off one node have the same sources
  (`check_apply_widgets`), which the least solution meets, and that
  each witness give one code id (`check_witness_code_ids`). The
  statement now covers calls that return out of the unit and calls in
  tail position, through chains of code that changes, with or without a
  wrapper over the enclosing return continuation
  (`poison_result_refines`, `masked_return_refines`), and to code that
  does not change or of unknown arity, through the plain wrapper over
  the return continuation (`tail_escape_refines`). Its premises are
  those of `rebuild_refines_observably_statement`, with
  `check_cc_closed` and `check_apply_widgets`: that each witness of a
  call gives one code id is proved of the traversal of every unit
  (`traverse_witness_code_ids`).
- The commit "reaper: fix miscompilation with unused unboxed variables
  (#7956)", ported here, fixes the case `Delete, Unbox _` of
  `make_apply_wrapper` (#7952): a result that the continuation of a
  call deletes is dropped whatever the callee's decision. The
  transcription follows (`unbox_dead`: the unit of the compiler's test,
  its tables passing every check, rebuilds into a unit that exits as
  it does, where the match before the fix makes the wrapper `Invalid`
  and the unit go wrong). The case `Keep _, Unbox _`, which
  `kept_result_unboxed` and `kept.ml` show, is another, and still a
  miscompile with both commits ported. It needs no cast: with a GADT
  whose constructors make the join's parameter a pair on one path and a
  function on the other, called only under the refinement, a type-safe
  program prints nothing with `-O3 -flambda2-reaper` (default flags of
  the reaper) where it prints 10 without it
  (`unbox_kept_result_gadt.fl`, whose header gives the program; the
  model shares the miscompile). The join's parameter must be only
  called, not read, not escaping, which in a type-safe program takes a
  refinement of its type (a GADT, or `Type.eq`); with lambda lifting
  and result types for all functions, a closure returned by code does
  it too. Random programs built around such joins (700) hit it in 159,
  with results of every shape (pairs, records, floats, boxed int64)
  and joins through exceptions, two joins, `try` and functions
  polymorphic in the witness; the program then stops early, skips
  effects or prints wrong values, in every configuration of the reaper
  with unboxing. After the reaper, `-dflambda` fails (an assertion in
  `Flambda.print_let_static`, on a `Let_static` left with no binding)
  and `-dfexpr-annot-after=reaper` stops ("todo") on some of them;
  compilation itself is not affected.
- Two more faults of the compiler's reaper, reached by type-safe
  programs with its default flags, both fatal errors at compile time
  (found by reading the rules, with a GADT that refines a join):
  - a field read immutably at one type and mutably at another: fields
    are keyed by index and kind, not by mutability, so the mutable
    read, which has any source, and the immutable one, which is
    dominated, read the same field, and `mk_unboxed_fields` stops
    ("flows to both unboxed and non-unboxed variables")
    (`mixed_mutable_field.fl`; the model, which keys fields as the
    compiler does, does not stop: it rebuilds a unit whose run is
    undefined where the unit returns 3); and where the immutable
    branch does not read the field, the inner block is unboxed while
    the field of the outer one is not, and the rebuild stops instead
    (`[fold2_unboxed_subset]`, in `bind_fields`), found by random
    programs with such records (636 programs, 2 of this kind; all
    their other differences were the faults above);
  - an argument that the analysis unboxes, given to a parameter of an
    exported function whose calling convention cannot change: the
    parameter has any source, so the rule that keeps a parameter of
    such code from being unboxed, which reads its sources, does not
    apply, and `rebuild_apply` passes the argument unrewritten under
    `Not_changing_calling_convention` ("Incorrect free names")
    (`escaping_parameter_argument.fl`; the model shares it: its rebuild
    passes the variable whose binding it removed). The test tool now
    goes on with the model where the compiler stops.
  In Rocq, `mixed_mutable_field` and `escaping_parameter_argument`
  (with their `_cause` statements): the tables pass every check of the
  model and the rebuilt units go wrong; each fix, proposed as a seed of
  `cannot_unbox0` and a check, rejects them, accepts the tables of the
  sound examples, and gives tables whose rebuild exits as the unit
  does: an allocation is not unboxed if it is stored in a field read,
  through a use of its holder, into a used name that it does not
  dominate (`check_field_readers`, which the mutable read meets, as
  its result has any source; it subsumes `check_result_receivers`,
  which rejects the sound tables of `unbox_dead`), and an allocation
  given as an argument to a call of code whose calling convention
  cannot change is not unboxed, whatever the sources of the parameter
  (`check_escaping_arguments`). The rule for results of code whose
  calling convention cannot change has the same gap as the second: a
  result with any source records no sources, and a call in tail
  position then reaches the case `Keep _, Unbox _`, harmlessly there,
  as nothing reads the result.
- The model runs with the fixes proposed (`derive_to_unbox_fixed`,
  extracted, with the seeds of `check_field_readers` and
  `check_escaping_arguments`), and the test tool has a fourth section
  for it, printed where the fixes change the tables: on the four
  examples of the faults the rebuild then behaves as the unit does,
  and on the 659 units of the corpus, in four configurations of flags,
  the fixes change no other table.
- The fixes, prototyped in the compiler, outside this branch: three
  seeds of `cannot_unbox0` in `unboxing_analysis.ml`, the rebuild
  unchanged. The first is `check_field_readers`; the second is its
  form for the results of code whose calling convention cannot change,
  which are kept with no use, so that a tail call there no longer
  reaches `Keep _, Unbox _`; the third is
  `check_escaping_arguments`, narrowed to arguments that the
  allocation dominates (a join it does not dominate gets poison, as
  under a change of calling convention). Every reproducer, the 335
  programs of the fuzzing that failed, 300 that passed, and the
  reaper's tests of the testsuite then agree with the compiler without
  the reaper. Of the unboxing of the programs that passed, 5% goes,
  nearly all of it where the old compiler had made a call never
  return without the output showing it; nothing goes in the testsuite
  and the standard library. With these rules, the stubbed check of
  `make_apply_wrapper` never fired, and could be restored for
  `Keep _, Unbox _`. The model's fixes now have the three seeds of the
  patch (`tail_result_seeds`, and `escaping_argument_seeds` narrowed
  as the patch has it); `tail_result` shows the second needed in the
  model, where the tail call goes wrong without it. On the patched
  compiler, 1,550 fresh random programs in seven configurations, and
  550 runs with lambda lifting and in classic mode, all agree with the
  compiler without the reaper; 41 of 200 of them fail on the compiler
  without the patch.
- The epoch of regions, read records and the heap (repaired after the
  rebase on the core that has them). The traversal reads the
  primitives of regions as `traverse_prim` does: `Begin_region` and
  `Begin_try_region` give their variable `any_source`, `End_region`
  and `End_try_region` give theirs `any_source`, the conditional
  `any_usage` and a use of the region; `Local_allocation`, which only
  says that the allocation before it is local, gives nothing, so a
  local allocation has the facts of one on the heap, as in the
  compiler; `projected_set_closures` looks past it to the projections
  of a local set. The rebuilds keep or remove its `Let` with the
  allocation's, by the same test of the same variable, and the rebuild
  with unboxing removes it where the allocation is unboxed. The
  machine now runs the primitives of the heap, so
  `in_flow_fragment` leaves them out: a cell is a location, which is
  at no node, and the rebuild, which removes an unused cell, would
  exit with another heap than the unit's (decision D40). The
  primitives of regions stay in it: the machine gives them no value,
  so a run that reaches one goes wrong. The proofs step a `Let` by
  `step_let_eval`, with no location in the state: the flow invariant
  gives it of the first run, `rel` of the second; the simulation of
  local unboxing keeps the heap empty. The test tool still converts
  with regions dropped (decision D37), and so gives the model no
  primitive of regions: its outputs do not change. Left as they are:
  the projections of a local set are rebuilt as other projections,
  removed when they have no use, where rebuild.ml keeps every closure
  of a set; and the `Local_allocation` after a boxed number made
  poison is kept, where the compiler's mode goes with the allocation
  (the machine of regions has no step for it).
- Arguments of kinds `Region` and `Rec_info` (`any_kind_arguments`,
  `region_argument_example`): the rebuild, with and without changes of
  calling convention, refines observably for calls with arguments of
  any kind, as the poison of those kinds is their only value.
  Projections of value slots into such variables stay out
  (`region_projection_needed`); the compiler makes no such slot. The
  older forms of sets of closures stay out of `in_flow_fragment`: no
  conversion of a compiler term makes them.
- The least solution (`FlambdaReaperLeast.v`, its Spec and its
  proofs): a solution is least when every tuple of its tables is
  derivable by the rules, read with their guards off the solution
  itself (`least_solution`, `least_solution_exact`); the Datalog
  program of the compiler is not stratified, and tables in its order
  may hold stale tuples that are closed but not least
  (`least_no_stale`, `stale_tables`). `check_least` and
  `compute_least` are proved to give a least solution. Of the checked
  premises, a least solution meets `check_field_used_exact`
  (`least_field_used_exact`), but not always `check_apply_widgets`
  (`widgets_counterexample`, with `-reaper-preserve-direct-calls`
  `always` or `never`) nor `check_unboxed_reads`
  (`reads_counterexample`, a name bound twice); each holds under a
  check of the graph (`check_widgets_read_only`,
  `check_reads_read_only`). The statements of the rebuild, of the
  change of calling conventions and of the unboxing follow for a least
  solution (`least_rebuild_refines_observably` and the two others).
  With `-reaper-preserve-direct-calls auto`, the default, the traversal
  of every unit passes `check_widgets_read_only`
  (`traverse_widgets_read_only`, with no premise), so the statement of
  the change of calling conventions asks nothing of the graph for a
  least solution (`least_rebuild_cc_auto_refines_observably`). The unit
  of `widgets_counterexample` is still rebuilt right for every flag and
  every least solution (`widgets_counterexample_refines`): the dead
  call that fails the check also makes the code keep its calling
  convention. A unit whose variables are bound once
  (`variables_bound_once`, which the compiler's units meet, as every
  binder is opened with a fresh name, but `well_formed_unit` does not
  ask: a row for C0) passes `check_reads_read_only`
  (`bound_once_reads_read_only`), and the unboxing statement for a
  least solution then asks nothing of the graph either.
- The fixed mode of the model is now the fix of the unboxing that the
  human wrote after the prototype above (not on the branch): two seeds
  of `cannot_unbox0`, `crr0_other_source` and `crr0_any_source_usage`
  without `is_real_field` (an allocation read, through a use of it with
  another source or of any source, into a used name: `fix_seeds`,
  `check_alias_readers`, `fix_closed`), and the flag `unused` of `Keep`
  (`Keep_unused`): a parameter of a continuation kept with no use is
  given poison by an `Apply_cont` and by the wrapper of a call, before
  the case of a disagreement on unboxing. The model gives decisions to
  the return and exception continuations of code only in that mode
  (`code_continuation_decisions`). The rebuild has a mode
  (`rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_mode`, `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed`); the
  default is the branch's, unchanged. The prototype's seeds and checks
  are gone. The fix also drops the callee of a call of code that does
  not use its `my_closure`; the model keeps it, as its machine runs a
  call by its callee's closure. The four counterexamples
  (`kept_result_unboxed`, `mixed_mutable_field`,
  `escaping_parameter_argument`, `tail_result`) now say that the fix
  repairs them: the first three by the rules, the pair of `tail_result`
  by the rebuild, which returns poison to the result of any source and
  no use (`Keep_unused`). `check_to_unbox_fixed_sound` and
  `check_to_change_representation_fixed_sound` give tables closed under
  the rules and the two of the fix. The fourth section of the test tool
  runs the model in that mode, printed where the fix changes the tables
  or the rebuilt term: on the 753 units of the corpus, with the default
  flags and with local fields and lambda lifting, it changes the tables
  of the four reproducers above and the rebuild of one more unit
  (`cc_known_arity.fl`, poison for a result with no use), and every run
  agrees with the input.
- A regression of the fix, found by transcribing it: where code keeps
  its calling convention and its result has no use, the decision of its
  return continuation is `Keep { unused = true }`, and
  `make_apply_wrapper` gives poison whatever the callee does, so a tail
  call there returns to a wrapper and is no longer in tail position.
  Two local functions that call each other in tail position, called at
  unknown arity through a join and whose results are ignored, stop on
  `Stack_overflow` with 100,000,000 calls, in the seven configurations
  of the reaper (with `-no-reaper-unbox` too), and run without the fix
  (`fix_tail_call.fl`, whose comment gives the program; in the model,
  the rebuild with the fix runs in 42 steps where that without it runs
  in 36). Matching `Keep { unused = true }` only against `Unbox _` and
  `Delete` of the callee, and passing a kept result on as before, keeps
  the tail call, and the 22 reproducers and 335 fuzzed programs that the
  fix was checked on still run as without the reaper, in eight
  configurations.
- The rewriting of types (`FlambdaReaperTypes.v`, its Spec and its
  proofs), extracted: `types_rewriter.ml` on the core's grammar of
  types, with four flags for the fixes that an earlier model of the
  reaper proposed (boxed numbers, aliases, the refinement of sets of
  closures, the null of `or_null`); the compiler has none of them. A
  type rewritten under the metadata that a value is rebuilt under holds
  of the rebuilt value where the type held of the value, for types
  without closures and with the fixes on (`rewrite_type_sound`, and
  `rewrite_symbol_type_sound` for the types of symbols); null keeps its
  types (`rewrite_keeps_null`). Without the fixes the statements fail
  (`boxed_fix_needed`, `alias_fix_needed`, `null_fix_needed`,
  `compiler_rewrite_unsound`). The four faults are still in the
  compiler: the type of an `or_null` closure makes a program of two
  units print 2 where it prints null without the reaper's types; the
  refinement of sets of closures, with local fields and result types
  for all functions, runs into an `Invalid`; the other two give wrong
  types in the `.cmx` that nothing reads wrongly. The result types of
  code are rewritten too (`rewrite_result_types`, sound with the fixes,
  `rewrite_result_type_sound`; `boxed_result_fix_needed`), and the types
  exported by a unit hold of the rebuilt unit's symbols at its exit
  (`exported_types_sound`), given a relation of the two runs at exit
  (`rebuild_exit_relation`), since proved (below). Next: closures and
  unboxing.
- The primitives of the heap, first piece: the flow invariant covers
  the heap. `flows` takes a map `located` from the locations of a run
  to their allocations, the node of the variable of the `Let` that
  made each cell, existential in the statement. A block made with an
  identity holds in each field what is at that field of its allocation,
  or, mutable and with an allocation that has any usage, a value that
  escapes; an array holds constants, and values at nodes with any usage
  when its allocation has (`cell_flows`, `heap_flows`). `flow_sound` now
  asks `in_heap_fragment`, which `in_flow_fragment` implies; a store
  gives any usage to its block and to what it stores, which the edges
  of the proof had not recorded. For the rebuild, `refines_up_to_heap`
  compares exits up to a one-to-one correspondence of the locations the
  values reach, cells compared by `observed_equal_at`: the comparison
  that decision D40 intends, weaker than its interim one, and a choice
  to review (`heap_rebuild_example`: a cell without use removed and an
  unread field made poison). The simulation now relates the two heaps:
  the rebuild, with and without changes of calling convention, refines
  units of `in_heap_fragment` up to that comparison
  (`rebuild_refines_up_to_heap`, `rebuild_cc_refines_up_to_heap`); the
  correspondence of locations is a function of the allocations the
  rebuild keeps, the n-th kept cell of the unit being the n-th cell of
  the rebuilt unit. The statements on `in_flow_fragment` are kept, and
  follow from those of the heap (`rebuild_refines_up_to_heap_flow`,
  `rebuild_cc_refines_up_to_heap_flow`): a run of `in_flow_fragment`
  binds no location, so the comparison up to the heap is one of equal
  heaps and of values `observed_equal`.
- `rebuild_exit_relation` is proved (`rebuild_exit_relation_holds`,
  `FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v`), and with it the types that a unit
  exports hold at its exit with no premise on the relation of the runs
  (`exported_types_sound_end_to_end`). The premises are those of
  `rebuild_refines_observably`, each variable bound once
  (`variables_bound_once`), and, where the unit exits, static blocks
  that hold values and static boxed numbers that hold numbers
  (`exits_of_values`, `statics_of_values`); the end-to-end statement
  takes the least solution and `sets_of_graph` too. `field_rebuilt`
  keeps its reading, at the kind of the field, which is how the
  compiler reads a static block, at its shape. The model's reading, at
  the kind value, was weighed and set aside: it makes the statements of
  the rewriting false of a static block whose naked field nothing reads,
  which the rebuild makes poison of kind value, where the type of the
  field, rewritten at its own kind, does not hold of it. A choice to
  review. The premise on static blocks is needed:
  `naked_field_exit_relation` is kept as it was, on the unit
  `let symbol s50 = { #3 } in return s50`, of which the relation is
  false. It is a premise on the runs. The check on the term
  `unit_of_values` implies it (see the entry on
  FlambdaReaperTypesTerm{Spec,Proofs}.v below).

  The proof. The relation of the simulation (`rel`, `statics_rel`) now
  records what `statics_rebuilt` asks beyond it: the allocation of a
  block or of a boxed number has a source, and the fields of a block
  are values, as the machine tests them where a primitive makes the
  block; a value slot kept is at a node that has a source, any source
  where it holds a constant; and, under a new section variable
  `sets_exact`, the allocations of the closures of a set hold only the
  facts of their set (`sites_exact`), the closures of a static set being
  related as those of a `Let`, with their value slots related at each
  allocation (`static_sites_rel`). `sets_exact` is `False` for the
  statements of the refinement, which are unchanged. For the exit it is
  `closures_exact` of the unit: every `Let` of a closure and every
  binding of a static closure of the unit is made where the graph holds
  exactly the facts of its set (`node_exact`). A run keeps that of every
  state it reaches (`run_exact`): it runs no expression that is not of
  the unit. `rebuild_exit_statics` gives, where the unit exits, an exit
  of the rebuilt unit through the same continuation with the same heap,
  `statics_rel` of the two static environments, and no location in
  them. `FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v` goes from there to
  `statics_rebuilt` (`statics_rel_rebuilt`, `rel_rebuilt_value`,
  `closure_rebuilt`); a field or a value slot used has a use at its
  node, local or not, from `field_used_exact` and the rules of local
  fields (`used_field_hu`).

  A new file, `FlambdaReaperClosuresExactProofs.v`, proves
  `closures_exact` of the graph of a unit of the fragment that is well
  formed and binds each variable once (`closures_exact_of_bound_once`).
  An invariant of the traversal (`inv`) sorts its facts at function
  slots and value slots by the call of `record_set_of_closures_deps`
  that made them (`sgroup`): the allocations of a call are disjoint
  from those of every other (the variable of its `Let`, the variables
  of the projections of its set, and nodes made then; or the symbol of
  its binding), and every other step makes no such fact (`ns_ok`). The
  closures of a static set have the facts of their set where the set is
  bound whole (`sets_well_formed` of `well_formed_unit`). No
  `FlambdaReaperUnboxing*.v` or calling-convention file changed.
- The test tool tests the least solution, where `FLREAPER_LEAST` is set
  (at the end of the first mode; not printed by default, so that no
  expected file changes): the extracted `check_least` on the model's
  solution with the model's graph and on the compiler's with its
  graph; the extracted `compute_least` on each graph, each solution
  compared with it tuple by tuple; and the least solution of the
  model's graph with the compiler's solution, on the names of the unit.
  A tuple beyond the least that is not stale is printed, said NOT
  DERIVABLE where no reading of the guards derives it. On the 754
  units of the corpus (`tests/semantics/{*,ocaml/*,reaper/*}.fl`, of
  which 728 are compared), the compiler's tables are least in 685, all
  the reaper's examples among them. In the other 43 they hold the least
  solution and, beyond it, only tuples that a rule concluded with a
  guard that is false at the end: stale `usages` (24 units, 40 tuples)
  and `sources` (21 units, 167 tuples), and in three units
  (`tupled_partial_application`, `poly_abbreviation_patterns`,
  `var_twelve_mixed`) 13 `alias` tuples that guarded rules concluded
  from stale `sources` or `usages`, which no rule concludes with the
  guards of the end. Their `any_usage`, `any_source`, `has_usage`,
  `has_source` and fields are those of the least solution. The solver
  of the tool has as many stale tuples as the compiler in every unit.
  No tuple of either is underivable, `compute_least` gives a least
  solution of every graph, and on the names of the unit the least
  solution of the model's graph differs from the compiler's tables
  only by those tuples and where the two graphs differ on those names,
  which the tool prints already (23 units, with calls of known arity,
  the closures of a set, regions). The two computations take some 130
  seconds over the corpus, 17 at most for a unit, within the budget of
  the tool (`FLREAPER_LEAST_BUDGET`).
- Local fields, the fault and its fix (`FlambdaReaperLocalFieldsSpec.v`
  and its proofs, `FlambdaReaperLocalFields.v`). The rules find the
  reads of a local slot by three cases: the read node is among the
  usages of the allocation, or the allocation is among the sources of
  the read node, or else the allocation has any usage and the read node
  any source (`escaping_field`, `reading_field`). They leave one case
  out. A value can reach the read node through a `use`, such as an
  `Array_load` from an immutable array (the default case of
  `traverse_prim`). The `use` gives the read node any source as soon as
  the value has a source. It gives the value any usage only once the
  read node has a usage. When nothing uses what the slot is read into,
  nothing uses the read node either, and what is read has no source:
  that is `local_slot_counterexample`. The fix is one rule, the last of
  `local_rules`, with the premises of `reading_field`: a read of a
  local field from a node with any source gives that node `has_usage`.
  The `use` then gives its argument any usage, the allocation escapes,
  and the third case applies. Two other ways to fix it were weighed and
  set aside. Making the field not local would make `is_local` depend
  on the solution. A seed of the traversal under `alias_if_any_source`
  would also mark a field used, as the keep-alive of tupled code does,
  or make the allocation escape when nothing reached it by a `use`.

  In the model the rule is a closure condition beside `closed`
  (`local_reads_used`), and `check_closed_local` tests both. The rule
  only adds `has_usage`, so every statement that asks `check_closed`
  holds of tables closed under the fixed rules. Without local fields
  the rule says nothing. A read of a local field that is used already
  gives its node a usage: the example of `FlambdaReaperSolutionSpec.v`
  passes the new check unchanged. On the unit of
  `local_slot_counterexample`, the least solution before the fix fails
  the new check. The least solution of the fixed rules, which a solver
  with the rule finds, passes every check that the rebuild asks, and
  the rebuilt unit exits as the unit does (`local_slot_fixed`). A
  second unit puts the closure in a block and the block in the array
  (`nested_slot`). Its least solution before the fix passes
  `check_closed` and its rebuild goes wrong. With the fixed rules its
  rebuild exits as the unit does.

  The flow invariant needs one thing at a projection of a local field:
  what the allocation holds in the field must be aliased to what is
  read. This is proved from `may_come_from` and the fixed rules
  (`local_read_alias`). It is false without the fix, on the
  counterexample (`local_read_alias_needs_fix`). A read that the
  rebuild keeps finds its local field used, with or without the fix
  (`local_read_field_used`). `PLAN-local.txt` plans the general proof.
  It replaces the premise `(∀ f, is_local f = false) ∨ projection_free
  e` with `check_local_reads_used`, which the old premise implies (a
  lemma still to prove).

  In the compiler, the same rule (`local-fields.patch`, with the test
  `testsuite/tests/reaper/local_slot_from_iarray.ml`) repairs the
  miscompile at `-O4`. In that miscompile, a closure is put in
  `[: clo :]`, or in a pair in the array, and read back with
  `Iarray.get`. Its call is inlined and the result ignored, and the
  program aborts with "Dead variable in bound pattern". The reaper
  testsuite passes (29 tests).

  A generator of such programs made 300 of them. Before the fix, 274
  abort in every configuration with local fields at `-O3` and `-O4`;
  only `-Oclassic`, which does not inline, runs them. With the fix, all
  300 run as without the reaper. 1569 earlier programs were also run in
  six configurations with local fields, `-O4` among them. They are the
  935 kept by the earlier fuzz campaigns (their failures), the 300 of
  `compfix-work/pass`, and 334 stdlib and testsuite sources. With the
  fix, 1116 run as without the reaper and 118 do not compile here. The
  other 335 fail, and the unfixed compiler gives each of them the same
  status and the same outputs. Those failures come from the open bugs
  of those campaigns: "Incorrect free names", which happens without
  local fields too, and the kept-result unboxing. The cost was
  measured as `kv/ub.sh` did, with
  `-O3 -flambda2-reaper -reaper-local-fields`, on 325 testsuite and
  stdlib sources and 359 fuzz programs. The 519 that the reaper's dump
  printer handles give identical reaper output. That includes the same
  1846 entries to unbox and 513 allocations, the same closures, value
  slots and projections. The other 63 that compile give identical
  object code. No unboxing and no local field is lost there. The fix
  changes the output only where a local slot is read, unused, from a
  value loaded by a `use`. There it keeps the dead load (cleaned up
  before code generation) and makes the closure escape. Without
  `-reaper-local-fields` the object code is unchanged (300 of 300).
- Local fields, the statements under the fix. The flow, the rebuild,
  the change of calling conventions and the local unboxing now have
  statements with local fields (`-reaper-local-fields`) in units that
  project slots. Each `_local` statement drops the premise
  `(∀ f, is_local f = false) ∨ projection_free e = true` and asks
  `check_local_reads_used is_local F T = true` instead, on the facts `F`
  of `check_closed`: the tables are closed under the rule of the fix as
  well. `is_local_slots_only` stays. The statements are
  `flow_sound_local` and `flow_sound_without_keep_alive_local` (the
  check on the facts without the keep-alive, which have fewer reads),
  `rebuild_refines_local`, `rebuild_refines_observably_local`,
  `rebuild_refines_up_to_heap_local`,
  `rebuild_cc_refines_observably_local`,
  `rebuild_cc_refines_up_to_heap_local`,
  `rebuild_unbox_refines_observably_local` and
  `rebuild_unbox_without_unboxing_observably_local`. The new premise is
  weaker than the old (`old_premise_local_reads_used`): without local
  fields the rule says nothing, and the traversal of a unit without
  projection reads no value or function slot, the only fields that may
  be local (`traverse_unit_reads_no_slot`, an invariant of every step of
  the traversal). Every old statement keeps its name and type and is
  now a corollary of its `_local` version. `local_slot_counterexample`
  points to `local_slot_fixed`, and the header of
  `FlambdaReaperFlowSpec.v` says that the statement asks the fix.

  In the proofs, the facts of a projection carried the old premise to
  the step of the projection. The invariant of the run is now stated
  for any premise `slots_read` that gives the rule of the fix
  (`FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.WithLocal`, and a section variable of the
  run of `FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v`). The names outside
  `WithLocal` are its instances before the fix, so that no other file
  changes. The step of a projection of a local field reads the alias
  that `local_read_alias` derives under the fix (`flows_read_slot`,
  `rel_read_slot`). A read that the rebuild keeps finds its field used,
  and what the field holds used, without the fix
  (`field_used_read_slot`, `field_used_hu_slot`). The statements of the
  types files keep the old premise.

  The claim of `PLAN-local.txt` that the existing statements are
  unaffected holds: the rule only adds a closure condition, so tables
  that pass `check_closed_local` pass `check_closed`. Its gloss, that
  the fix can only add tuples, is not true of the least solution.
  When the fix gives an allocation any usage, the guarded rules
  (`usages_accessor`, `usages_alias`, `alias_constructor_usage`) stop
  applying to it. On the unit of `local_slot_counterexample` with a
  second, direct read of the slot from the closure, the least solution
  of the fixed rules loses `usages x10 x10`. It keeps the field used and
  the alias of the direct read, now from `sources x10 x10`
  (`field_used_local_source`, `alias_local_accessor_source`; computed
  with both solvers, not a statement).

  The unboxing across calls and the unboxing of closures now have
  `_local` statements too:
  `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_observably_local`,
  `rebuild_unbox_fixed_tail_refines_observably_local`,
  `rebuild_repr_fixed_refines_observably_local` and
  `rebuild_repr_fixed_tail_refines_observably_local`. Their proofs have
  no projection step of their own. The premise only went on to
  `rebuild_cc_refines_observably`, and now goes to its `_local`
  version. The statements with the old premise are corollaries.

  The least solution has them as well (`FlambdaReaperLeastSpec.v`).
  `derivable_local` is `derivable` plus the rule of the fix, as the
  constructor `byl_has_usage_local_reading`; its other constructors are
  those of `derivable` renamed from `by_` to `byl_`.
  `least_solution_local` asks `check_closed_local` and a derivation of
  each tuple, with the guards read off the tables. `compute_least_local`
  gives such a solution (`compute_least_local_sound`), as it does on the
  two units of `FlambdaReaperLocalFieldsSpec.v`
  (`least_local_examples`). The three lemmas on the checks hold of it
  (`least_field_used_exact_local`, `least_apply_widgets_local`,
  `least_unboxed_reads_local`), with proofs copied from those before
  the fix. The rule concludes only `has_usage`. The proofs either invert
  derivations of the other relations or induct on derivations, where
  `has_usage` is a case of no interest. The five statements of the
  rebuild for a least solution have `_local` versions that ask
  `least_solution_local` instead of the old premise:
  `least_rebuild_refines_observably_local`,
  `least_rebuild_cc_refines_observably_local`,
  `least_rebuild_unbox_refines_observably_local`,
  `least_rebuild_cc_auto_refines_observably_local` and
  `least_rebuild_unbox_bound_once_observably_local`. The last one drops
  `refines` from its name so that its definition fits on one line. A
  least solution of the rules before the fix that passes
  `check_local_reads_used` is one of the rules with the fix
  (`least_local_of_least`). Under the old premise, every table passes
  that check. So the five old statements are now corollaries of their
  `_local` versions.

  None of the three lemmas becomes false under the fix. They are not
  corollaries of the old lemmas, though, since the least solution of the
  fixed rules can lose tuples of `usages`. The remark above is now a
  statement, `local_fix_drops_usages`, on the counterexample unit with
  a second, direct read of the slot from the closure
  (`DirectReadExample`). Before the fix, `x10` has no usage, and the
  direct read gives it `usages x10 x10`. With the fix, `x10` has any
  usage, and the least solution has no `usages x10 x10`. In both, the
  slot is used in `x10` and the direct read is an alias of the
  constants.
- The rewriting of types, closures (`FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v` and its
  proofs): `rewrite_type_sound` now covers closure types, the closure
  case of `types_rewriter.ml` included (`refine_sets_of_closures`, the
  slots of each set kept, `follow_field_for_set_of_closures`, the
  sources outside the unit and the join of all of them), with every fix
  on, for the types whose cases of closures have their slots among those
  of their index (condition 8a of `FlambdaTypeWellFormedSpec.v`) and
  aliases for closure types (`shaped_type`, which replaces
  `closure_free_type`). Two premises are new, in this statement and in
  those of symbols, of result types and of the two runs: the solution
  is the least (`least_solution`, in place of `closed`: in it an
  allocation is a source of each of its usages that has not any source,
  which `refine_sets_of_closures` relies on), and the sets of closures
  given to the rewriting are those of the graph (`sets_of_graph`). What
  the rebuild makes of a closure (`rebuilt_value`, and `static_rebuilt`
  for a closure of a static set; an unnamed static closure is not
  allowed, as in `statics_rel`) now says what `rel` says of its value
  slots, with two facts of the graph that hold where each variable is
  bound once: the allocations of a set hold one another at their
  function slots and nothing else (`set_sites`), and one node at each
  value slot (`slot_held`). `rebuild_exit_relation` therefore asks that
  of static closures too. `refine_fix_needed`: without the fix of
  `refine_sets_of_closures` the statement is false, on two copies of a
  set at one function slot, with a local value slot that the copy which
  escapes does not use: `join_metadata` leaves the escaping copy out of
  the usages, the set recovered is the other copy, and the rewritten
  type says that the rebuilt closure has the value slot that the rebuild
  removed (`refine_example` computes the two rewritings). That is the
  fourth counterexample; the compiler's miscompile is the same fault
  with both sets unboxed. Left: closure types that are not aliases (the
  closure of another slot is rewritten under `Single_source` of an
  allocation that may have no use), unnamed closures and unboxing.
- The rewriting of types, unboxing (`FlambdaReaperTypes.v`,
  `FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v` and its proofs): the result types of the
  parameters and results of code that the unboxing unboxes are
  transcribed (`rewrite_result_types_u`: the patterns of
