  `changed_closure_rebuilt`, the `crel` of the simulation of the closures),
  of what the run rebuilt without has (`rebuilt_value`). The static
  environment of the last two runs is one, as that simulation has it.
  Three premises are new. Of the graph: no closure that changes is held
  in a field other than a function slot (`changed_unstored`), since `crel`
  gives the new slots what the old ones held, which is right only there;
  the fragment of the simulation of the closures has it (its value slots
  and blocks hold plain names). Of the representations: they are those of
  sets (`reprs_of_sets`: the closures of a set change together, each with
  the function slot of its closure, the same fields and new function
  slots, the new slots distinct and given to each function slot of the
  set; a check, not derived from `changed_representations`), and they keep
  no value slot (`reprs_without_slots`, the fragment). The proof is the
  induction of `rewrite_sound` again, on the fuel of `rewrite_arbitrary_u`,
  for a relation of the first run to the third (`rel_u`): a value that
  does not change, related as `rebuilt_ext` relates it to the second run,
  at a node or in a set none of whose allocations changes, or a closure
  whose set changes, rebuilt with its set, and the closure of the third
  run in the representation of its allocation. The identity and closure
  cases are those of `rewrite_sound` for that relation (`With_rw_u`,
  `closure_sound_u`, `closure_rebuilt_view_u`), with a context whose
  `rc_changed` is not empty: the rule of a closure keeps no set that
  changes (`rewrite_rule_u`), so that the closures at the other slots of
  a set kept do not change, and what a block or a value slot holds does
  not change (`changed_unstored`). The case of `changed_rule`
  (`changed_sound`): the set kept is that of the closure (`refine_keeps`),
  with the representation of its allocation, and a closure that does not
  change has no set kept so; the index has the new function slots of the
  set (renamed injectively, `rename_inj`) and no value slot; the code ids
  of the slots that may be called are those of the set; the closure at
  another slot is the closure of the third run at its new slot, in the
  representation of its own allocation (`reprs_of_sets`), so that the
  induction applies to it. Left: the new value slots, the leaves of
  `changed_leaves`. They need the correspondence of the leaves with
  `fields_leaves` (the fields of the representation are read from the
  closures of the set, so that `classify_field_map` is not empty, and the
  new value slots are distinct), the metadata of `pattern_metadata` read
  from `get_fields_of_constructors` (its `field_used_top` and
  `field_used_as` are those of `FlambdaReaperTypes.v`, and then as in
  `follow_field_for_set_at`), and, for a value slot unboxed in turn,
  `leaves_sound` with `rewrite_arbitrary_u` and the context with changes;
  `reprs_of_sets` from the construction of the representations.
- The frame of a call of code that never returns is never returned to,
  in the runs of the unit (`FlambdaReaperDeadFrames{Spec,Proofs}.v`,
  new): `dead_frame_statement`. For a unit of the flow fragment (with no
  local field, or no projection), tables closed under the rules on its
  graph, any flag, and a state of its run at a call of known arity whose
  callee is a closure of code whose code dep has a result of rank `i`
  with no source (as `code_never_returns` has it), and whose continuation
  is a handler: with the continuation of the call rebound to the same
  handler whose parameter of rank `i` is renamed `x`, a variable with no
  source (`rename_param`), the run from that state never binds `x`. The
  renamed handler is applied only where the frame is returned to with a
  value for each of its parameters, so the frame is never returned to.
  The exception continuation of the call is another continuation. With
  `Auto` and a least solution in particular; the statement asks only that
  the tables be closed.
  - The machine has no stack and does not mark its frames: a frame is the
    continuation value that the call gives its callee, and the same value
    may be given to another call that returns. The statement marks the
    frame by renaming a parameter; the frame is that of this call.
  - The proof (no change to FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v): the run keeps
    `flow_invariant` (with no primitive of the heap and no location), from
    which a variable with no source is never bound (`inv_unbound`). The
    call is entered as `direct_call_ok` enters it (`marked_call`), its
    return continuation the renamed handler, which is a continuation of
    the invariant at the results of the code (`marker_ok`): at a rank
    other than `i` what the code returns is read into the parameter of
    the handler the call returns to, and at `i` nothing is, the result
    having no source; that typing is kept through every tail call and
    every handler that captures the frame, by the invariant.
  - Not covered: calls of unknown arity (a partial application returns at
    once to the frame, an over-application returns to the rest of the
    call; `never_returns_of_call` reads the witness of the last group);
    frames that are not handlers (a way out, what is left of an
    over-application); and calls of known arity whose code is not known,
    from `never_returns_of_call` (the code entered has its known witness
    among the sources that it reads, so its result of rank `i` has no
    source: not written). The link from `apply_never_returns` to the
    premise on the code dep (the code entered is the one
    `code_id_actually_called` gives) is not written either.
  - No statement changed. The Makefile lists the two new files.
  - `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- Nothing found false. A call of unknown arity with several results that
  makes a partial application returns one value to its frame, and
  `never_returns_of_call` may hold of it at a rank above 0; a handler of
  as many parameters as results then goes wrong, so this is no
  miscompile, and a way out of the unit is excluded where a call returning
  to it has one result (`exits_one_value`), as rank 0 at a witness that
  is not the last of the code has a source.
- The link from `apply_never_returns` to the code entered, for calls of
  known arity (`FlambdaReaperDeadFrames{Spec,Proofs}.v`): two new
  statements.
  - `dead_frame_of_call_statement`: for a unit, tables and a state of its
    run as in `dead_frame_statement`, at a call of known arity that
    `apply_never_returns` makes one that never returns (with the code
    deps of the traversal and `bound_code_metadata`), whose callee is a
    closure of the code `id`, and which steps: the code dep of `id` has a
    result of some rank with no source. This is the premise of
    `dead_frame_statement` on the code.
  - `never_returns_frame_statement`: the two together. Such a call
    returning to a handler: there is a rank `i` such that, with the
    continuation of the call rebound to the handler whose parameter `i` is
    renamed `x` (no source), the run from there never binds `x`. No
    premise on the callee: a call that does not enter code with the
    results it expects does not step, and the rank is any.
  - The proof (`never_returns_result`): the call reads, at a node `a`, the
    call witness of known arity of its callee, which may come from the
    allocation `b` of the closure (`closure_alloc`); the witness `w` of the
    code dep is among the sources of `a`. If the code is known
    (`code_id_actually_called`), `w` gives `a` the code id `id`
    (`directly_member`), so the code known is `id` (`singleton_some`) and
    `code_never_returns` gives the result. Otherwise `w` is among the
    witnesses that `never_returns_of_call` reads, and the result of rank
    `i` of `w` that it finds with no source is the one of the code dep
    (`known_witness_edges`); the call expects as many results as the code
    returns, as it steps (`entered_dead_result`).
  - The setup of the proof of `dead_frame` is now a lemma (`unit_inv`),
    and its end another (`marked_run`), which the new statements use.
  - Not covered: calls of unknown arity. A partial application returns at
    once to the frame, with one value; `never_returns_of_call` may hold of
    it at a rank above 0 (no result of that rank at an intermediate
    witness), and the frame is then returned to, where a handler of as
    many parameters as results goes wrong.
  - No statement changed. Two new statements.
  - `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- Nothing found false.
- The frames of code that never returns, in the rebuilt terms
  (`FlambdaReaperDeadFrames{Spec,Proofs}.v`): new statement
  `dead_return_dropped_statement`. For a unit of the flow fragment,
  tables closed under the rules on its graph, any flag, and code of the
  unit whose code dep has a result of rank `i` with no source: every
  `Apply_cont` of the body of the code to its return continuation, and
  every arm of a switch (`applies_to`: not under a binding of that
  continuation, nor in code the body binds), that gives a value at rank
  `i`, gives it a name with no source, and both rebuilds drop it: the
  change of calling conventions (`rewrite_apply_cont`: `Invalid`, or no
  arm) and the rebuild with unboxing (`rewrite_apply_cont_u`), whatever
  their environments. The two rebuilds share the test of
  `rewrite_apply_cont_expr` on the arguments of the unit.
  - The proof: the body of the code has the edges of the flow, its return
    continuation at the results of the code dep (`unit_codes_edges`); an
    `Apply_cont` to it aliases each result to its argument
    (`applies_to_edges`), so that an argument with a source, or a constant,
    would give the result one.
  - What is left to apply such a frame in a rebuilt term: a call given the
    return continuation as its continuation (in tail position), and the
    wrappers that the rebuilds make around calls (applied only when the
    call returns).
  - New: `applies_to`, `handler_applies_to` (to review). No statement
    changed.
  - `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The route for the frames of the runs of the rebuilt terms (milestone 2):
  neither a flow invariant on the runs of the term rebuilt with the change
  of calling conventions nor a relation of three runs, but the terms: the
  frame of code that never returns is dead in both rebuilt terms by their
  construction, with no invariant of their runs.
  - A flow invariant on the rebuilt term needs the graph of the original
    renamed: the change of calling conventions renumbers the parameters,
    arguments and results of each code that changes it, and adds the
    parameters of its wrappers and poison. The renamed graph is closed
    under the rules only where every source of a call witness is of one
    code, which a solution that is closed and not least does not give;
    and then the edges of the rebuilt term, for the whole rebuild. The
    relation of three runs composes the simulation of
    FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v with that of the unboxing, and gives only
    the statements up to observation, not
    `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc`.
  - The terms give it for calls whose code is known: the change of calling
    conventions makes them direct calls of that code
    (`rewrite_call_kind_direct`), so the code entered is read off the term;
    its returns are dropped (`dead_return_dropped_statement`); what applies
    its return continuation otherwise is a call given it, which, for the
    frame to stay dead, must call code whose result of the same rank has
    no source (derivable from the flow with a least solution, as in
    `call_never_returns`; simplest as a row of the fragment).
  - Not by the terms: calls whose code is not known (`ind_call_ok`, and
    `never_returns_of_call`): the code entered is known only through the
    values of the run, which the terms do not give. These keep
    `apply_never_returns = false`, or need the flow invariant on the
    rebuilt runs.
- Nothing found false. `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc` without
  `apply_never_returns = false` would fail only if the first rebuild
  returned to a frame of code that never returns; its returns are
  dropped, as above, and its calls in tail position are of code that never
  returns where the fragment asks it.
- The rewriting of types, closures whose representation changes, for every
  closure, the representations keeping value slots
  (`FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v` and its proofs). No fault found.
  `rewrite_changed_type_sound_slots`: the statement of
  `rewrite_changed_type_sound` without `reprs_without_slots`. Each new
  value slot is a leaf of a field of the representation (`changed_leaves`);
  its type is the type at its path in the closure type, rewritten under
  the metadata that `patterns_for_unboxed_fields` gives it
  (`pattern_metadata`), or unknown where no variable is bound, and it holds
  of what the closure of the third run has at that slot
  (`changed_closure_rebuilt`). A value slot unboxed in turn is covered.
  Four premises are new, in place of `reprs_without_slots`:
  - `check_dominated T dominated`: the table of
    `dominated_by_allocation_point` is checked. The allocation that a
    representation is given for is then the closure that dominates it.
  - `function_slots_mutual` (new, on the graph): a node that holds a
    closure at a function slot is held by that closure at one. The closures
    of a set hold one another, as `record_set_of_closures_deps` makes them.
    The closure of `tcr` whose fields the representation has is then in
    the set, so the fields are read from the same closures as in the
    rewriting. With any table, an allocation may be given the
    representation of another set, whose fields its own set need not read.
  - `block_fields_of_one_kind` and `check_unboxed_uses`, for a value slot
    unboxed in turn: the premises of the unboxed result types.
  `reprs_of_sets` is still a check.
  `rewrite_changed_type_sound` is unchanged and still proved: both
  theorems come from the same induction, now for representations that
  meet `repr_leaves_ok` (proofs file). Representations with no value slot
  meet it trivially, and those of the unit meet it under the premises
  above (`unit_reprs_leaves_ok`).
  - The leaves line up with `fields_leaves`
    (`changed_field_leaves_paths`). Some field of the representation is
    read from the closures of the set, so the classification of the fields
    read is not `Class_empty`. The new slots need not be distinct: the
    slots of the type and of the closure are the same list, so a lookup
    finds the same index in both (`combine_lookup`).
  - Leaf metadata (`gfoc_metadata_at`): the `field_used_top` and
    `field_used_as` of FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v are those of
    FlambdaReaperTypes.v (`field_used_top_agree`, `field_used_as_agree`).
    After that the argument is the one of `follow_field_for_set_at`. A
    value slot that `get_fields_of_constructors` reads is used by an
    allocation of the set (`gfoc_used`), so the second run has it, related,
    at the node that the set holds it at.
  - Slots unboxed in turn: `leaf_sound`/`leaves_sound` are now instances of
    `leaf_sound_with`/`leaves_sound_with` (section `Leaves_with`), which
    work for any rewriting that is sound of what a field other than a
    function slot holds. `rewrite_arbitrary_u` with the context with
    changes is one, through `changed_unstored`. The top level is
    `gfoc_level`. A slot that the first run lacks but the second has is of
    the unknown type at every path (`destructure_path_unknown`).
  - `unit_reprs_leaves_ok`: the fold of `changed_representations`
    (`changed_representations_in`), then `check_dominated_sound` and
    `function_slots_mutual`. The closures of the set of the `tcr` closure
    are those of the set (`set_function_slots_in`/`_has`), so
    `get_fields_of_constructors` gives the same fields with the same uses,
    up to order (`gfoc_ext`, `gfoc_spec`), and `mk_shape` follows
    (`nested_shape`, `get_fields_ext`).
- Left: `reprs_of_sets` from the construction. Its NoDup part should be
  easy (`rename_slots` gives consecutive names). Its first part is not
  derivable from the premises: in an arbitrary graph, a closure of a set
  can have another source, through an alias into its node. The table
  then does not dominate it, so it has no representation while another
  closure of its set does, and `reprs_of_sets` fails. A premise on the
  graph would be needed (closures of a set whose representation changes
  have no other source), plus the uniqueness of the entries across the
  fold. Neither `function_slots_mutual` nor the other premises are proved
  of `traverse_unit`.
- `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The relation of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v relates
  code that never returns and the calls that the fix makes never return,
  and is still a simulation (`related2_answered`, `uc_refines`). No
  statement changed: the rebuild proof does not use the new cases yet.
  - `never_ksh` (`Kshape [] [Value] false`) describes the return
    continuation of code that never returns, in its body. No case of `uc`
    applies it: `ac_rel2` gets the conjunct
    `lookup (ac_continuation ac) K <> Some never_ksh` (`uc_apply_cont`,
    `uc_switch`); a call returns to a continuation with `ks_handler = true`;
    `exn_call_rel` asks a shape `[o]` or `o :: sh`, and `never_ksh` has
    none. Its kinds are not as many as its shapes, so no continuation
    described by pdescs is `never_ksh` (`K_of_not_never`).
  - `conts_rel_never`: a continuation at `never_ksh` relates any two
    frames. `resolve_rel2` has a third case,
    `lookup k K = Some never_ksh`, and each use of it rules that case out.
  - The section `Related` has a new variable `NR : code_id -> bool`, the
    code that never returns. Its body is related at
    `code_K_of NR id c cs`, which is `code_K_never c cs`
    (`[(ret, never_ksh); (exn, ...)]`) when `NR id` holds and `code_K c cs`
    otherwise (`uc_bindings_code`, `code_rel`). The relations of the file
    take `NR` after `CS`.
  - New `uc` cases:
    - `uc_call_never`: the first run's `Apply ap`, whatever its
      continuation, against
      `Let_cont_non_recursive w (Handler wps wmd Invalid) (Apply ap')`,
      where `ap'` returns to `w`.
    - `uc_call_never_wraps`: the same with the first run's wrapper
      `wrap wc cps cmd k cargs (Apply ap)`.

    Both ask `call_rel`, `exn_call_rel`, `ap_call_kind ap = Direct id`,
    `CS id = Some cs` and `NR id = true`, and that no wrapper is the
    exception continuation. These are the shapes that
    `make_apply_wrapper_u ... true` and `make_apply_wrapper_cc` build.
  - `uc_let_cont_never`: a `Let_cont_non_recursive k h body` that only
    the first run has. Its body is related with `k` at `never_ksh`. This is
    the handler of such a call, which the second rebuild drops when no
    other use is left. `conts_rel_never_l` relates its frame.
  - `related_call`: the return frame is related, or the call is
    `Direct id` of code with `NR id` and `CS id = Some cs`. `related2` has
    no new case. `call_answered` and `call_code_same` enter the code at
    `code_K_of`, with `conts_rel_never` where `NR id` holds. A call whose
    code is not known may enter such code with its frame related; the
    frame is then skipped.
  - `main_answered`:
    - The call that never returns is answered by the `Let_cont` step
      and the call of the second run (`related_call` after one step).
    - Through a wrapper of the first run, it is answered by one step of
      each run.
    - `uc_let_cont_never` is answered by the step of the first run alone
      (`spine` decreases).
- FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v:
  - `NRf` is `fun _ => false`: the fragment still has no such call.
  - `uc_agree` handles the new cases.
  - New `uc_never_ext` and `uc_never` (with `never_ext` and
    `uc_handlers_keys2`): a continuation that is not free in the second
    term may be described by `never_ksh`. The rebuild proof needs these for
    the body of code that never returns and for the handlers that the
    second rebuild drops.
- Departures from the design:
  - The skipped continuation is not `lookup k K = None`. A continuation
    not in `K` is a way out of the unit: `uc_apply_cont`, `uc_switch` and
    `exn_call_rel` apply it, and `resolve_rel2` reads it as `Outer k`. A
    frame skipped there could be applied, so the relation uses the marker
    `never_ksh` instead.
  - The call site has to know that the code it enters is related at
    `never_ksh`, and `CS` does not say. Hence the variable `NR`: `uc` and
    the other relations of SimProofs take one more parameter.
  - The simulation does not need "dead at the same rank" in the
    tail-call row. Any callee with `NR` will do, because its frame is not
    related.
- Nothing found false.
- `make all` and `make assumptions` pass.
- `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc` and its versions up to observation now
  cover calls of known code that never returns, which the fix makes
  return to a handler `Invalid`
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCalls{Spec,Proofs,SimProofs}.v). Three new
  statements, for a fragment with such calls; the old statements are
  their corollaries, unchanged in name, type and meaning:
  - `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc_never_returns_statement`,
    `rebuild_unbox_fixed_observably_never_returns_statement` and
    `rebuild_unbox_fixed_local_never_returns_statement`: the premises of
    `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc_statement`,
    `..._refines_observably_statement` and
    `..._refines_observably_local_statement`, for any
    `nr : code_id -> bool` (the code that the fragment takes to never
    return), with `calls_unboxing_nr nr` in place of `calls_unboxing`.
  - `calls_unboxing` is now `calls_unboxing_nr (fun _ => false)`, which is
    the old fragment: `call_ok` asks
    `negb (code_never_returns ..) || nr id` and, for the continuation,
    `code_never_returns .. || <the old result check>`; `calls_code` asks
    `negb (nr id) || negb (among ret (live_conts body))`. With `nr`
    empty these are the old conditions.
- The fragment (`calls_unboxing_nr nr`, to review):
  - A call of known code whose result has no source is of code of `nr`.
    Its continuation is not checked (the fix makes it return to a handler
    `Invalid`); its arguments, callee and exception continuation are
    checked as before.
  - The return continuation of code of `nr` is not live in its body.
    `live_conts` (new, in the Spec) is `free_continuations_of_expr` but
    for an `Apply_cont` or an arm one of whose arguments has no source
    (both rebuilds drop it: `rewrite_apply_cont`, `rewrite_apply_cont_u`)
    and for the continuation of a call that `apply_never_returns`. So a
    return to it gives a name with no source, a call that returns to it
    never returns, and no call raises to it. This is the cheaper first
    version of point 4 of the previous entry's plan, as a check on the
    original term; it should hold of the compiler's units for the reasons
    of `dead_return_dropped`, which is not proved.
- The proof (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v):
  - `uc_result` is now `uc K p q`, `incl (fc q) (fc p)`,
    `binds_statics q = true -> binds_statics p = true`, and
    `incl (fc q) (live_conts e)` (in place of `incl (fc p) (fc e)`, which
    nothing used). `code_rebuilt`, `bindings_rebuilt` and the handler
    lists follow.
  - The `Let_cont` case splits three ways: both rebuilds keep the handler
    (`uc_let_cont`), neither (`uc_agree`), or only the first
    (`uc_never_shadow`, then `uc_let_cont_never`). A handler that only
    the second keeps cannot be, by the inclusion.
  - `NRf` is `nr`, a new section variable. `code_rebuilt` and
    `code_rebuilt_nc` relate the body at `code_K_of NRf`
    (`code_K_of_body`): for code of `nr`, its return continuation is not
    live, so not free in the second body (`never_ret_dead`), and
    `uc_never_shadow` gives `code_K_never`.
  - `call_rebuilt_nc` and `call_rebuilt`: where `code_never_returns id`,
    the second rebuild is `make_apply_wrapper_u .. true` (handler
    `Invalid`) and the first `Apply ap` or a wrapper
    (`make_apply_wrapper_cases`, `make_apply_wrapper_cc_cases`);
    `uc_result_never` gives `uc_call_never` or `uc_call_never_wraps`,
    with `call_rel` and `exn_call_rel` as before. `call_rebuilt_ind`
    reads `live_conts` as the free continuations (`apply_never_returns`
    is false there).
- FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v: `code_frame` and
  `uc_let_cont_rec` ask `binds_statics` of the second run's code (or
  handlers) to imply that of the first's, not equality; the first run is
  undefined where its code binds statics (`impl_false`). Still a
  simulation.
- New example, `never_returns_example_statement`: a unit whose code 50
  calls itself through its closure in tail position (its result has no
  source) and which calls 50 returning to a handler, with the tables of
  the least solution. It is not of `calls_unboxing`, it is of
  `calls_unboxing_nr` with the code 50; the fix makes both calls return
  to a handler `Invalid` and drops the handler of the unit, which the
  change of calling conventions alone keeps (with a wrapper); the rebuilt
  unit refines the unit up to observation. It uses `uc_call_never`,
  `uc_call_never_wraps`, `uc_let_cont_never` and `code_K_never`.
- Nothing found false.
- Left:
  - Point 4 of the previous plan in full: derive "the return
    continuation is not live" from rows of the fragment and the flow
    (returns of full arity,
    with `dead_return_dropped`; tail calls only of code that never
    returns; no call raising to it; no call of unknown code in tail
    position), rather than check it. With it, `nr` could be
    `code_never_returns` and the check dropped.
  - Calls whose code is not known keep `apply_never_returns = false`
    (`ind_call_ok`).
  - Closures (point 6): `rr` of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v has none of the new
    cases, and `repr_call` keeps `negb (code_never_returns ..)`.
- `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- Of the check of `calls_code` with `nr := code_never_returns`, the
  returns are derived (FlambdaReaperDeadFrames{Spec,Proofs}.v):
  `dead_returns_not_live_statement`. For code of the unit
  (`unit_codes`) that `code_never_returns`, in the flow fragment, with
  tables closed under the rules (any flag), every `Apply_cont` to its
  return continuation and every arm kept that `applies_to` finds is not
  live (`live_apply_cont .. = []`). No new premise: the full arity of the
  returns comes from `conts_counted` of `code_flow_fragment`, read off
  `code_edges` (`applies_to_counted`), and then `dead_return_dropped`
  applies. Code in the body of code adds nothing to `live_conts`: in the
  flow fragment a body binds no statics.
- What is left of the check, for such code: its calls in tail position
  (continuation `ret`) that do not `apply_never_returns`, its calls whose
  exception continuation is `ret`, and the arms that `Keyed.compact`
  drops (`live_conts` counts every arm; `applies_to` and the traversal
  only the arms kept).
- Found false: with `nr := code_never_returns`, the check does not
  follow from `Auto`, a least solution and premises on the term
  (`unused_never_returns_example_statement`). The code 50 calls the code
  51, which returns, in tail position, and nothing uses 50. The facts of
  a call in code hold only where the code is used (`cond_alias`, a
  `Fact_propagate` on `Node_code_id 50`), so in the least solution the
  result of 50 has no source and `code_never_returns 50` holds, while
  the call is of 51, which may return: the return continuation 3 is
  live, and `calls_unboxing_nr code_never_returns` is false. The unit is
  of the flow fragment and `well_formed_unit`. Not a miscompile: the
  fix's `Never_returns` of 50 is wrong (50 returns if called), but
  nothing calls it, and a call from code that is used would make it
  used. The fragment leaves the unit out by another row too: the callee
  `x42` has no use (`is_var_used` is false); it seems that no name of
  code that is not used has one (not proved).
- Not done: `rebuild_unbox_fixed_refines_cc_never_returns_derived` is not
  stated. Plan, for the rest:
  - Tail calls: the flow gives the row only for code that is used. With
    `any_usage (Node_code_id id)`, the witness of the call reads the call
    witness of the callee, and each result of the callee flows into the
    result of `id` at its rank (`alias_accessor_source`): a result of
    `id` with no source gives one of the callee
    (`code_never_returns id'`). Where the callee is `Any_source` the
    result of `id` has one (`any_source_accessor`), a contradiction. Use
    is to be had from the row of the fragment that the callee is used
    (`is_var_used`), by inversion of the derivation in the least
    solution: a use of a name of code that is not used should not be
    derivable.
    Leastness is needed again to read the code id of the callee off the
    sources of the witness of this call (`code_id_actually_called`
    reads those of every call of the same callee). Calls of unknown
    arity with several groups need the chain of witnesses
    (`caller_chain_edges`, `code_chain_edges`).
  - Raises to `ret`: not from the flow (the exception of the callee need
    not have a source at the rank with no source). It follows from
    `well_formed_unit` (`code_exn_handlers_well_formed`:
    `ways_out_well_formed exn body` has `raises_to ret body = false` for
    `ret` free), a premise of the two statements up to observation but
    not of `..._refines_cc_never_returns`; or a check on the term.
  - Arms that `Keyed.compact` drops: a check on the term (the keys of
    the arms of a switch are distinct). The compiler's switches are maps,
    so converted units meet it; not checked with `flreaper`.
  - The statement needs `calls_unboxing_nr` without the conjunct of
    `calls_code`, a copy of `calls_expr` in the Spec (its definitions are
    not to change), and the induction from that copy and the check per
    code to `calls_unboxing_nr`.

- The fragment (`repr_unboxing_nr nr`, to review). `repr_expr` and the
  functions of the section take `nr` after `my_closures`.
  - `repr_call` asks `negb (code_never_returns ..) || nr id` and, for
    the continuation, `code_never_returns .. || returns_ok ..`: a call of
    code whose result has no source is of code of `nr`, and its
    continuation is not checked. Its callee, arguments and arities are
    checked as before.
  - `repr_code` asks `negb (nr id) || negb (among ret (live_conts ..))`
    of its body, with `live_conts` of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSpec.v: the return continuation of
    code of `nr` is not live in its body.
  - With `nr` empty these are the old conditions.
- The relation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v):
  - `rr` is indexed by the dead continuations `D : list continuation`,
    which no case applies or calls with, and whose frames need not be
    related. `rr_apply_cont`, `rr_switch` and `rr_call` ask their
    continuations not in `D`; `rr_let_cont` relates its body at
    `without [k] D`, `rr_let_cont_rec` its handlers and body at
    `without (map fst hs) D`. `rr_handlers` takes `D`; `rr_bindings` does
    not (code is closed).
  - `code_info` has a new field `ci_never`. The body of code is related
    at `code_dead ci c`, `[ret]` if `ci_never ci` and `[]` otherwise
    (`rr_bindings_code`, `code_rel`). This is the `NR`/`code_K_of` of the
    calls relation, kept in `CS`.
  - New cases, each asking `call_ok_c ap id ci` (the premises of
    `rr_call`), `ci_never ci = true`, and the exception continuation not
    in `D` and no wrapper:
    - `rr_call_never_r`: `Apply ap` against
      `Let_cont_non_recursive w (Handler wps wmd Invalid)
       (Apply (with_continuation ap w))` (`uc_call_never`).
    - `rr_call_never_wraps`: the first run's wrapper
      `Let_cont_non_recursive wc (Handler cps cmd hb) (Apply ap)`
      against the same (`uc_call_never_wraps`).
    - `rr_call_never`: `Apply ap` against
      `Apply (with_continuation ap k)`, `k ∈ D`: the state of
      `rr_call_never_wraps` after the two `Let_cont`s.
  - `rr_let_cont_never`: a `Let_cont_non_recursive k h body` that only
    the first run has, its body related at `k :: D`, with
    `k ∉ free_continuations_of_expr body'` (`uc_let_cont_never`).
  - `conts_rel D conts conts'` is now
    `∀ k, k ∉ D -> cont_rel (resolve k conts) (resolve k conts')`, not
    pointwise: frames at dead continuations are any, of both runs or of
    one (`conts_rel_never`, `conts_rel_never_l`). `cont_rel_closure`
    carries the `D` of its handler. `related` is `∃ D`.
  - `rr_shape`: a free continuation of the second term is one of the
    first or in `D`, and the second binds statics only where the first
    does (were equalities). `rr_dead_ext` (with `rr_dead`,
    `rr_dead_shadow`): `D` may change at continuations not free in the
    second term (`uc_never_ext`, `uc_never_shadow`).
  - `related_answered` (`call_entered` for the entry into code):
    `rr_call_never_r` is answered by two steps of the second run
    (`run_two`), `rr_call_never_wraps` by one of each,
    `rr_let_cont_never` by the first alone (`spine` decreases).
    `rr_refines` asks `rr []`. Still a simulation.
- The proof (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v):
  - `rebuild_ok` builds `rr D` for any `D` not live in the original
    term (`dead_in D (live_conts ..)`), checked at the leaves, and asks
    that no continuation of the term is `ff` or `ffu` (`conts_ok`, from
    `fresh_name (names_of e) <= next` at the top). `CSf` gives
    `ci_never := nr id`.
  - `code_rebuilt` relates the body at `code_dead`; the fragment's check
    gives `dead_in`.
  - `call_rebuilt`: where `code_never_returns id`, the second rebuild is
    `make_apply_wrapper_u .. true` (handler `Invalid`) and the first
    `Apply ap'` or its wrapper: `rr_call_never_r` or
    `rr_call_never_wraps`. Otherwise as before.
  - The `Let_cont` case splits three ways: both rebuilds keep the
    handler, only the first (`rr_let_cont_never`, `rr_dead_shadow`), or
    neither. Only the second cannot, by `rr_shape` at
    `without [k] D`.
- Departures from the calls relation:
  - Dead continuations are a list indexing `rr`, not `never_ksh` in a
    map: the closures relation had no `K`, and frames are related by
    lookup, so no case is needed for a frame of the second run alone.
  - `D` goes down the rebuild and is checked against `live_conts` at the
    leaves, instead of `incl (fc q) (live_conts e)` in the result of the
    rebuild (`uc_result`).

- The premises of the closure types of a set whose representation
  changes, of the traversal (`FlambdaReaperTypesTraversal{Spec,Proofs}.v`,
  new). No fault found.
  - `function_slots_mutual_traversal`: the graph of `traverse_unit Auto u e`,
    for a unit of the flow fragment, well formed and binding each variable
    once, has `function_slots_mutual`. A new invariant of the traversal
    (`minv`): a function slot of the set of a `Let` of a closure is held by
    each allocation of the set, which are distinct, not symbols, and include
    the variable of the `Let` (`closures_of_set_exact`); one of a static
    closure is a symbol of its set. The other steps add no slot fact (the
    `ns_ok` lemmas of FlambdaReaperClosuresExactProofs.v). The symbol that
    a static closure holds is bound to a closure of the same set
    (`static_sets_whole`), whose symbols include the first (the lookup of
    `static_set_whole`, `static_lookup`), and holds the slots of the set
    (its group, `traverse_unit_groups`).
  - `function_slots_of_sets_traversal`, for a new predicate
    `function_slots_of_sets`: an allocation holds one closure at a function
    slot, a closure at one slot only, and the closures it holds hold the
    same function slots. From the groups of
    FlambdaReaperClosuresExactProofs.v (functional, with disjoint
    allocations; those of a `Let` hold one another), and, for a closure
    held at one slot only, from `minv`: the slots of the group of a `Let`
    are those of its call of `record_set_of_closures_deps`, whose closures
    are distinct.
  - `reprs_of_sets_derived`: `reprs_of_sets` of
    `unit_changed_representations`, for a least solution, a checked table
    of `dominated_by_allocation_point`, a graph with `function_slots_of_sets`
    and `function_slots_mutual`, and `changed_closures_one_allocation`
    (new): no closure of the set of an allocation of `tcr` has
    `multiple_allocation_points`. The fold of `changed_representations`
    keeps `rinv`: a closure with a representation has that of the set of a
    closure `x` of `tcr`, at its own slot, and so do the other closures of
    that set, which are seen. A closure of the set of `x` has no
    representation before `x` is met: the closure whose set gave it one
    would hold `x`, which would then be seen. The new premise is needed: a
    closure with a source other than itself is dominated by nothing, so it
    has no representation where the other closures of its set have one.
    The `NoDup` part: `rename_slots` gives increasing names.
  - `rewrite_changed_type_sound_traversal`:
    `rewrite_changed_type_sound_slots` for `traverse_unit Auto u e`, with
    `function_slots_mutual` and `reprs_of_sets` discharged and
    `changed_closures_one_allocation` asked instead.
  - FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v: two comments point to the new file.
- Left, of the traversal: `changed_closures_one_allocation` of its least
  solution. It should hold in the flow fragment, each variable bound once:
  nothing aliases into the variable of a `Let` of a closure or into the
  symbol of a static closure, and a projection that binds another closure
  of the set reads only that closure from the first. It needs an induction
  on `derivable` with an invariant on what may alias into a closure.
  `block_fields_of_one_kind`: true (only `block_deps` makes a constructor
  at a field of a block, of kind `Value`), but it needs an invariant of the
  traversal step by step, as the `ns_ok` lemmas are. `changed_unstored`
  is not looked at.

    belongs to the third run of the statement (`changed_at`,
    `changed_closure_rebuilt`), whose fields hold the second run's

- Left: `closures_unaliased` of the graph of the traversal (flow fragment,
  each variable bound once). Nothing but a constructor should put into
  the variable of a `Let` of a closure, a symbol of a static closure, or a

- `FlambdaExtract.v`: requires `FlambdaReaperFixFailsSpec`,
  `FlambdaReaperNeverReturnsSpec` and `FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec`, and

  - `FlambdaReaperNeverReturnsSpec.keys_and_groups_ok`
  - `FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec.check_closures_unaliased`

  All four are already `bool`-valued, so no boolean twin was needed and no
  other Rocq file was edited. The extraction adds three new pairs of files
  (`FlambdaReaperFixFailsSpec.ml/.mli`,
  `FlambdaReaperNeverReturnsSpec.ml/.mli`,
  `FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec.ml/.mli`). No other extracted file changes.
- `flreaper.ml`, section "The premises of the wrapper": when
  `FLREAPER_WRAPPER_PREMISES` is set, the premises line now also gives
  `keys_and_groups_ok`, `unknown_calls_have_args`, `results_within_codes` and
  `check_closures_unaliased`. The first three run on the same term as the
  older checks. The fourth runs on `traverse_unit Auto` of that term, as the
  statements do, whatever the command line says. When a check fails, a line
  says where; these walks are not trusted:
  - for a call of unknown arity with no group of arguments, the code it is in
    and the callee;
  - for a switch, the keys it has more than once;
  - for `results_within_codes`, the call and the first code that returns
    fewer results;
  - for `check_closures_unaliased`, the offending fact.

  A shared `code_shown` helper prints code ids by name, without colour codes.
  `plain_kinds_failure` uses it too.
- `closures_unaliased` of the graph of the traversal, proved
  (new FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalUnaliased{Spec,Proofs}.v). No fault
  found.
  - `closures_unaliased_traversal`: for a unit of `in_flow_fragment`,
    `well_formed_unit` and `variables_bound_once`, the graph of

    with `closures_unaliased` discharged (the premises of

    discharged; `changed_unstored` stays).
  - The proof: an invariant `jinv` of the traversal of the unit `e0`,
    kept by every step (`jstep_traverse`), then by
    `resolve_delayed_deps` (`resolve_ok`, `unit_final`).
    - `fact_ok`, per fact, of what it puts into (`tgt`: the `to_` of
      alias, use, accessor, parameter, propagate, alias on any source,
      and `any_source`, `any_source_if_used`): into the variable of a
      `Let` only its binding in `e0` (nothing for `Set_of_closures` and
      `Closure_of_set`, only its own read for `Project_function_slot`),
      into a parameter anything, into a symbol only the binding of a
      constant other than a closure or the `any_source` of an imported
      symbol. Steps: `po_ok`, one lemma per step, after the `Kinds.ko_*`
      lemmas.
    - The made nodes held at a function slot (`cm`) are put into by no
      fact and are none of the nodes of the code deps
      (`code_dep_nodes`), the call witnesses of the apply deps and the
      parameters of the continuations in scope (`jenv`). The non-slot
      steps reuse `KnownWitnesses.ext_*` with
      `UnusedCodeTraversalProofs.ghost (acc_next a) (cm a)` (`mk_of_kw`);
      `prepare_code` reuses `AutoWidgets.ext_prepare_code` with a new
      ghost whose widgets are `cm` (`ghost_w`, `mk_of_aw`). `cm` is
      unchanged by these steps (`ns_ok` of
      FlambdaReaperClosuresExactProofs.v); the slot steps
      (`jstep_slots`: `Let` of a closure, static closure, old sets) add
      only constructors and make the new nodes of `cm` fresh.
    - `held_ok`: a node held at slot `g` is made, the variable of a `Let`
      of a closure, the variable of a projection of `g` off a `Let`
      variable that holds it at `g` (`set_closures_projs`), or a symbol
      of a static closure.
    - The end (`into_closure`): with bound once, `NoDup` of the symbol
      bindings, `static_sets_whole` and `sym_bindings_static_names`, a
      fact into a closure is the read of its slot off a variable that
      holds it there.

    `unpromised_callee_statement` shows it: a direct call of the code 21
    (two results) whose callee is a closure of the code 20 (one result)

    check bounds every code the flow could find.
  - New, to review:
    - `call_promises ap id c`: the code that a call promises to call, as
      `shape_of_closure_call` reads it. For `Direct named`, the code

      (any code if there is no list), that is not tupled, and that has one
      parameter for each group of arguments (the call has at least one
      group). Unknown arity promises nothing. The choice for the two cases
      left open: a call of known arity with `None` promises every code of
      its arity (`known_arity_full`); a direct call of code that the unit
      does not bind promises no code of the unit.

      than each code of the unit that it promises to call returns.

    - `callees_promised F T e` (flow): the code that the flow finds a call

      that the call promises to call. Same role as `kind_calls` in
      `call_ok` of the calls refinement.
    - `fix_never_fails_callees_statement`: `fix_never_fails_full_statement`
      with `results_within_callees e` and `callees_promised F T e` in place
      of `results_within_codes e`. The old statements are unchanged. The

      still needs the coarse check.

    `cc_known_arity` has a direct call of `pass` (one and one). Its call

- `FlambdaExtract.v` also extracts, in alphabetical order,

- `callees_promised` runs on the same facts as `check_closures_unaliased`:
  `traverse_unit Auto`, whatever the command line says. The tables are what

- Location lines, which are not trusted, in the style of the other checks:
  - `results_within_callees`: the call, and the first code of the unit that
    the call promises to call (the extracted `call_promises`) and that
    returns fewer results.
  - `callees_promised`: the call, the code that the flow finds it calls (the
    extracted `code_id_actually_called`, with the tool's hashed
    `solution_of_tables`), and why the call does not promise that code. The
    reasons are: the direct call names another code; no group of arguments;
    the list leaves the code out; the code is tupled; or "N groups of
    arguments, and the code M parameters".
- `results_within_codes_failures` and the new walk share one function,
  `results_failures ~callee ~which`. The output of `results_within_codes` is
  unchanged on every unit.
- Calls whose code is not known, of known arity, that the fix makes never
  return: the code they enter in the runs of the unit
  (FlambdaReaperIndirectNeverReturns{Spec,Proofs}.v, new). This is the
  first step toward lifting `apply_never_returns = false` from
  `ind_call_ok` for the statement up to observation. The statement up to
  observation is not reached; the obstacles are below.
  - `entered_used_never_returns_statement`: for a unit of the flow
    fragment, tables closed under the rules on its graph, any flag, and a
    state of its run at a call of known arity that `apply_never_returns`
    makes one that never returns, whether its code is known or not, and
    that steps, the code of the closure called is in `used_never_returns`.
    That is the `nr` of the derived statements of
    FlambdaReaperNeverReturnsSpec.v. The result with no source is
    `dead_frame_of_call`. The use: the call's witness reads the code id
    with any usage, and the known witness of the code dep may come to that
    witness through the allocation of the closure (`closure_alloc`), as in
    `marked_call`.
  - Milestone 1 (dead frames for calls of unknown code), known arity with
    no code: already covered. `never_returns_frame_statement` and
    `dead_frame_of_call_statement` ask only
    `ap_call_kind ap <> Indirect_unknown_arity`, and
    `never_returns_result` handles `code_id_actually_called = None`. No new
    statement for it.
  - No statement changed. The Makefile lists the two new files.
- Not done: milestone 1 for unknown arity. The route of the flow proof
  does not carry the marked frame. `FlowProofs.call_ok` would take the
  renamed handler as the return continuation at nodes whose rank `i` is a
  variable with no source. Its `pending` then asks that, for every `W`
  that may come from the last witness of the call, the result of rank `i`
  of `W` has no source. `never_returns_of_call` gives that only for the
  witnesses it reads, starting from the sources of the callee. With tables
  that are only closed, the call's witnesses may have more sources. Two
  ways:
  - Follow the closure entered, group by group, as `never_returns_result`
    does for one group: `closure_alloc` at the callee, then the chain of
    the code (`code_chain_edges`, the node between two witnesses being its
    own source), for full calls, partial applications (rank `i` is not 0,
    if the result 0 of an intermediate witness has a source, as the
    earlier entry says; one value to a handler of `nres >= 2` parameters
    is undefined) and
    over-applications (the frame under `Over_application`, at the results
    of the next code's chain).
  - Or ask for a least solution, and invert: nothing but the call's own
    facts puts into its witnesses and the nodes between them. That is a
    traversal invariant, as in FlambdaReaperUnusedCodeTraversalProofs.v.
  The dead frame for unknown arity must be "the renamed parameter is never
  bound" (or "never returned to with all results"), not "never applied".
- Not done: milestones 2 and 3. What the three-run route needs, as found:
  - On the side of the unboxing (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v),
    for calls of known arity: `uc_call_never`, `uc_call_never_wraps` and
    the second disjunct of `related_call` generalised from
    `ap_call_kind ap = Direct id ∧ CS id = Some cs ∧ NR id = true` to
    "the code of the callee's closure is of `NR`". `closure_call_answered`
    uses the frame only where the code is not of `NR` (`call_code_same`,
    first branch), and a call of known arity is a full call
    (`known_arity_full`), so the frame is not needed. But this condition is
    on the values of the first run, which the relation does not know: it
    must come from outside, as a premise on every state that the first
    run reaches.
  - That premise on the run rebuilt with the change of calling
    conventions, from the original run: `entered_used_never_returns` gives
    it at the original state. Moving it to the CC state needs the relation
    of FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v (`related`: `env_ok` relates the
    callees, a closure of the same set), and needs every CC state at such a
    call to be related to an original state. `answered` hides the states
    in the middle of an answer of several steps
    (`run (S (S n)) t = Step t'`). A call with a wrapper of the change is
    one: the CC state at the call, after the wrapper is bound, is in the
    middle. Its callee is that of the related `Let_cont` state before it.
    A proof needs either the answers of `related_answered` to say what
    they pass through (the lemmas of the section `Located`), or the
    unboxing relation to take the premise at the `Let_cont` state and
    keep it through the step that binds the wrapper. Even then, it needs
    that no answer of several steps passes through a call. That is true by
    the header of FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v (the middle states are
    wrapper bindings and `Apply_cont`s), but it is not stated there.
  - Cost: `uc` is used by FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v and its proofs as well
    as by FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v, so changing its
    constructors means rebuilding and fixing those three. The fragment
    needs a copy of `calls_expr` with the new `ind_call_ok` (its
    definitions are not to change), and the rebuild induction of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v moved onto it. The old
    fragment is included in the new one, so the old statements follow.
  - A cheaper route, not taken: a static premise, that every code of the
    unit whose arities fit the call is of `nr`. The machine enters, at a
    call of known arity, only code of its arities, so the unboxing
    relation would need no premise on the run, and the `refines_cc` form
    would hold too. It needs the same changes to `uc` and to the fragment,
    and it leaves out units with other code of the same arities.
- Nothing found false. No counterexample to the statement up to
  observation without `apply_never_returns = false`. The unknown-arity
  case of a partial application that returns one value to a frame of
  `nres >= 2` parameters makes the original run go wrong (with
  `results_kinded`), as the earlier entry said.
- `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass.
- `exits_of_values`, the premise on the runs of the end-to-end
  statements of the types, now follows from a check of the term
  (FlambdaReaperTypesTerm{Spec,Proofs}.v, new). The check,
  `unit_of_values`: under the kinds that the binders in scope give the
  variables, the fields of each static block are of kind value, and the
  contents of each static boxed number of a kind that can be boxed. These
  are the two cases of `FlambdaWellKindedSpec.static_const_well_kinded`
  that `statics_of_values` needs, and nothing else is asked. Code and the
  handlers of a recursive `Let_cont` are not looked into, because the
  machine binds nothing static there (`call_code` and `step`, by
  `binds_statics`).
  - `unit_of_values_runs`: in every state that the run of a unit that
    passes the check reaches, `statics_of_values` holds.
    `unit_of_values_exits`: so `exits_of_values` holds.
  - The invariant of the run (in the proofs): the expression passes the
    check under the kinds of the values of the variables. So does every
    handler that a continuation keeps, under the kinds of the values it
    keeps. The static values are of the fragment. The machine tests the
    kind of what it binds at a `Let` and where it applies a handler
    (`FlambdaWellKindedProofs.bind_params_kinds`). A `Let_static` makes
    its blocks of the values of its fields. The body of code that is
    called, and the handlers of a recursive `Let_cont`, bind nothing
    static, so they pass the check under any kinds. Nothing is asked of
    the heap or of the values.
  - `well_kinded_unit_of_values`: the check of FlambdaWellKindedSpec.v
    implies `unit_of_values`. `naked_field_not_of_values`: the unit of
    `naked_field_exit_relation` (`let symbol s50 = { #3 } in return
    s50`) fails it.
  - The statements that took `exits_of_values` are restated with
    `unit_of_values e = true` in its place. The other premises are
    unchanged and in the same order. `exit_relation_term` restates
    `rebuild_exit_relation_holds`, `exit_relation_whole_term` restates
    `rebuild_exit_relation_whole_holds`, `types_end_to_end_term` restates
    `exported_types_sound_end_to_end`, and
    `unaliased_types_end_to_end_term` restates
    `exported_unaliased_types_sound_end_to_end`. Each has a `_local`
    form. The old statements are kept.
  - No counterexample exists. Nothing writes to a static block after it
    is made. Before a `Let_static` runs, every binder that the machine
    has run tested the kind of what it bound.
  - Left out: static blocks whose fields are not all values. The
    compiler reads a static block at its shape, which the model does not
    have. The header of FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v says so, in place of its
    item "a premise on the term in place of `exits_of_values`". The
    comment of `exits_of_values` points to the check.
  - The Makefile lists the two new files. `make assumptions` is clean.
- The `_refines_cc` forms of the derived check of code that never returns,
  with local fields (FlambdaReaperNeverReturns{Spec,Proofs}.v). The earlier
  entry left them unstated.
  - `never_returns_local_cc_statement`: the premises of
    `never_returns_derived_refines_cc_statement`, with
    `least_solution_local` in place of `least_solution`. `check_closed` is
    not asked, because `least_local_checks` gives it.
  - `repr_never_returns_local_cc_statement`: the same for the closures
    fragment (`repr_never_returns_derived_refines_cc_statement`).
  - Each proof is a few lines. `least_local_checks` gives `check_closed`.
    `unit_checked_either`, or `repr_unboxing_checked`, with
    `or_intror`, gives the fragment with the check, and the
    `rebuild_*_refines_cc_never_returns` statement of the fragment does the
    rest. The shorter names keep each `Theorem` line on one line.
- Dead frames for calls of unknown arity: the run part only
  (FlambdaReaperDeadFramesProofs.v, section `Unknown`). No new statement.
  - `marked_indirect_run`: under the invariant of the run (`inv`), take a
    call of unknown arity that returns to a handler. Rebind the call's
    return continuation to that handler with its parameter of rank `i`
    renamed `x`, a variable with no source. The run from there never binds
    `x`, provided `dead_rank ap i` holds. `dead_rank ap i` says: for every
    chain of witnesses that the graph gives the call
    (`caller_chain_edges`), every `W` that may come from the last witness
    has its results of rank `i` without a source.
  - The proof reuses the flow proof's `call_ok`, so full calls, partial
    applications and over-applications all go through it. The return
    nodes passed to it are those of the call, with rank `i` replaced by
    `Node_variable x` (`replace_nth`). `marker_ok` makes the renamed
    handler a continuation at those nodes. `pending_marked` turns the
    call's `pending` (`indirect_pending`) into one at those nodes. At rank
    `i`, nothing reaches a result with no source, and for `i = 0`,
    `returned_at` is empty. A partial application that returns its value
    to the renamed handler goes through `apply_ok` at those nodes.
  - Left: `dead_rank` from `apply_never_returns`. `never_returns_of_call`
    starts from the sources of the callee and reads the witnesses they
    hold. `dead_rank` asks the property of every source of the call's
    own witness nodes. Tables that are only closed may give those nodes
    more sources. Two ways remain, as in the earlier entry:
    - With a least solution: an invariant of the traversal saying that
      only the call's own facts (`unknown_arity_apply_deps`) put anything
      into its fresh witness nodes and the nodes between them. Then
      invert `derivable` along the chain.
    - Without one: follow the closure that is entered, group by group,
      and do not go through `pending`, as `never_returns_result` does for
      one group.
  - No statement in a Spec file yet, because `caller_chain_edges` is
    defined in a Proofs file. Nothing found false.
- The checks of the tables of the refinement across changes of
  representation, derived where they hold
  (`FlambdaReaperClosuresTables{Spec,Proofs}.v`). Of the conjuncts of
  `changed_set_ok` (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSpec.v`) that
  check the tables and the representation `r` of the variable of a
  `Let x kind (Closure_of_set slot decls vs) body`, three follow from
  the construction of the representations (`changed_representations`):
  - `changed_leaves_distinct`: the new value slots of every
    representation that `unit_changed_representations` makes are
    distinct, for any tables (`mk_unboxed_fields` names them from `next`
    on, each once);
  - `changed_decls_renamed`: for any tables, every such representation
    and every `Let` of a closure of the unit, the renamed declarations
    find at the renamed slot what the declarations find at the slot (the
    new function slots of a representation are distinct and after every
    slot of the unit, so that renaming is injective on its slots);
  - `changed_slot_current`: `slot` is `cr_current r`, for the traversal
    with `Auto` of a unit of the flow fragment, well formed and binding
    each variable once, a least solution, and a table of
    `dominated_by_allocation_point` that passes `check_dominated` (as
    `derive_dominated` does, `derive_dominated_checked`). The traversal
    holds the variable at `slot` in itself (`set_edges` of
    `traverse_unit_facts`); `rinv` of the proof of
    `reprs_of_sets_derived` gives the closure at `cr_current r` in the
    set of a node of `tcr`, with `function_slots_of_sets`,
    `function_slots_mutual` and `closures_unaliased` of the traversal,
    and so `slot` is `cr_current r`.

  The fragment that drops them: `changed_set_frag_ok` asks the rest
  (kind `Value`, no projection of another closure of the set,
  `repr_field_ok` of each field); `repr_expr_frag` and
  `repr_unboxing_frag_nr` are `repr_expr` and `repr_unboxing_nr` with it
  (a copy, to review). `repr_unboxing_frag_sound`: with the premises of
  `changed_slot_current`, `repr_unboxing_frag_nr` implies
  `repr_unboxing_nr`. New statements, corollaries of the
  `_never_returns` ones for `Auto`, with the weaker fragment and with
  `variables_bound_once`, `well_formed_unit`, `least_solution` and
  `check_dominated` added: `rebuild_repr_frag_refines_cc`,
  `rebuild_repr_frag_observably`, `rebuild_repr_frag_local`. The old
  statements are unchanged. Left as checks: the conjuncts of
  `repr_field_ok` (each field a value slot of the set, used in a closure
  of the set, holding a plain name with a use or, unboxed in turn, a used
  name unboxed alike). The first two would need an invariant of the
  traversal on the constructors at closures and the relation of
  `get_fields_of_constructors` to `s_field_used`; the others tie the
  unboxing of a field to that of the name stored in it, which the
  statements, taking any `to_unbox`, do not. Nothing found false; no
  counterexample. `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions`
  pass.
- Dead frames for calls of unknown arity, toward deriving the premise of
  `marked_indirect_run` (FlambdaReaperDeadFramesProofs.v, section
  `Unknown`). Only the run part changed. `dead_rank` is not derived, and
  there is no Spec statement yet.
  - Found: `dead_rank ap i`, as committed, cannot be derived. It covers
    every chain of nodes that has the edges of `caller_chain_edges` for the
    arguments and continuations of `ap`, but nothing ties the first witness
    of such a chain to the callee of `ap`. Another call with the same
    arguments and continuations, of another closure, returning to the same
    handler, has such a chain, and its results may have sources.
  - New: `call_dead_rank ap i` is `dead_rank` restricted to the chains that
    the call reads off its own callee. The first witness is the target of
    a `cond_alias_edge` from a node that reads the call witness of unknown
    arity off the callee, which is what `call_edges` gives.
    `dead_rank_call` shows that `dead_rank` implies it. The lemmas
    `marked_indirect_call_at` and `marked_indirect_run_at` take
    `call_dead_rank`. `marked_indirect_call` and `marked_indirect_run` keep
    their types and are now corollaries.
  - A draft of the Spec (FlambdaReaperIndirectDeadFramesSpec.v) is not
    committed: it compiles, but its statements are unproved.
    - `only_alias_into F x from`: nothing puts anything into `x` but
      aliases and propagations from `from`.
    - `check_unknown_chains F`, a test of the facts. For each read `x` of
      the call witness of unknown arity off `n`:
      - `only_read_into F x unknown n false` holds;
      - if a code id is read off `x`, then `n` is only the read of the
        first result off some node;
      - what `x` is aliased or propagated to is `only_alias_into` from `x`.
    - `indirect_dead_frame_statement`: the analogue of
      `never_returns_frame_statement` for unknown arity. It asks
      `least_solution` and `check_unknown_chains` of the graph, and gives a
      rank `i` below the number of results such that the renamed
      parameter is never bound.
    - `ChainsExample`: `let code f50 x40 x41 = invalid`, a closure of it,
      and a call of unknown arity with two groups returning to `k20`. Under
      `traverse_unit Auto`, `vm_compute` gives `in_flow_fragment`,
      `check_unknown_chains` and `apply_never_returns` of the call all
      `true`, and `compute_least` gives tables.
  - The planned derivation of `call_dead_rank` from `apply_never_returns`
    with a least solution and the check. Every lemma it needs exists.
    - `covers P x` means that `x` has no `any_source`, and each source of
      `x` that has a source satisfies `P`.
    - Read step: from `read_only F x f base false`, `covers P base`, and
      `get_field_sources F S P f = Sources P'`, deduce `covers P' x`. This
      uses `LP.any_source_into`, `LP.alias_into` and `LP.sources_into`.
    - The usage case of `alias_into` goes through `LP.usages_flow`. An
      allocation used at the base is a source of it, since the base has
      no `any_source`. The witness then has a source through
      `has_source_constructor`.
    - Alias step for the first witness: an inversion of `derivable` under
      `only_alias_into`.
    - An induction along `combine (call_groups ap) ws`, against a function
      that gives the sets `never_returns_of_call` reaches. At the end, a
      result of rank `i` with a source would give its witness a source,
      which `result_has_source_of` excludes.
  - Left:
    - the proofs of the draft (the derivation above and the statement,
      through `unit_inv` and `marked_indirect_run_at`);
    - then `check_unknown_chains` of `traverse_unit Auto`, by the ghost
      pattern of FlambdaReaperUnusedCodeTraversalProofs.v: `KnownWitnesses`
      steps with `ghost (acc_next a) sp`, where `sp` is the chain nodes
      made so far, plus a spec of `make_unknown_arity_apply_widget`.
  - Nothing was found false beyond `dead_rank` above. `make all` (with
    `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass on the tree with the patch. The
    statements of Flambda 2 depend on no axiom.
- Over-applications of code whose result has no use, for calls of known
  arity (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver{Spec,Proofs}.v`): the check
  `ind_code_ok` of a call whose code is not known asks of every code of
  the unit that a single result of kind `Value` have a use, because an
  over-application calls it. On the machine a call of known arity
  (`Indirect_known_arity`) is never an over-application: it is undefined
  unless the code of its callee has as many parameters as it has groups
  of arguments (`known_arity_full`), and its callee is never a partial
  application. The new fragment `calls_unboxing_over_nr` is
  `calls_unboxing_nr` with `ind_code_ok_o`, which asks that conjunct only
  of a call of another kind; every unit of the old fragment is of it
  (`calls_unboxing_in_over`). The three statements of the fix with code
  `nr` that never returns hold of it (`rebuild_unbox_over_refines_cc`,
  `rebuild_unbox_over_refines_observably`,
  `rebuild_unbox_over_refines_observably_local`). In the simulation
  (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v`), the shape of a call
  whose code is not known is now `ind_targets_k` of the call's kind: for a
  call of known arity `ind_targets_w`, which drops the plain result of
  `ind_compat`; `closure_call_answered_g` asks the plain result only
  where the shape is an over-application, `known_arity_no_over` rules
  that out for a call of known arity, and the old lemmas
  (`closure_call_answered`, `ind_call_answered`) are its corollaries.
  The rebuild keeps a call of known arity whose code is not known one of
  known arity (`rewrite_kind_known`). FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v
  now proves its construction on `calls_expr_o` and the old statements
  from it; their names and types are unchanged. `known_arity_example`: a
  unit whose three codes keep their calling convention and return
  results with no use, calls 52 directly and 50 or 51 (chosen by a
  `Switch`) at known arity, dropping both results; with the least
  solution it is refused by `calls_unboxing` and accepted by
  `calls_unboxing_over`, and the rebuilt unit (50 and 51 return poison,
  both calls return to a wrapper) refines the unit up to observation.
  Left:
  - Calls of unknown arity with one group of arguments (`f x`, as
    `List.iter` makes). In a unit with a call of unknown arity, code has a
    parameter (`in_flow_fragment`), and a partial application that the
    machine makes holds fewer groups than its code has parameters, so a
    call with one group over-applies nothing. The simulation would need
    that invariant of the first run: every partial application in its
    state, `(cl, applied, aa)`, has `length aa` below the parameters of
    the code of `cl` (statics only grow with fresh keys, so the bound
    is stable). `FlambdaSemanticsProofs.step_values_all` cannot carry it,
    as it asks `P` of a partial application to be exactly `P` of its
    closure and arguments; a copy of its five call lemmas, with the bound
    given where `shape_of_code_call` makes a `Partial_call`, would. Then
    `ind_targets_k` would also excuse a call of unknown arity with at
    most one group, `ind_call_answered_k` would take the bound for the
    callee, `cont_rel_over` would not arise from such a call, and
    `related2_answered` would carry the invariant beside `related2`.
  - The flow invariant (calls whose code is not known in units where some
    code changes its calling convention): `ind_code_ok` asks of every
    code that it keep its calling convention, as nothing relates the
    callee of such a call to the code that the analysis says reaches it.
    `FlambdaReaperFlowSpec`'s `state_flows` gives, of a run of the
    original unit, that a closure at a node has its code among the
    `called` facts of an allocation that the node may come from, and
    `check_cc_closed` puts every code reached by a call witness of
    unknown arity in `cannot_change`. Plan: (1) prove that the CC rebuild
    preserves flows up to the renaming of the rebuild (a simulation of
    the original unit by the first run that keeps closures as they are,
    which `FlambdaReaperCallingConventionsProofs` nearly has); (2) carry
    `flows v (Node_variable f)` for the callee into `related_call`, and
    replace the `∀ id` of `ind_targets` by `∀ id ∈ reach f`, where
    `reach f` is the code of the `called` facts of the sources of `f`;
    (3) in the check, ask `ind_code_ok` only of that code. Not started.
  No counterexample found.

- Dead frames for calls of unknown arity: `call_dead_rank` derived from
  `apply_never_returns`, with a Spec statement
  (FlambdaReaperIndirectDeadFrames{Spec,Proofs}.v).
  - `only_alias_into F x from`: nothing puts anything into `x` but aliases
    and propagations from `from`. `check_unknown_chains F`, a test of the
    facts. For each read `x` of the call witness of unknown arity off `n`:
    `only_read_into F x unknown n false` holds; if a code id is read off
    `x`, `n` is only the read of the first result off some node; and what
    `x` is aliased or propagated to is `only_alias_into` from `x`.
  - `indirect_dead_frame_statement`: the analogue of
    `never_returns_frame_statement` for calls of unknown arity, for any
    flag. Take a unit of the flow fragment, tables that are a
    `least_solution` on its graph, a graph that passes
    `check_unknown_chains`, and a state of its run at a call of unknown
    arity that `apply_never_returns` makes one that never returns,
    returning to a handler. Then some rank `i` is below the number of
    results, and if the parameter of rank `i` of the handler is renamed `x`,
    a variable with no source, the run from the call never binds `x`.
  - `chains_example_statement`: the unit `let code f50 x40 x41 = invalid`,
    a closure of it, and a call of unknown arity with two groups,
    returning to `k20`. Under `traverse_unit Auto`, `compute_least` gives a
    least solution, and `vm_compute` finds `in_flow_fragment`,
    `check_unknown_chains` and `apply_never_returns` of the call `true`.
    So the premises can all hold.
  - The proof. `covers P x`: `x` has no `any_source`, and every source of
    `x` that has a source is in `P`.
    - `read_covers`: a node that is only read off a covered base is
      covered by what `get_field_sources` computes. This uses
      `LP.alias_into`, `LP.any_source_into` and `LP.sources_into`. In the
      usage case, `LP.usages_flow` makes the constructor a source of the
      base, since the base has no any source.
    - `alias_covers`: the same for a node that is `only_alias_into` from a
      covered node, by inversion of `derivable`.
    - `chain_dead`: an induction along the chain, against the sets that
      `never_returns_of_call` reaches (`after_witness`). At the last
      witness, a result of the rank found that has a source would make
      `result_has_source_of` true.
    - `never_returns_dead`: `apply_never_returns` gives a rank `i` with
      `call_dead_rank ap i`. `unit_inv` and `marked_indirect_run_at` then
      give the statement.
  - The header of FlambdaReaperDeadFramesProofs.v now points to the new
    file; only its comment changed. The Makefile lists the two new files.
  - Left: `check_unknown_chains` of `traverse_unit Auto`, not started.
    Only two kinds of fact read that field: the node that the call reads
    off its callee, and the witnesses after the first. These nodes, the
    first witness and the reads of the first result between two
    witnesses are all fresh. Only `make_unknown_arity_apply_widget` and the
    read after it in `traverse_call_kind` put anything into them. The
    delayed facts put constructors of that field only at the let-bound
    names of closures. So the check should hold for every unit, whatever
    the flag. The plan follows the ghost pattern of
    FlambdaReaperUnusedCodeTraversalProofs.v: `KnownWitnesses` steps with
    `ghost (acc_next a) sp`, where `sp` is the chain nodes made so far,
    plus a spec of `make_unknown_arity_apply_widget` and the delayed deps.

- Dead frames for calls of unknown arity: `check_unknown_chains` of the
  traversal, and the frame statement without it
  (FlambdaReaperIndirectDeadFramesTraversal{Spec,Proofs}.v).
  - `traverse_unknown_chains_statement`: for every flag, unit interface and
    term, `check_unknown_chains (traverse_unit flag u e) = true`. Nothing
    is asked of the unit (not even `in_flow_fragment`), and the flag does
    not matter: the delayed facts of the calls of known arity touch no node
    of a chain, whatever the flag.
  - `indirect_dead_frame_traversal_statement`: `indirect_dead_frame_statement`
    without the premise `check_unknown_chains`, for every flag; its proof
    gives it `traverse_unknown_chains`.
  - The proof (module `Chains`).
    - `chain S L x n`: what the check asks of the read `x` off `n`, on the
      list of facts `L`, with every node it looks at in `S` (`x`, the
      targets of aliases from `x`, and `n` when a code id is read off
      `x`). `check_of_chains`: if every read with the field of unknown
      arity has `chain`, the check holds of `facts_of_list L`.
      `chain_frame`: adding facts with no node of `S` keeps `chain`.
    - `uinv S a`: every node of the facts of `a` is made before it, so is
      every node of `S`, every read with the field of unknown arity has
      `chain S`, and no node of a code dep (all its nodes), of an apply dep
      (closure and witness) or of a closure dep is in `S`.
    - `ext`: the AutoWidgets steps of FlambdaReaperLeastProofs.v, redone
      on all the nodes of a fact (`nodes_of`), not only the nodes facts put
      something into, and with no read with the field of unknown arity.
      Every step is one, but the call of unknown arity; calls of known
      arity (`ext_call_known`) and the delayed deps (`ext_resolve`)
      included.
    - `deps_spec`: the facts of `unknown_arity_apply_deps`. Each read of a
      witness after the first is all that puts anything into it, and the
      read of the first result before it is all that puts anything into
      that; the nodes put into are `exn`, `called`, the results, the nodes
      made by the recursion and the witnesses after the first.
      `uinv_call_unknown`: the whole call of unknown arity. Its facts check
      its chain alone (the first witness is put into by the
      `cond_alias` from the read off the callee only), and `S` grows by
      the nodes made in the call. A step (`tstep`) gives a new `S`.
    - `uinv_traverse_unit`, then `traverse_unknown_chains`.
  - The header of FlambdaReaperIndirectDeadFramesSpec.v now points to the
    new file; only its comment changed. The Makefile lists the two new
    files.
  - Left of this item: nothing. The analysis of the previous entry held,
    with one detail: the `cond_alias` into the first witness and the read
    of a code id off a witness are the only other facts on the chain
    nodes, and the check allows both.
  - Nothing was found false. `make all` (with `claims`) and
    `make assumptions` pass, and the statements of Flambda 2 depend on no
