- Over-applications for calls with one group of arguments (`f x`, as
  `List.iter` makes), in a unit whose code has parameters
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver1{Spec,Proofs}.v and
  FlambdaReaperPartialGroupsProofs.v, new). The three statements of the
  fix hold of the wider fragment. A call with one group never
  over-applies: a closure gets at most as many groups as its code has
  parameters, and a partial application made by the machine holds fewer
  groups than its code has parameters.
  - The run invariant (FlambdaReaperPartialGroupsProofs.v).
    `partials_of_value` lists the partial applications in a value.
    `pa_bound statics cl aa` says that `cl` is a closure of bound code
    with more parameters than `aa` has groups. `pa_ok` asks it of each
    partial application in a value, and `pa_inv s` of every value of the
    state.
    - `step_values_bound` is `FlambdaSemanticsProofs.step_values_all`,
      with the property of a partial application asked one way only (of
      its closure and arguments) and given where `shape_of_code_call`
      makes a `Partial_call`. Its call lemmas are copied as
      `*_values_bound`.
    - `pa_inv_step`, `pa_inv_initial`, `pa_inv_run`: every state of a run
      from the empty state satisfies `pa_inv`. Statics only grow with
      fresh keys (`pa_ok_extend`).
    - `one_group_no_over`: a call with one group of arguments, of a value
      that satisfies `pa_ok`, is never an `Over_call` when every code of
      the statics is not tupled and has a parameter.
  - The check (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver1Spec.v).
    `never_over ap` holds of a call of known arity, or of a call with one
    group of arguments when every code of the unit has a parameter
    (`codes_have_params`). `ind_code_ok_o1` asks for a use of a single
    `Value` result only when `never_over` fails. `ind_call_ok_o1`,
    `call_ok_o1`, `calls_expr_o1` (and its mutual functions),
    `calls_unboxing_over1_nr` and `calls_unboxing_over1` follow the `_o`
    functions. In a unit of `in_flow_fragment` with a call of unknown
    arity, every code that is not tupled has a parameter.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_over1_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_unbox_over1_refines_observably`,
    `rebuild_unbox_over1_refines_observably_local` (those of `_over`, for
    `calls_unboxing_over1_nr`), and `calls_unboxing_over_in_over1` (the
    `_over` fragment is contained in the new one).
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v).
    - `call_target` also admits the shape `ind_targets_1`: one group,
      `ind_targets_w`, and a parameter for every code shape. `code_frame`
      turns the last one into a parameter for every code of the statics.
    - `ind_call_answered_1` answers such a call by
      `closure_call_answered_g`, using `one_group_no_over` for the
      callee.
    - `call_answered`, `main_answered` and `related2_answered` now take
      `pa_inv` of the first state. These lemmas are used only in this
      file.
    - `uc_refines` keeps its type: it runs `shrinking_refines` on
      `related2 ∧ pa_inv` (`answered_pa_inv`, by `pa_inv_step`).
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). The
    construction of `uc` in the section `RebuildsCalls` is now made on
    `calls_expr_o1`. `ind_code_shape1` and `ind_shape_targets1` give the
    shape `ind_targets_k` or `ind_targets_1`.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over1` is new.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over` keeps its type and follows by
    `calls_unboxing_in_over1_nr` (section `InOver1`). The statements of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSpec.v and of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOverSpec.v are unchanged.
  - `one_group_example`: `KnownArityExample` with its call made of
    unknown arity (`f 5`, where `f` is the closure of 50 or of 51). With
    the least solution, `calls_unboxing_over` refuses it and
    `calls_unboxing_over1` accepts it. In the rebuilt unit, 50 and 51
    return poison and the call stays of unknown arity. The rebuilt unit
    refines the unit up to observation.
  - Left: calls with two groups or more, of unknown arity, still ask
    every code for the plain result (they may over-apply). The flow
    invariant (entry above) is not started. Nothing was found false, and
    there is no counterexample.
- The failure sites of the fix's rebuild and analyses, on the fragment
  (FlambdaReaperFixFailsAll{Spec,Proofs}.v, new):
  `fix_never_fails_all_statement`, and three units that reach a site.
  Every `Misc.fatal_error*`, `assert`, `Option.get`, `List.hd`/`tl`,
  `List.map2`/`fold_left2`/`iter2`/`combine`/`map3` and `Map.find`
  without a default of rebuild.ml (53 fatal errors), unboxing_analysis.ml
  (14), analysis.ml, points_to_analysis.ml, traverse*.ml and
  slot_offsets_analysis.ml (types_rewriter.ml left out), with the model's
  counterpart. Classes: (A) modelled, the model taking a fallback where
  the compiler fails; (A=) excluded by a premise as it stands; (A+)
  proved never taken, in `fix_never_fails_all_statement`; (A!) taken in
  the model under the premises (a unit below); (A?) not proved; (B)
  outside the fragment; (C) unreachable by construction, no counterpart.
  - `fix_never_fails_all_statement`: the premises of
    `fix_never_fails_full_statement`, and its conclusion with
    `uses_fit`: every continuation that the unit or any of its code
    (used or not) applies or gives a call (`unit_cont_uses`: the return
    and exception continuations of each call, the continuation of each
    `Apply_cont` and of each arm) has, in the rebuild of the fix, as many
    decisions as it is given values, or no decisions and is a
    continuation of the unit with that count (the compiler keeps it
    whole, being of fixed arity). The decisions are those that
    `rebuild_expr_u` binds (`handler_decisions`,
    `code_continuation_decisions`). The proof is that of `calls_bounded`
    (FlambdaReaperFixFailsProofs.v) with equalities (`uses_fit_walk`):
    `conts_counted`, `handler_decisions` has one decision for each
    parameter, and each code of the unit has a code dep with as many
    results as the code (`code_edges`).
  - `more_results_statement` (A!): code 21 returns two values, a direct
    call of it expects one; the unit meets the premises of
    `fix_never_fails_callees_statement` (those of
    `fix_never_fails_full_statement` too), the code changes its calling
    convention, the call is rebuilt, may return, and its callee has two
    return decisions where its continuation has one. rebuild.ml:
    `make_apply_wrapper` raises `Invalid_argument` (`List.fold_left2`);
    the model cuts the callee's decisions to the call's results
    (`callee_decisions`, `combine`). `results_within_codes` allows a call
    to expect fewer results than the code returns. Not reached on the
    compiler: an `Obj.magic` of a function returning an unboxed pair to
    one returning a value is called at unknown arity.
  - `empty_callee_codes_statement` (A!): a call of known arity of a
    block, `x10 (1)` with `x10 = [5]`; the unit meets the premises, the
    call is rebuilt with its callee and argument not dead, and
    `code_id_actually_directly_called` finds no code. rebuild.ml:
    `decide_whether_apply_needs_calling_convention_change` stops ("Empty
    potentially called code_ids"), before the test of an unboxed callee
    (the block is unboxed, and the model makes the call `Invalid`). The
    model's `called_call_kind` keeps the call kind there. Not reached on
    the compiler: its calls of known arity and direct calls are of
    callees that its types say are closures of known code; the traversal
    gives a closure of `Deleted` code no call witness, which would reach
    it.
  - `no_parameter_code_statement` (A!): a unit binding code with no
    parameter meets the premises (`in_flow_fragment` asks for parameters
    only where the unit makes a call of unknown arity).
    traverse_acc.ml: `create_unknown_arity_non_tupled_call_witnesses`
    stops ("add_deps: no params"), for any such code; the model gives it
    one group of none (`at_least_one_group`). The compiler makes no code
    with no parameter.

- The checks of the fields of a representation, derived where they hold
  for the tables of the unboxing analysis
  (`FlambdaReaperClosuresTablesFields{Spec,Proofs}.v`). Of
  `repr_field_ok`, which `changed_set_frag_ok` still asked of each field
  of the representation `r` of the variable `x` of a `Let` of a closure,
  for the traversal with `Auto` of a unit of the flow fragment, well
  formed and binding each variable once, a least solution, and the
  tables of the analysis with the fix (`analysis_tables`:
  `check_dominated`, `check_to_change_representation_fixed`,
  `check_sets_whole`):
  - `changed_field_used`: each field of `r` is a value slot with a use in
    a closure of the set (`s_field_used`). `mk_unboxed_fields` keeps only
    a field with a source (`mk_field_source`), held by the allocation of
    `tcr` whose set gives `r` (`source_constructor`, `reprs_in`), which
    holds no field of a block, not being in
    `cannot_change_representation` (`tcr_not_block`); `gfoc_used` gives a
    closure of that set that uses it, and `x`, dominated by a closure of
    the set, is one (`dominated_self`), holding the same function slots
    (`function_slots_of_sets`).
  - `changed_field_kept`: if `vs` has the slot `w` of a field, at `a`, `a`
    is a constant or a name with a use (`kept_name`): `closure_node_exact`
    and the edges of the `Let` make every closure of the set hold `a` at
    `w`, and the rules give a use to what a closure holds at a field read
    with a use (`gfoc_holds_used`).
  - `unboxed_changed_disjoint`: no name is both unboxed and changed (one
    allocation dominates it, and sets are unboxed or changed whole); so a
    variable unboxed in a slot is not changed.
  - `changed_in_slot_counterexample`: `plain_c` of the simple of a field
    not unboxed is false of the construction. The closure `x60`, called
    through the slot 80 of `x61`, and `x61`, called by the unit, both
    change their representation; the field 80 of that of `x61` is not
    unboxed and holds `x60`. Not a fault of the compiler: the read of the
    slot, dominated by `x60`, is rewritten with the representation of
    `x60`, as is the closure the new slot holds; only the relation of the
    proof (no changed closure in a slot) needs it. The unit is outside
    the fragment anyway (the read is a changed name bound to no set).
  - `cyclic_in_slot_counterexample`: `unboxed_field_pairs` of a field
    unboxed and the variable it holds is false of the construction. The
    model bounds the depth of an unboxing by `S (length to_unbox)`,
    taking a chain of allocations to unbox, each in a field of the one
    before, not to exceed it; a block made by a loop that holds the block
    of the iteration before (`CyclicInSlot`, one allocation to unbox) is
    such a chain. Its slot, unboxed from the closure, is cut one level
    before the block, unboxed from itself. The compiler has no bound and
    unboxes both as deep as the reads go (by hand: the fields of the slot
    are then among those of the block). A fault of the bound of the model
    (`mk_unboxed_fields` and its comment in FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v), not
    of the compiler.

  The fragment that drops what is derived: `changed_set_frag2_ok` asks
  kind `Value`, no projection of another closure of the set, and
  `repr_field_frag_ok` of each field (the slot in `vs`, a plain simple
  for a field not unboxed, a variable unboxed alike for one unboxed).
  `repr_expr_with` is `repr_expr_frag` with the check of a changed set as
  an argument (a copy, to review, meant for later fragments too), and
  `repr_unboxing_frag2_nr` is it with `changed_set_frag2_ok`.
  `repr_unboxing_frag2_sound`: with the premises above,
  `repr_unboxing_frag2_nr` implies `repr_unboxing_frag_nr`. New
  statements, corollaries of the `rebuild_repr_frag_` ones, with the
  weaker fragment and `analysis_tables` in place of `check_dominated`:
  `rebuild_repr_frag2_refines_cc`, `rebuild_repr_frag2_observably`,
  `rebuild_repr_frag2_local`. The old statements are unchanged. Left as
  checks: that the slot of a field is one of `vs` (it needs an invariant
  of the traversal on the value slots of the group of a `Let`;
  `ClosuresExactProofs.sg_ok` keeps only their keys' uniqueness), that the
  simple of a field not unboxed is not unboxed (true by hand, through the
  inversion of a least solution: the reads of an unboxed name off the
  slot would be dominated by its allocation, and the field unboxed), and
  the two conjuncts above that are false of the construction.
- The rewriting of types with the primitives of the heap
  (FlambdaReaperTypesHeap{Spec,Proofs}.v and
  FlambdaReaperTypesHeapExitProofs.v, new). No counterexample: the
  statements of the types hold of the units of `in_heap_fragment`.
  - Where the proofs needed `in_flow_fragment`: not in the flow
    (`flow_sound_local` is of `in_heap_fragment` and gives
    `statics_allocated`), nor in the meaning of types, which reads no heap,
    but at the exit of the simulation: `rebuild_exit_relation` asks the
    same heap at the two exits (`unit_exit_statics` takes no primitive of
    the heap), `rebuilt_value` has no case for a location
    (`statics_rel_rebuilt` asks `statics_no_location`), and
    `closures_exact_of_bound_once` asks `in_flow_fragment`, of which it
    reads only that the unit has no older form of set.
  - How the types speak of a value of the heap: `type_satisfied` reads a
    value through `block_of`, `boxed_number_of`, `closure_in_set`,
    `project_function_slot` and `string_of`, none of which reads the heap;
    a location is none of these and `Mutable_block` holds of no value. A
    type holds of a location as of the immutable array of that location,
    but for an alias (`erase_locations`; `type_satisfied_erase`: a type
    holds of a value exactly as of its erasure, in the erased static
    environment and valuation). The rewriting keeps `Mutable_block`
    (`Rule.identity`) or forgets it, so the rewritten types speak of a
    location only through aliases, which the fragment excludes for a name
    that holds one.
  - The relation: `rebuilt_value_heap β` (a location rebuilt as the one
    `β` gives it, otherwise `rebuilt_value`), `static_rebuilt_heap`,
    `statics_rebuilt_heap`, `rebuilt_under_heap`, `locations_one_to_one`;
    `rebuild_exit_relation_heap` (the exits observed equal up to a
    correspondence of locations, `exits_observed_equal` as
    `rebuild_refines_up_to_heap` has them, and the static environments
    related by `statics_rebuilt_heap` for a `β` one to one) and
    `rebuild_exit_relation_whole_heap` (the static sets kept whole too).
  - Proofs: the statements of a graph and its tables reduce to those
    without the heap by erasing both runs (`rebuilt_value_heap_erase`, and
    the erasure keeps the fragment, the copies, `statics_allocated` and
    `static_sets_whole`). The exit: the simulation's `unit_exit_related`
    with the heap gives `statics_rel` with `kept_beta`
    (`rebuild_exit_statics_heap_local`, in the Exit file), whose `rel`
    gives `rebuilt_value_heap` with no premise on locations
    (`rel_rebuilt_value_heap`); `kept_beta` is one to one (`kept_beta_fun`,
    `kept_beta_inj`); `closures_exact_of_bound_once_heap`.
  - New statements, all proved (each `_statement`, and each but the first
    with `_landed` for `landed_fixes`): `type_satisfied_erase`,
    `rewrite_type_sound_heap`, `rewrite_symbol_type_sound_heap`,
    `rewrite_result_type_sound_heap`, `rewrite_unaliased_type_sound_heap`,
    `rewrite_unaliased_symbol_type_sound_heap`,
    `rewrite_unaliased_result_type_sound_heap`,
    `exported_types_sound_heap`, `exported_unaliased_types_sound_heap`,
    `exported_types_sound_end_to_end_heap`,
    `unaliased_types_end_to_end_heap`, `types_end_to_end_term_heap`,
    `unaliased_types_end_to_end_term_heap`, the four last also with
    `_local` and `_local_landed`; and, with no `_landed`,
    `rebuild_exit_relation_heap_holds`,
    `rebuild_exit_relation_whole_heap_holds` and their `_local`. The
    premises are those of the statements without `_heap`, with
    `in_heap_fragment` in place of `in_flow_fragment`; the conclusions of
    the two runs give the rebuilt unit's exit its own heap, observed equal
    to the unit's up to a correspondence of locations.
  - Left: `Mutable_block` has no meaning in the model of types (workstream
    C4), so no valuation satisfies an environment that gives it to a name
    with a value, and the statements say nothing of such an environment;
    with a meaning that reads the cell of a location the argument is to go
    through, as `kept_beta` relates only locations whose allocation is
    kept, to cells of the same mutability and tag (`heaps_rel`). The
    unboxing and changes of representation with the heap
    (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversal*, whose simulation is of
    `in_flow_fragment`). The statements of a graph with
    `least_solution_local` (the lemmas take `least_either`).
- The flow invariant for calls whose code is not known, first step: calls
  of known arity (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach{Spec,Proofs}.v`).
  `ind_call_ok_o1` asked `ind_code_ok` of every code of the unit for such
  a call, so a unit with one such call and one code that changes its
  calling convention was out of the fragment.
  - Where the flow is read. Not on the run of the original unit: that run
    may go wrong where the first rebuilt unit does not (a dead `Let` that
    the rebuild drops), and the refinement of the two rebuilt units must
    hold there too; carrying the original's flow would need a simulation
    of three runs and would hold only where the original does not go
    wrong. Instead the first rebuilt unit (`first_rebuild`, the change of
    calling conventions alone) is analysed again: its facts
    `traverse_unit flag u (first_rebuild ...)` and tables `T1`, which pass
    `check_closed` and `check_local_reads_used`, the unit being in
    `in_heap_fragment`. The run of FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v applies to it
    as it is.
  - The check (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReachSpec.v).
    `alloc_has_code F1 a id`: the allocation `a` has a witness of known
    arity that names `id`, or one with any source. `flow_reach F1 T1 f
    id`: `f` has any source, or a source with `alloc_has_code`.
    `ind_call_ok_f` is `ind_call_ok_o1`, but a call of known arity of `f`
    asks `ind_code_ok_o1` only of the code in `reach f`. `call_ok_f`,
    `calls_expr_f` (and its mutual functions) follow `_o1`.
    `calls_unboxing_reach_nr` takes any `reach`; `calls_unboxing_flow_nr`
    and `calls_unboxing_flow` take `flow_reach F1 T1`.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_flow_refines_cc` (the
    rebuild with unboxing in the mode of the fix refines the first rebuilt
    unit, given `F1`, `T1`), `rebuild_unbox_flow_refines_observably` and
    `rebuild_unbox_flow_refines_observably_local` (with the premises of
    `rebuild_cc_refines_observably`), `calls_unboxing_over1_in_flow`
    (the `_over1` fragment is in the new one, whatever `F1`, `T1`).
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v). A new
    section variable `RCH : variable -> code_id -> Prop` (after `NR`; the
    relations now take it). `ind_targets_f kind callee cs`: a call of
    known arity of a variable `f`, every code in `RCH f` fitting `cs`
    (`ind_compat_w`); a third case of `call_target`.
    `closure_call_answered_r` asks the fit of the code called alone
    (`closure_call_answered_g` is its corollary); `ind_call_answered_f`.
    `flow_ok s`: at a call of `f`, the code of a closure that `f` holds is
    in `RCH f`; `flow_run s`: `flow_ok` of every state that `s` comes to.
    `call_answered`, `main_answered`, `related2_answered` take `flow_ok`
    (used only in this file). `uc_refines_flow` takes `flow_run` of the
    first unit; `uc_refines` now asks that `RCH` hold everywhere.
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). The section
    `RebuildsCalls` is made on `calls_expr_f` with a variable `reach`
    (`RCHf`); `ind_code_shape1_one`, `ind_shape_targets_f`. Section
    `InFlow`: `calls_in_flow`, `calls_unboxing_in_flow_reach`.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_reach` is new, with `flow_run` of the
    first rebuilt unit as a premise; `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over1`
    keeps its type and follows with `reach` true everywhere.
  - The flow (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReachProofs.v).
    `run_flows_called`: `flow_sound_gen_local` with the closure deps of
    the code (`called_by`) and their witness facts. `alloc_reach`,
    `closure_reach`: a closure that `f` holds, of a set or a symbol of a
    static set, has its code in `flow_reach` of `f`.
  - `flow_example`: `KnownArityExample` with the least table of
    `cannot_change_calling_convention` (51 and 50; code 52, called
    directly, changes its calling convention: it loses its parameter and
    result). `calls_unboxing_over1` refuses it, `calls_unboxing_flow`
    accepts it with the least solution of the first rebuilt unit, where
    `flow_reach` of the callee is 50 and 51, and the rebuilt unit refines
    the unit up to observation.
  - Left:
    - Calls of unknown arity still ask every code: the callee may be a
      partial application, and `flows` of a partial application at `f`
      says only that what the last call of its chain returns may come to
      `f`, not its code. FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v reaches that code
      through the witnesses the call reads (`call_reads_hu`, `wchain`):
      the reach of such a call would be the code whose witness of unknown
      arity is used (the second rule of `derive_cannot_change`, on the
      analysis of the first rebuilt unit), or a run invariant giving the
      code of each partial application.
    - Over-applications: `cont_rel_over` still asks `ind_targets` of every
      code for what is left of the call.
    - The flow of the original unit carried through the change of calling
      conventions, in place of the analysis of the first rebuilt unit
      (whose premises the statements ask): only where the original run
      does not go wrong (above).
  - No counterexample: the change of calling conventions already shows
    that a call of unknown arity calls code whose witness of unknown arity
    is used (`unchanged_of_unknown`), and a call of known arity whose code
    is not known calls code in `cannot_change` (`direct_call_recognized`);
    nothing was found false.
- The three units that reach a failure site of the fix are excluded by
  three premises, with which none of the three sites is reached
  (FlambdaReaperFixFailsAll2{Spec,Proofs}.v, new):
  `fix_never_fails_all2_statement`.
  - The premises, each a check of the term or, as `callees_promised`, of
    the flow:
    - `results_match_callees`: every call expects exactly as many results
      as each code of the unit that it promises to call returns
      (`call_promises`); `results_within_callees` with an equality
      (`results_match_within_statement`). Excludes
      `more_results_statement`. The compiler guarantees it: Simplify
      checks the return arity of every full application of code it knows
      (simplify_apply_expr.ml, "Wrong return arity for direct OCaml
      function call", a fatal error under kind checks and `Invalid`
      otherwise), and it makes the direct calls and the calls of known
      arity. Like `results_within_callees`, it reads every code of the
      right number of parameters for a call of known arity with no list
      of code ids, so that it may refuse a unit where such a call and
      unrelated code differ in their results.
    - `unit_codes_have_params`: every code of the unit has a parameter
      (`codes_have_params` of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver1Spec.v, of
      `bound_code_metadata e`, lists the same code). Excludes
      `no_parameter_code_statement`. The compiler guarantees it:
      `Lambda.lfunction'` asserts that a function has a parameter
      (lambda.ml), and the code that Flambda makes besides (wrappers of
      partial and tupled applications) has parameters.
    - `known_callees_have_code`: for every direct call and call of known
      arity whose callee is a name with a source, the flow finds code
      (`code_id_actually_directly_called` is not `Some []`). A check of
      the flow, as `callees_promised`. Excludes
      `empty_callee_codes_statement`. Believed guaranteed: Simplify makes
      such a call only of a callee whose type is a closure of known code,
      and its types and the flow are both sound, so that a source of the
      callee is that closure, with the call witness that the traversal
      gives it. A closure of `Deleted` code, to which the traversal gives
      no witness, would fail it if called; no such unit has been
      found.
    - `premises_exclude_units_statement`: each premise fails of the unit
      that it excludes and holds of the other two.
  - `fix_never_fails_all2_statement`: the premises of
    `fix_never_fails_callees_statement`, with `results_match_callees` in
    place of `results_within_callees`, and the other two. Conclusion:
    that of `fix_never_fails_all_statement`, and
    - `calls_fit`: each call that the rebuild makes, if its code changes
      its calling convention, has as many return decisions as it expects
      results (the `List.fold_left2` of `make_apply_wrapper`) and a group
      of arguments ("Empty argument groups in direct apply rewrite": a
      direct call has one by `in_flow_fragment`, a call of known arity by
      `call_promises`); and the flow finds code for its callee if it has
      a source ("Empty potentially called code_ids").
    - `codes_grouped`: the parameters of the code dep of each code of the
      unit fall in a group at least ("add_deps: no params for code ID",
      "Empty parameter groups when changing calling convention"); the
      traversal's `at_least_one_group` changes nothing.
  - The proof: `fix_never_fails_callees`, `uses_fit_walk`, the code deps
    of `code_edges`, `fragment_calls` (every call of a term of the
    fragment is of the fragment), and `lookup_codes` (the metadata that
    `apply_change_u` finds is that of code of the unit).
  - Sites of the "A?" class of the entry "The failure sites of the fix's
    rebuild and analyses", now proved never reached
    (FlambdaReaperFixFailsSlots{Spec,Proofs}.v, new):
    - `changed_decls_all_renamed_statement`: of every `Let` of a closure
      of a unit of the flow fragment, well formed and binding each
      variable once, with a least solution and a table `dominated` that
      passes `check_dominated`, a representation that the changes give
      its variable has a new slot for every function slot of the
      declarations of its set. rebuild.ml: `rewrite_set_of_closures`
      (480, "Could not find rewritten function slot"), and
      slot_offsets_analysis.ml (41); the model's `rename_function_slot`
      keeps the slot there. The proof follows
      `changed_slot_current_statement`: the representation is that of
      the set of a closure of `tcr` (`rinv` of
      FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalProofs.v), whose function slots are
      those that the `Let` holds (`let_edges`, `function_slots_of_sets`).
      Static sets (1916) and projections (1608, 1609) are not covered.
    - `changed_current_renamed_statement`: every representation that
      `unit_changed_representations` makes has a new slot for its

      `cinv_fold`, like `binv_fold`). rebuild.ml: `Function_slot.Map.find`
      of the current slot in `rewrite_field_access_chg_repr` (671) and
      `load_field_from_value_which_is_being_unboxed` (1445); the model's

    - `call_args_grouped_statement`: with a fourth check of the term,

      (`group_by_parameter`) cut or drop none. rebuild.ml
      (`rebuild_apply`, 1279, 1283) and traverse_acc.ml
      (`make_unknown_arity_apply_widget`, 653) stop there
      (`Flambda_arity.group_by_parameter`); the model cuts. The flow
      fragment does not ask it (a call of two arguments of the arity
      `[Singleton Value]` is of the fragment); the compiler guarantees
      it, as `Apply.create` checks it of every call (apply_expr.ml,
      "Length of argument and arity lists disagree"). The parameters of
      the code dep of code have as many nodes as its arity has
      components (`code_edges`), so that their groups (traverse_acc.ml
      596, unboxing_analysis.ml 1216) cut none; not stated. The
      `List.map3` of `rebuild_apply`
      (1160) also needs the arity of the arguments of a direct call to be
      that of the parameters of its code (Simplify checks it:
      "Mismatched arities for arguments to direct OCaml function call");
      not stated.
  - Reclassified (B): the `assert` of `get_allocation_point`
    (points_to_analysis.ml 1255) is reached only through
    `get_single_source`, which only types_rewriter.ml calls, left out of
    the enumeration.
  - Left of the "A?" class: an argument for an unboxed parameter that is
    not unboxed alike (`get_simple_unboxable`, `fold2_subset*`),
    `bind_field_to_simple` and the field reads of unboxed or changed
    values (607-636, 1369-1445 but the current slot), the fields of

    sets (1905-1916) and of projections (1608, 1609), the shapes of

    1073), and the `List.map3` of `rebuild_apply` (1160). Each needs a
    property of the derived tables (`dominated`, `to_unbox`) or of the
    facts of the traversal beyond those now proved.
  - Not found false.
  - New statements: `fix_never_fails_all2_statement`,
    `results_match_within_statement`,
    `premises_exclude_units_statement`,
    `changed_decls_all_renamed_statement`,
    `changed_current_renamed_statement`, `call_args_grouped_statement`.
    No statement changed. The Makefile lists
    FlambdaReaperFixFailsAll2{Spec,Proofs}.v and
    FlambdaReaperFixFailsSlots{Spec,Proofs}.v.
- The value slots of the closures of the traversal, and a third
  fragment of the refinement across changes of representation
  (`FlambdaReaperClosuresTablesSlots{Spec,Proofs}.v`).
  - `closure_value_slots`: in the graph of the traversal (any mode) of a
    unit of the flow fragment that binds each variable once, the
    variable of a `Let x _ (Closure_of_set slot decls vs)` holds at a
    value slot `w` only what `vs` has there (`lookup w vs = Some a`, at
    `node_of_simple a`). A fresh induction on the traversal (`vinv`,
    `traverse_vinv`): the steps other than sets of closures add no
    constructor at a slot (`ClosuresExactProofs.ns_ok`); a `Let` of a
    closure adds the facts of its group (`record_set_group`) at its
    variable, the variables of the projections of its closures and made
    nodes (`closures_of_set_spec`, `set_closures_projs`); a static set
    at its symbol.
  - `changed_field_in_set`: with the premises of `changed_field_used`,
    the value slot of each field of the representation of `x` is one of
    `vs` (the allocation of `tcr` holds the slot, `field_origin`, and
    holds what `x` holds, `closure_node_exact`).
  - The fragment that drops it: `repr_field_frag3_ok` asks nothing of a
    slot that `vs` lacks, `changed_set_frag3_ok`, and
    `repr_unboxing_frag3_nr`, built with `repr_expr_with` (no new copy
    of the term checker; `with_mono` relates `repr_expr_with` for two
    checks of a changed set). `repr_unboxing_frag3_sound`: it implies
    `repr_unboxing_frag2_nr`. New statements `rebuild_repr_frag3_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_repr_frag3_observably`, `rebuild_repr_frag3_local`, the
    `frag2` ones with the weaker fragment; the old statements are
    unchanged.
  - Not derived: that the simple of a field not unboxed is not unboxed
    (`plain_simple`). The route: if the simple `n` were dominated by an
    allocation `a0` to unbox, then (i) a field read with any usage makes
    `n` of any usage, hence `a0` (an inversion of `sources` in a least
    solution: `sources n a0` is a chain of aliases from `n` down to `a0`,
    along which `any_usage` goes), so `a0` is in `cannot_unbox0`; (ii) the
    field has one source, `a0`, since `n` is dominated; (iii) the case
    left is a field whose reads have known uses, one of them `y` not
    dominated by an allocation to unbox. Since `y` is an alias of `n`,
    `sources y a0`, and `y` would need another source or any source:
    both come only through facts that put something into `y`, and the
    variable of a projection of a value slot receives only its read in
    the traversal. That is an invariant of the traversal not proved
    anywhere (`TypesTraversalUnaliasedProofs.named_tgt` asks nothing of
    such a variable); with it, the inversion of `alias y _` in a least
    solution leaves only the value slots of the closures that the use
    may be, which are those of the set (`cannot_change_representation0`
    of the closure otherwise).
  - The bound of `mk_unboxed_fields` (`cyclic_in_slot_counterexample`),
    left as is. The change: in `name_unboxed_fields` and
    `changed_representations` (FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v), the fuel
    `S (length to_unbox)` becomes
    `S (length to_unbox * 2 ^ length (f_accessor F))`, and the comment on
    `mk_unboxed_fields` says why. A recursive call depends only on the
    allocation it unboxes, which is in `to_unbox`, and on the names its
    field flows to (`flow_to`), all of them targets of accessors; a
    branch that meets one such pair twice never ends in the compiler,
    whose `mk_unboxed_fields` has no bound. So on a unit on which the
    compiler ends, a branch has at most `length to_unbox * 2 ^ length
    (f_accessor F)` levels below the top, and that fuel never cuts. It
    is only a bound: the recursion goes as deep as the reads. With it,
    the unboxing of a slot (from its closure) and that of the name it
    holds are cut nowhere, which "unboxed alike" needs.
  - Found, not checked with the compiler: a unit on which the
    compiler's `mk_unboxed_fields` looks like it never ends. A loop
    `rec k21 (x30)` makes `x10 = [x30; 7]`, reads `x11 = x30.(0)` and
    gives `k21` either `x10` or `x11`; the loop is entered from a handler
    never applied, so `x30`'s only source is `x10`, which is to unbox.
    The field 0 of `x10` flows to `{x11}`, and the uses of `x11` (an
    alias of `x30`) read field 0 into `x11` again: the same pair, so the
    model's unboxing of `x10` is cut at the bound (computed with the
    least solution: depth 2, `to_unbox = [x10]`). No fuel makes the
    model agree with the compiler there; to be confirmed with a build of
    the compiler.
- The flow invariant for calls whose code is not known, second step:
  calls of unknown arity with one group of arguments
  (`FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2{Spec,Proofs}.v`). Such a call asked
  `ind_code_ok` of every code: its callee may be a partial application,
  whose code `flow_reach` (sources with a witness of known arity) does
  not give.
  - Choice: a reach read off the sources of the callee, partial
    applications included, rather than the code whose witness of unknown
    arity the call uses (`call_reads_hu`): the latter needs the facts of
    the call and the witness chains of FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v,
    while the former needs only the run of FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v. A
    partial application of a closure of code `A`, `k` groups given, is at
    the callee through the chain of the `k` calls that made it
    (`call_chain`, `chain_end` of `flows`); the code dep of `A` has a
    witness of unknown arity per group, the first result of each but the
    last an allocation whose call witness is the next
    (`code_chain_edges`), each naming `A`. So the allocation that the
    `k`-th call returns may come to the callee and its call witness of
    unknown arity names `A`.
  - The check (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2Spec.v).
    `alloc_unknown_code F1 a id`: `a` has a call witness of unknown arity
    that names `id`. `flow_reach_u`: `flow_reach`, or a source with
    `alloc_unknown_code`. `flow_reach2 F1 T1 b`: `flow_reach` for a call
    of known arity (`b`), `flow_reach_u` otherwise. `ind_call_ok_f2` is
    `ind_call_ok_o1`, but a call that `never_over` says over-applies
    nothing asks `ind_code_ok_o1` only of the code in `reach b f`.
    `call_ok_f2`, `calls_expr_f2` (and its mutual functions),
    `calls_unboxing_reach2_nr`, `calls_unboxing_flow2_nr` and
    `calls_unboxing_flow2` follow the `_f` functions.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_flow2_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_unbox_flow2_refines_observably`,
    `rebuild_unbox_flow2_refines_observably_local` (the premises of the
    `_flow` statements), and `calls_unboxing_flow_in_flow2` (for the same
    analysis of the first rebuilt unit).
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v). `RCH` now
    takes whether the call is of known arity: `RCH true f`, the code of a
    closure that `f` holds; `RCH false f`, the code of the closure that a
    call of what `f` holds calls (`called_closure`: that of a partial
    application). `ind_targets_f b`: of known arity if `b`, with one group
    otherwise, the code in `RCH b f` fitting the shape and, for one group,
    having a parameter. `flow_ok` gives both. `ind_call_answered_f` takes
    `b`; for one group it is no over-application, by the parameter of the
    closure's code (`closure_one_no_over`) or the partial application that
    the machine made (`partial_one_no_over`). `uc_refines` asks that `RCH`
    hold everywhere, for both values of `b`.
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). The section
    `RebuildsCalls` is made on `calls_expr_f2` with
    `reach : bool -> variable -> code_id -> bool`; `ind_shape_targets_f`
    gives `ind_targets_f` for every call that over-applies nothing.
    Section `InFlow2`: `calls_in_flow2`, for any `reach'` that is `reach`
    for calls of known arity; `reach2`; `calls_unboxing_in_flow2_reach`.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_reach2` is new;
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_reach` now takes `flow_run` for
    `reach2 reach` and follows from it, as
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over1` does.
  - The flow (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2Proofs.v).
    `run_flows_called2`: `run_flows_called` with the witness chains of the
    code of the static environment (`code_chains`) and its partial
    applications (`env_pa_ok`). `chain_reach`: the witnesses of a chain
    of calls stand for those of the code dep, group by group.
    `closure_alloc`, `partial_reach`.
  - `flow2_example`: `OneGroupExample` (`f 5`, of unknown arity, of 50 or
    51) with the least table of `cannot_change_calling_convention`, which
    has 50 and 51 and not 52 (called directly; it changes its calling
    convention). `calls_unboxing_flow` refuses it, `calls_unboxing_flow2`
    accepts it with the least solution of the first rebuilt unit, and the
    rebuilt unit refines the unit up to observation.
  - Left: calls with two groups or more, which may over-apply, still ask
    every code, and so does what is left of an over-application
    (`cont_rel_over` holds `ind_targets`). Narrowing it would need the
    flow of the value that the code returns into the over-application:
    the chain of the call goes on past the code's last witness
    (FlambdaReaperFlowProofs' `pending`), and `cont_rel_over` would need
    the reach of that chain. Nothing was found false; no counterexample.
- The local unboxing with the primitives of the heap
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesHeap{Spec,Proofs}.v, new), and the
  statements of a graph of the rewriting of types with the heap and local
  fields (FlambdaReaperTypesHeap{Spec,Proofs}.v).
  - How the unboxing meets the heap: what the fragment unboxes is an
    immutable block of values or a boxed number (`unboxed_binding_ok`),
    which the machine makes as a value, not a cell; an unboxed variable is
    no argument of a primitive of the heap (`plain_binding_ok`), so no
    block unboxed is stored into a cell or compared physically; a field of
    an unboxed block may hold a location, which the variable of the field
    holds, so a store into the cell is seen alike through either. The two
    rebuilds run the same primitives of the heap on the same values, so
    the heaps are one.
  - `rebuild_unbox_refines_rebuild_heap`: the statement of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesSpec.v for `in_heap_fragment`, still with
    `program_refines` (heaps equal). `rebuild_unbox_refines_up_to_heap`
    and `_local`: the observable statements for `in_heap_fragment`, with
    `program_refines_up_to_heap`, composed with
    `rebuild_refines_up_to_heap{,_local}` (`refines_up_to_heap_refines`).
  - Proofs: the relation `ub` and its simulation are copied as `ubh`,
    `relatedh` (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesProofs.v is reused for the
    rest, through abbreviations): the `Let` of a variable that is not
    unboxed may be of a primitive of the heap (`named_plain_exec` runs it
    alike on the one heap), an unboxed allocation reads no heap
    (`named_reads_heap`, `exec_named_not_reading`), a read of an unboxed
    variable runs on a heap as without one (`read_exec`: its argument is a
    block, a boxed number, or a location, of which the unboxed fields hold
    no path), and the heaps of the two runs are one, not empty.
    `rebuild_ubh` is `rebuild_ub` on the edges with the heap
    (`traverse_unit_facts_gen`).
  - `heap_example`: an immutable block holding a cell, unboxed, the cell
    stored into through the field read; the unit and its rebuilds exit
    with 2 and the same heap. `cell_example`: the rules unbox a cell that
    is only read (`derive_to_unbox`, as the compiler unboxes a mutable
    block that no store reaches: traverse.ml gives a mutable `Make_block`
    the facts of an immutable one, and a store gives the block any usage);
    the fragment leaves it out, and the rebuilt unit exits with the same
    values and one cell fewer: it refines the plain rebuild up to the
    heap and not by `program_refines`. Not a fault: the cell is not
    observable at the exit.
  - Types, local fields: `rewrite_type_sound_heap_local`,
    `rewrite_symbol_type_sound_heap_local`,
    `rewrite_result_type_sound_heap_local`,
    `rewrite_unaliased_type_sound_heap_local`,
    `unaliased_symbol_type_sound_heap_local`,
    `unaliased_result_type_sound_heap_local` (two names shortened), each
    with `_landed`, for `least_solution_local`.
  - Left: the unboxing of cells (`cell_example`). Plan: a fragment that
    admits `Make_block_with_identity` of an unboxed variable and mutable
    loads of it (stores are already excluded, an unboxed variable being no
    argument of another primitive); a simulation whose plain variables are
    related up to a correspondence of locations, as `rel`/`sim` of
    FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v (the plain run allocates the cell, the
    other does not, so later locations shift), with the cell of an
    unboxed variable holding, in the first heap, what its fields hold;
    and the statement up to the heap only.
- The repair after the core took subkinds (decision D25): code states
  what its parameters and its results are beyond their kinds
  (`cm_params_subkinds`, `cm_result_subkinds`), a handler what its
  parameters are (`hm_params_subkinds`), and the machine is undefined
  where the body of code is entered, or a handler applied, with values
  that are not of what the parameters state (`values_of_subkinds`, in
  `call_code` and `apply_cont_value`); what code states of its results
  is not tested.
  - The model is the reaper without types
    (`Traverse.With_types.Without_types`), whose
    `rewrite_kind_with_subkind` is `erase_subkind` everywhere the
    compiler rewrites a subkind (the header of
    `FlambdaReaperRebuild.v`). The compiler's `erase_subkind` is
    `Flambda_kind.With_subkind.erase_subkind`: `Anything` and nullable
    for the kind `Value`, and a naked kind as it is, of which nothing is
    stated. So an entry erased states nothing, and the model's rebuilt
    terms state what the compiler's state without types, written as the
    test tool converts them: the empty list. Code that keeps its calling
    convention has what its parameters and its results state erased
    (`erase_code_subkinds`, as `compute_code_changes` rewrites the
    arities): in the plain rebuild, in the rebuild with changes of
    calling convention and in the unboxing. Code that changes its
    calling convention (`changed_code_metadata`,
    `changed_code_metadata_u`) and every rebuilt handler
    (`with_params_kinds`, and the wrappers, `make_handler_metadata`)
    state nothing: what is kept is erased (`should_keep_param`, `Keep`
    of `compute_code_changes`, `Keep_unused` of a wrapper), and the
    fields of an unboxed parameter are `With_subkind.anything`
    (`get_parameters`). The arities of a call have no subkinds in the
    model, so their rewriting (`rebuild_apply`) has nothing to act on.
    (The compiler's `erase_subkind` kept `Anything` and
    `Tagged_immediate` and whether the value may be null, its poison
    being an immediate that is not null; the core accepts poison only
    where nothing is stated, and the compiler's reaper now erases as the
    model does, so that difference is gone.)
  - The proofs. Where the run of the unit takes a step, it has passed
    the new test; without types the rebuilt code and handlers state
    nothing, so the rebuilt run passes it (FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v:
    `rebuilt_code_subkinds_sound`, `with_params_kinds_subkinds`,
    `erased_subkinds_say_nothing`). The simulations between two
    rebuilds (the unboxing: `ub`, `ubh`, `uc`; the closures: `rr`) now
    carry that the handlers, the wrappers and the code of the second
    run state nothing: a premise of their `let_cont`, `handlers_cons`
    and `cont_rel_closure` cases, a conjunct of `wrap_u_ok`,
    `wrap_both_ok` and of each `code_frame`, which the rebuilds give.
    Where the two runs apply one handler (the closures), the test reads
    only what the static environments bind the symbols to, which is the
    same in both (`values_of_subkinds_same_values`). The rest is the
    extra case of the test in the proofs that step through `call_code`
    and `apply_cont_value`.
  - No statement of the rebuilds changed or took a premise, and none
    was found false. The examples state no subkind
    (`make_code_metadata`, `make_handler_metadata`, and the empty lists
    of the records written out), so the terms they compute are as
    before.
  - The reaper with types. `rewrite_kind_with_subkind` is transcribed
    in FlambdaReaperTypes.v (`rewrite_subkind`, with
    `rewrite_boxed_number_kind` and `erase_subkind`), and
    FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v states what the subkinds it keeps promise
    of the values of the rebuilt run. Proved, with the machine's own
    test (`FlambdaSubkindSpec.value_at`, under which poison is of
    `Anything` that may be null and of nothing else):
    `rewrite_kind_with_subkind_sound`, what a parameter states,
    rewritten under the metadata of its node, holds of the value that
    the rebuild makes of one that the first run gives it
    (`rebuilt_value`, under the least solution, with
    `statics_rebuilt`) and that is of what the parameter stated. Under
    metadata that erases, nothing is stated. Under a single source or
    usages, `Anything` is erased, which forgets that the value is not
    null: it may be a boxed number whose contents no use reads, which
    the rebuild makes poison (`BoxedCounterexample`). A kept
    `Tagged_immediate` is of a constant, which the rebuild keeps. A
    boxed number whose contents no use reads has its subkind erased
    (`rewrite_boxed_number_kind`). A variant keeps the subkinds of the
    fields that its allocations use, rewritten in turn, and erases the
    others, which the rebuild may make poison. Under any usage
    everything is kept: every allocation that the value may come from
    has any usage, so no value is made poison
    (`any_usage_not_poison`). No case of the compiler's rule keeps a
    subkind that the rebuild's poison can break. The subkinds of arrays
    and of a block of floats are `Anything` in the model, with whether
    the value may be null, and are erased there where the compiler keeps
    them at their head: the model states less, and the naked fields of
    a block of floats, poison or not, are not tested.
  - Before, the compiler kept `Anything` under a single source or
    usages, and with the core's test that was false
    (`BoxedCounterexample`, at `Anything` that may not be null): the
    statement was then proved with poison read as a value that is not
    null, and the counterexample stood beside it. The compiler's rule now
    erases there; that reading, and the counterexample, are gone.
  - The rebuild with types (the compiler's `With_types` mode). The
    rebuilds take what the parameters kept state as an argument,
    `types : subkinds_rewriting` (FlambdaReaperRebuild.v), after
    `first_fresh`: `None` is the reaper without types, whose rebuilt
    code and handlers state nothing as before, and `Some rewrite` gives
    each parameter kept by kept code (`kept_code_metadata`), by code
    that changes its calling convention (`changed_code_metadata_with`)
    and by a handler (`kept_handler_metadata`) what `rewrite` gives of
    it and of what it stated (`rewritten_subkinds`; a list that says
    nothing is the empty list). FlambdaReaperTypes.v instantiates it
    with `rewrite_kind_with_subkind` at the node of each parameter
    (`subkinds_of_context`): `rebuild_unit_with_types`,
    `rebuild_unit_cc_with_types` and their `_from_tables` forms, which
    FlambdaExtract.v extracts. `rebuild_unit`, `rebuild_unit_cc` and the
    statements of the rebuilds are unchanged. What code states of its
    results (not tested by the machine) and what the parameters of a
    wrapper state (the compiler takes it from the continuation's kept
    parameters) state nothing with types too; the unboxing
    (`rebuild_expr_u`) has no form with types.
  - The simulation of FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v is over any `types`,
    under `types_sound`: what is stated of a parameter kept holds of
    every value of the second run related by `rel` to one of the first
    that is of what the parameter stated, for a parameter with no use of
    every value, where the static environments are related
    (`statics_rel`) and every symbol bound is an allocation of its own
    (`statics_sourced`, from the invariant of the flow). Entering a
    handler and calling code (direct, indirect, tupled, with a change of
    calling convention) then pass the machine's test in the second run
    (`kept_handler_subkinds_sound`, `rebuilt_code_subkinds_sound`,
    `rebuilt_code_subkinds_sound_cc`); `call_step` and `full_call_step`
    (FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v) give the test of the first run.
    `rebuild_with_refines_checked_local` and
    `rebuild_cc_with_refines_checked_local` are the refinements for any
    `types` with `types_sound_all`; the former lemmas are their `None`
    instances.
  - FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v proves the rewriting sound for any
    relation that has of the values what the proof reads
    (`Subkinds_sound`, over `R`), of which `rebuilt_value` gives
    `rewrite_kind_with_subkind_sound` as before and the simulation's
    `rel` and `statics_rel` give `types_sound_all` of
    `subkinds_of_context` (`subkinds_of_context_sound_all`), under the
    least solution and the check of `field_used_exact`. A parameter
    with no use has metadata that erases, so nothing is stated of it.
  - New statements, proved (FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v):
    `rebuild_with_types_refines_local_statement` and
    `rebuild_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement`, the
    refinements of the rebuild without types for the rebuild with
    types, and `rebuild_cc_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement`
    for the rebuild with types and changes of calling convention, each
    with the premises of the statement without types and the least
    solution, for any sets of closures given to the rewriting. No fault
    found.
  - The unboxing with types. The rebuild with unboxing and changes of
    representation (FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v: `rebuild_expr_u` and the
    functions with it, `rebuild_apply_u`, and `rebuild_fails_u`, which
    reads the rebuilt term) takes `types` and, for the reaper with types,
    the parameters of the continuations of the unit with what they state
    (`cont_params`, made by `conts_params`: those of a handler; the nodes
    of the results of code in its code dep for its return continuation),
    after `changed`; `make_apply_wrapper_u` takes what the parameters of
    the wrapper state, before the environment. With `None` the functions
    are those before: `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_mode` passes `None []`,
    and no statement changed. With `Some rewrite`, as rebuild.ml and
    unboxing_analysis.ml state:
    - a handler: each parameter kept, with a use or not
      (`should_keep_param`), states what `rewrite` gives at its variable,
      each field of a parameter unboxed nothing (`get_parameters`,
      `With_subkind.anything`): `decisions_subkinds`;
    - code that keeps its calling convention: each parameter and each
      result what `rewrite` gives at its node in the code dep
      (`code_dep.params`, `code_dep.return`, `compute_code_changes`);
    - code that changes: the fields of an unboxed `my_closure` nothing,
      then each parameter kept what `rewrite` gives at its node in the
      code dep, each field of one unboxed nothing; its results the same
      (`arity_of_decisions`);
    - the wrapper of a call of code that keeps its calling convention:
      each result what the parameter of the continuation of the call
      states, rewritten, where the continuation keeps it, and nothing
      where it deletes or unboxes it (the return arity of
      `rebuild_apply`, from `cont_params_to_keep`); of a call of code
      that changes: the results of the callee as its code dep and its
      metadata give them (its `return_decisions`, `callee_results_u`).
    `subkinds_rewriting` now rewrites at a node, so that code is
    rewritten at the nodes of its code dep, as the compiler does. The
    plain and calling-convention rebuilds still rewrite code at the
    variables of its parameters, and state nothing of its results; the
    unboxing follows the compiler there. FlambdaReaperTypes.v:
    `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_with_types` and
    `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed_with_types`, with the arguments of
    the rebuilds without types (`rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_mode_with_types`;
    the subkinds read neither the sets of closures nor the changes of
    representation of the context), extracted.
  - What the compiler states that the model cannot: the subkinds of the
    arities of a call (`args_arity` and `return_arity` in
    `rebuild_apply`) and those of the fields of a block
    (`rebuild_make_block_default_case`), which the model's terms do not
    have. No subkind that the rule states of a parameter of code, of a
    handler or of a wrapper was found that the rebuild's poison breaks:
    each is rewritten under the metadata of a node at which the values
    given are, as for the rebuild with types, and the fields of unboxed
    parameters state nothing.
  - Left: the refinements of the unboxing with types. The simulations
    between the rebuilds (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefines*: `ub`, `ubh`,
    `uc`, `rr`) run with `None`; with types they would need what the
    third run's code states, at the nodes of its code dep, to hold of
    the values that the second run's code takes at its variables. And
    what the plain and calling-convention rebuilds' wrappers state with
    types.
- The test tool after the subkinds of the core. The model's rebuilds
  state no subkind; the compiler's reaper, with types, keeps the
  subkinds of what its rewriting of kinds finds unchanged. The tool
  compares the two terms also with no subkind on either side
  (`subkinds_erased`, in `flreaper.ml`), and says "the same up to bound
  names and subkinds" where only those differ. Where the run of the
  compiler's output is undefined, the tool runs it again with no
  subkind and prints both, which tells a fault of the subkinds from one
  of the term. Before the reaper erased subkinds fully (the change
  "reaper: erase subkinds fully, nullability included"), it kept
  `Anything`, `Tagged_immediate` and the nullability where its poison,
  a tagged immediate, could arrive, and the core's machine, which
  accepts poison only where nothing is stated, found the compiler's
  output undefined in 14 of the 824 units of the corpus of
  `tests/semantics`, among them three examples of
  `tests/semantics/reaper/` (`escaping_parameter_argument`,
  `lambda_lifting_dead_callee`, `unbox_kept_result_gadt`), each of
  which behaved as the input with no subkind. With the full erase, none
  is.
- The depth of the unboxing of a block (`mk_unboxed_fields`), which the
  model bounded by `S (length to_unbox)`, taking a chain of allocations
  to unbox, each in a field of the one before, to be no deeper. A block
  made by a loop that holds the block of the iteration before is such a
  chain of one allocation, as deep as the reads go: in `CyclicInSlot` a
  slot and the block it holds were cut at two depths
  (`cyclic_in_slot_counterexample`). The compiler's `mk_unboxed_fields`
  has no bound.
  - The fix (FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v). `mk_unboxed_fields_with` is the
    compiler's function with its recursive call as an argument (`nest`,
    `None` where the model cuts). `mk_unboxed_fields_full`, which
    `name_unboxed_fields` and `changed_representations` now call, runs
    it `length (t_sources T) * 2 ^ length (t_usages T)` levels deep:
    `nest_levels l` makes `2 ^ length l` levels by a structural
    recursion on `l` that nests a call in a call
    (`nest_levels l (nest_levels l nest)`), and `unboxing_nest` that
    many for each pair of `t_sources T`. The levels are partial
    applications, made as the recursion goes down: the extracted OCaml
    builds no number (`nat` is unary there), a level that the recursion
    does not reach costs nothing, and where the compiler's recursion
    ends the model does the compiler's work. `mk_unboxed_fields`, of a
    `nat` depth, is kept with the same body, for the proofs. The
    extracted signatures of `unit_unboxed_fields`,
    `unit_changed_representations`, `name_unboxed_fields`,
    `changed_representations` and the rebuilds are unchanged;
    `mk_unboxed_fields` is no longer extracted, and the four new
    functions are (tests/semantics/extracted/FlambdaReaperUnboxing.ml,
    regenerated).
  - The bound. A recursive call depends only on the source of the
    field, one of `t_sources T`, and on which first names of the pairs
    of `t_usages T` are among the names that it is read into
    (`get_direct_usages` reads nothing else). A chain of calls in which
    a call comes back is infinite, and the compiler's recursion does not
    end on it; so where it ends, the calls of a chain are distinct, and
    at most `length (t_sources T) * 2 ^ length (t_usages T)`. The
    proposed `length to_unbox * 2 ^ length (f_accessor F)` needs the
    source to be in `to_unbox`, which holds of the tables of the rules
    only (through `dominated`), and the names that a field of the top
    level of a changed representation is read into to be targets of
    accessors, which `t_reading_field` does not give; the bound taken
    holds for any facts and tables.
  - Statements (FlambdaReaperUnboxingFuel{Spec,Proofs}.v, new).
    `unboxes`: the compiler's recursion, with no bound, as a relation
    (with the model's variables where the compiler stops with a fatal
    error); `nested_call`; `unboxing_ends` (no infinite chain of nested
    calls: the compiler's recursion ends); `calls_within n` (no chain
    of more than `n`). `full_depth`: `mk_unboxed_fields_full` is
    `mk_unboxed_fields` at `unboxing_depth T`, for any facts and tables.
    `ends_within`, `within_ends`: the recursion ends if and only if no
    chain is longer than `unboxing_depth T`. `unboxes_ends`: the
    relation gives a result, for every `next`, where the recursion ends,
    and only there. `fuel_enough`: where the relation gives a result,
    the model gives that one (so it gives at most one). `more_fuel`:
    where the recursion ends, every depth from `unboxing_depth T` on
    gives the model's result. `fuel_changes`: where it does not end, one
    more level changes the unboxing, at every depth. `ends_stable`: so
    it ends if and only if the depths `unboxing_depth T` and one more
    agree. `name_unboxed_fields_relation`: `fuel_enough` for the unboxed
    fields of a name. Proofs: no call of a chain reaches the one it is
    made from (`ends_no_cycle`), each is one of `children`, of length
    `unboxing_depth T`, so a chain, whose calls are distinct, is no
    longer (`within_from`); where two depths agree, the unboxings of
    each nested call agree a level below (`agree_within`, with
    `mk_next`: the name after the variables is given by their
    number).
  - The recursion that does not end (`loop_field`, `LoopField`), found
    before by reading the model, now stated: a loop makes `x10 = [x30;
    7]`, reads `x11 = x30.(0)` and `x12 = x30.(1)`, and goes round with
    `x10` or `x11`, or returns `x12`; it is entered from a handler never
    applied. Its tables pass `check_least` and `x10` is the one
    allocation to unbox. The field 0 of `x10` is read into `x11`, whose
    uses read the same fields: the call is the one it is made from, and
    the recursion on `x10` does not end (not run on the compiler). The
    depth `n` gives fields that nest `n` levels, and those of two depths
    `n`, `S n` are not unboxed alike; the model gives
    `unboxing_depth T` = 48 levels. What the model does there: the field
    is unboxed that many levels, then a variable. Not faithful, and no
    fuel is: the compiler has no result, and the model's depends on the
    fuel. On a unit of real size, `2 ^ length (t_usages T)` levels would
    not be finished by the extracted tool either. Cutting where a call
    comes back on its chain would give the same result where the
    compiler's recursion ends and a small one where it does not; not
    done, being a further choice where the compiler has none.
  - `cyclic_in_slot_counterexample` is now `cyclic_in_slot_alike`, the
    statement changed (unavoidably: it is false of the fixed model).
    The slot is unboxed three levels deep, its fields are among those of
    `x10`, and it passes `repr_field_frag_ok`. "Unboxed alike" is not
    derived. It needs that the compiler's recursion end (on
    `LoopField`, two depths are never alike, and a slot is unboxed one
    level below its closure while the name of its block is unboxed from
    the top: so a premise that it ends, which no Spec statement has);
    the inversion of a least solution, for the variable of the slot to
    be dominated by the one source of the slot, which is to unbox (as
    for `plain_c`); that the uses of the reads of the slot be among
    those of the variable; and that no field be read into names some to
    be unboxed and some not (the compiler's fatal error, which the rules
    allow: `mixed_mutable_field`).
  - Repaired: the proofs that unfolded `name_unboxed_fields` or
    `changed_representations` rewrite with `name_unboxed_fields_depth`
    and `changed_representations_depth` (FuelProofs), and the lemmas
    that named the depth `S (length to_unbox)` name `unboxing_depth T`
    (UnboxingRefinesProofs, TypesProofs, TypesTraversalProofs,
    ClosuresTablesProofs, ClosuresTablesFieldsProofs,
    FixFailsSlotsProofs). No other statement of a Spec file changed or
    took a premise. The Makefile lists
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingFuel{Spec,Proofs}.v.
- Over-applications for calls of unknown arity with two groups of
  arguments or more (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver2{Spec,Proofs}.v,
  new). Such a call may over-apply, and `ind_code_ok_o1` asked of every
  code a single result of kind `Value` with a use.
  - What the compiler does. A call of unknown arity reads a witness for
    each group of its arguments (`make_unknown_arity_apply_widget`): the
    first result of each witness but the last into a variable from which
    it reads the next witness, and the code id of each into `called`, which
    has any usage; so each of those variables has a use. Where the call
    over-applies code `A`, the witness of the call for the last group of
    `A` stands for the last witness of unknown arity of `A`, whose first
    result is that of `A`: the rule of `alias_constructor_usage` aliases the
    variable of the call to it, which so has a use (any usage of the
    witness gives it any usage). So the traversal and any closed solution,
    the least among them, give the result of code that a call over-applies
    a use: no fault of the compiler, and no counterexample.
  - Arity alone does not say which code a call may over-apply: a call with
    `n` groups of a partial application of `A`, `k` groups given,
    over-applies `A` when `k + n` exceeds the parameters of `A`, also when
    `n` does not (`g = A a` then `g b c`, `A` of two parameters). What the
    model asks is read off the flow instead.
  - Where the use is read. The simulation follows the run of the first
    rebuilt unit, analysed again (`F1`, `T1`), and the decisions of the
    rebuild with unboxing are those of the tables `T` of the unit. The
    check: `over_reach F1 T1 id`, the code `id` has a witness (of known
    arity or not) whose first result has a use in `T1`; `ind_call_ok_o2`
    (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver2Spec.v) is `ind_call_ok_f2`, but a
    call that may over-apply asks `ind_code_ok_o1` with the use of a single
    result only of the code of `ovr` (the flag `never_over ap || negb (ovr
    id)`). `calls_unboxing_reach_over_nr` takes `reach` and `ovr`;
    `calls_unboxing_over2_nr` takes every code reached and `over_reach F1
    T1`. Every code still keeps its calling convention where a call may
    over-apply.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_over2_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_unbox_over2_refines_observably`,
    `rebuild_unbox_over2_refines_observably_local` (the premises of the
    `_flow2` statements), `calls_unboxing_over1_in_over2` (whatever `F1`,
    `T1`).
  - The run (FlambdaReaperPartialGroupsProofs.v, FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v).
    `shape_over_ok`, `cont_over_ok`, `step_over_ok OV s`: the step of `s`,
    a call or an `Apply_cont`, over-applies only code of `OV`, and gives a
    partial application that it makes only to a continuation that does too
    (what is left of an over-application is the call of the value it is
    given). `pending` has one more conjunct: each witness of the call but
    the last is read on, by its first result, into a node with a use
    (`indirect_pending`, from the facts of the call; kept by what is left of
    an over-application). `first_result_used`: a witness read on past gives
    the first result of each witness it stands for a use, by the rules.
    `closure_over_ok`, `tupled_over_ok`, `call_over_ok_of`, `known_over_ok`
    and `cont_over_ok_of` follow `closure_call_ok`, `tupled_call_ok`,
    `call_ok` and `apply_ok`; `step_over_used`: where the invariant of the
    run holds, a step over-applies only code that is `over_used` (the last
    witness of the code over-applied, read on past). No statement of
    FlambdaReaperFlowSpec.v changed.
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v). A new section
    variable `OV` (after `RCH`). `ind_targets_o cs`: every code fits `cs` as
    `ind_compat_w` says, and that of `OV` has its single result of kind
    `Value` plain; a fourth shape of `call_target`. `cont_rel_over` now
    holds `ind_targets_o` (implied by `ind_targets`, `ind_targets_o_of`).
    `closure_call_answered_r` asks, of an over-application, the plain result
    of the code called and `ind_targets_o`, and of a partial application
    only what the continuation does with it; `ind_call_answered_o`;
    `cont_answered` takes `cont_over_ok` of the first run's continuation;
    `call_answered`, `main_answered`, `pending_answered`,
    `related2_answered` take `step_over_ok`. `over_run s`: `step_over_ok`
    of every state `s` comes to. `uc_refines_flow` takes `over_run` of the
    first unit; `uc_refines` asks `OV` everywhere.
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). The section
    `RebuildsCalls` is made on `calls_expr_o2` with `ovr` (`OVf`);
    `ind_shape_targets_o2` gives `ind_targets_o` for a call that may
    over-apply. Section `InOver2` (`calls_in_over2`,
    `calls_unboxing_in_over2_reach`): the `_f2` fragment is in the `_o2`
    one, whatever `ovr`. `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over2` is new,
    with `flow_run` and `over_run` of the first rebuilt unit;
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_reach2` keeps its type and follows with
    `ovr` true everywhere.
  - The flow (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesOver2Proofs.v). `run_over_used`:
    `step_over_used` of every state of the run of the first rebuilt unit;
    `over_used_reach`: `over_reach` reads `over_used` off the facts and the
    tables.
  - `two_groups_example`: code 50 makes a closure of 51 and returns it; the
    unit calls a closure of 50 with two groups (`f 5 7`, of unknown arity),
    which over-applies 50 and calls 51 with 7, returning to 21, which calls
    52 directly and returns to 20; the parameters of 20 and 21 have no use,
    so neither have the results of 51 and 52. With the least solutions,
    `calls_unboxing_over1` and `calls_unboxing_flow2` refuse it (the call
    asks the used results of 51 and 52), `over_reach` holds of 50 alone,
    `calls_unboxing_over2` accepts it, the rebuilt unit (51 returns poison,
    20 and 21 lose their parameter, both calls return to a wrapper that
    drops the result) is the one written out, and it refines the unit up to
    observation.
  - Left: the flow form for two groups or more. Where a call may
    over-apply, every code must still keep its calling convention and fit
    the shape of the call: what is left of the over-application calls the
    value that the code returns, and `cont_rel_over` asks `ind_targets_o`
    of every code. Narrowing it needs the reach of that value: the start of
    the pending call of `Over_ok` is the first result of the last witness
    of the code over-applied, at which the code of the closure called, or
    of that of a partial application, is read as `flow_reach_u` reads it at
    a variable. `make all` (with `claims`) and `make assumptions` pass; the
    statements of Flambda 2 depend on no axiom.
- The flow for calls that may over-apply, and what is left of an
  over-application (`cont_rel_over`)
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach3{Spec,Proofs}.v, new). A call of
  unknown arity with two groups of arguments or more asked every code to
  keep its calling convention and to fit its shape, as what is left of an
  over-application may call any code.
  - What is left of an over-application. Where a call over-applies code
    `A`, the pending call that remains starts at the first result of the
    last witness of unknown arity of `A`, which has a use (entry above);
    what `A` returns may come there. The code that it calls, of a closure
    or of a partial application, is so the code that the flow brings to
    the first result, with a use, of a witness of code. The check:
    `node_reach F1 T1 n` is `flow_reach_u` at the node `n`; `rem_reach F1
    T1 id`, `id` is in `node_reach` of the first result, with a use, of a
    witness that names code. `ind_call_ok_f3` is `ind_call_ok_o2`, but a
    call of `f` that may over-apply asks `ind_code_ok_o1` only of the code
    of `reach false f` and of `rem`. `calls_unboxing_reach_rem_nr` takes
    `reach`, `ovr` and `rem`; `calls_unboxing_flow3_nr` takes
    `flow_reach2 F1 T1`, `over_reach F1 T1` and `rem_reach F1 T1`.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_flow3_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_unbox_flow3_refines_observably`,
    `rebuild_unbox_flow3_refines_observably_local` (the premises of the
    `_flow2` statements), `calls_unboxing_flow2_in_flow3`,
    `calls_unboxing_over2_in_flow3` (for the same analysis of the first
    rebuilt unit).
  - The run (FlambdaReaperPartialGroupsProofs.v, FlambdaReaperFlowProofs.v).
    `cont_rem_ok`, `step_rem_ok REM s`: what is left of an over-application
    that the step of `s` applies calls only a closure of code of `REM` (of
    the value, or of the partial application it is), and gives a partial
    application that it makes only to a continuation that does too.
    `Over_ok` takes `first_result_of n0`: the start of what is left is the
    first result, with a use, of a witness of code (`closure_call_ok`,
    `tupled_call_ok`, by `first_result_used`). `rem_used`;
    `chain_reach_w` (`chain_reach` of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2Proofs.v
    within the run); `closure_called_rem`, `cont_rem_ok_of`,
    `step_rem_used`: where the invariant holds, what is left of an
    over-application calls only code that is `rem_used`.
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v). A section
    variable `REM` (after `OV`). `ind_targets_r cs`: the code of `REM` fits
    `cs` as `ind_compat_w` says, and that of `OV` with a plain result;
    `cont_rel_over` now holds it (`ind_targets_o` implies it).
    `ind_targets_fo callee cs`: the same of the code of `RCH false f` and
    of `REM`, `callee` being `f`; a fifth shape of `call_target`.
    `shape_of_call_called`: a call is that of its `called_closure`;
    `ind_call_answered_c` answers a call made at a shape that the code of a
    predicate `P` fits, the closure called being of code of `P`.
    `cont_answered` takes `cont_rem_ok`; `call_answered`, `main_answered`,
    `pending_answered`, `related2_answered` take `step_rem_ok`; `rem_run`;
    `uc_refines_flow` takes `rem_run`; `uc_refines` asks `REM` everywhere.
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). `RebuildsCalls`
    is made on `calls_expr_f3` with `rem` (`REMf`); `ind_shape_targets_o3`
    gives `ind_targets_fo` for a call that may over-apply. Section
    `InOver3` (`calls_in_over3`, `calls_unboxing_in_over3_reach`): the `_o2`
    fragment is in the `_f3` one, whatever `rem`, for a narrower reach.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over3` is new, with `rem_run` of the
    first rebuilt unit; `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over2` keeps its
    type and follows with `rem` true everywhere.
  - The flow (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach3Proofs.v). `run_rem_used`;
    `rem_used_reach`: `rem_reach` reads `rem_used` off the facts and the
    tables; `step_rem_ok_mono`.
  - `flow3_example`: `TwoGroupsExample` (entry above) with the least table
    of `cannot_change_calling_convention`, which has 50 and 51 and not 52:
    code 52, called directly, changes its calling convention.
    `calls_unboxing_over2` refuses it (the call with two groups asks every
    code to keep its calling convention), `rem_reach` holds of 51 alone (the
    closure that 50 returns), `calls_unboxing_flow3` accepts it with the
    least solution of the first rebuilt unit, and the rebuilt unit refines
    the unit up to observation.
  - Left: nothing of over-applications of unknown arity. The fragment still
    asks, of a call that may over-apply, that the code which the flow
    brings to its callee keep its calling convention, as the compiler does
    (`unchanged_of_unknown`). `rem_reach` is read over every witness of code
    whose first result has a use, not only those that a call over-applies:
    coarser than the flow of each call, and sound. `make all` (with
    `claims`; 493 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 11 new) and `make
    assumptions` pass; the statements of Flambda 2 depend on no axiom.
- Calls whose code is not known that never return, in the refinement of
  the rebuild with unboxing (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesNever{Spec,Proofs}.v,
  new). Such a call, of known arity or of unknown arity, that
  `apply_never_returns` makes one that never returns, and which the fix
  makes return to a handler `Invalid`, is now in the fragment: every
  fragment since `calls_unboxing` asked `apply_never_returns = false` of it
  (`ind_call_ok`).
  - The check (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesNeverSpec.v, to review).
    `ind_never_ok`: the call is not direct; its callee `f` is used and not
    unboxed; the arguments are given at the mask of the call and the
    exception continuation takes the exception of `ind_exn_decision`, as
    for a call that may return; and every code of the unit that the flow
    of the first rebuilt unit brings to `f` (`reach b f`, `b` whether the
    call is of known arity) is code of `nr` that keeps its calling
    convention and, for a call of unknown arity, has no more parameters
    than the call has groups of arguments (`never_code_ok`). Its
    continuation is not checked. `ind_call_ok_n` is `ind_never_ok` for a
    call that `apply_never_returns`, `ind_call_ok_f3` otherwise; `call_ok_n`,
    `calls_expr_n` (and its mutual functions), `calls_unboxing_reach_never_nr`
    and `calls_unboxing_never_nr` follow the `_f3` functions.
  - Why the code entered. The two frames are not related: the second run's
    is the handler `Invalid`. The first run must then not return to its own,
    which it does not where the code that the call enters is related with
    its return continuation out of the relation, as code of `nr` is
    (`code_K_never`). That code is read off the flow of the first run
    (`flow_ok`, `RCH`), as for the calls whose code is not known since
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReachSpec.v. A call of unknown arity that
    made a partial application would return it at once to the first run's
    frame; the check on the parameters excludes it (a partial application
    as callee holds groups already, so it only adds to them). An
    over-application enters code of `nr` first, which never returns to what
    is left of it.
  - New statements, all proved: `rebuild_unbox_nr_ind_refines_cc`,
    `rebuild_unbox_nr_ind_refines_observably`,
    `rebuild_unbox_nr_ind_refines_observably_local` (the premises of the
    `_flow3` statements, with `calls_unboxing_never_nr` in place of
    `calls_unboxing_flow3_nr`), `calls_unboxing_flow3_in_never` (the
    `_flow3` fragment is in the new one, for the same analysis of the first
    rebuilt unit), and `never_ind_example`. No statement changed.
  - Simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v). New
    `never_targets` and `never_rel` (`call_rel` with `never_targets` in place
    of `call_target`), and one new case of `uc`, `uc_call_ind_never`: the
    first run's `Apply ap`, whatever its continuation, against
    `Let_cont_non_recursive w (Handler wps wmd Invalid) (Apply ap')`, `ap'`
    returning to `w`. The first run's wrapper needs no case: it is
    `uc_let_cont_never` over `uc_call_ind_never`. `main_answered` answers the
    case by the `Let_cont` step and the call of the second run
    (`never_call_answered`): the closure called, or that of a partial
    application, has its code in `RCH` (`flow_ok`), so in `NR`; the call is a
    full call or an over-application of that code
    (`never_closure_answered`; `shape_partial_fewer`, and `known_arity_full`
    for a call of known arity); the body is entered in related states at
    `code_K_never`, whatever the frames (`call_code_never`, which is
