    `call_code_same` without the frame). `related_call` and `related2` are
    unchanged; no new premise on the runs.
  - Construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsProofs.v). `uc_agree` and
    `uc_never_ext` handle the new case. The section `RebuildsCalls` is made
    on `calls_expr_n`: `call_rebuilt_ind` sends a call that
    `apply_never_returns` to `call_rebuilt_never` (with `rewrite_kind_never`,
    `never_code_shape`, `uc_result_ind_never`), and `call_rebuilt` reads
    `call_ok_f3` off `call_ok_n` for a call of known code, so that
    `call_rebuilt_nc` and the rest are unchanged. Section `InNever`
    (`call_in_never`, `calls_in_never`) and `calls_unboxing_in_never_reach`.
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_never` is new (the former proof of
    `_over3`); `rebuild_unbox_modes_refines_cc_over3` keeps its type and
    follows from it.
  - `never_ind_example`: the codes 50 and 51 raise their parameter, so that
    no result of theirs has a source; the unit calls the closure of 50 or of
    51 (chosen by a `Switch`) at known arity, returning to the handler 20,
    or at unknown arity, returning to 24. `apply_never_returns` holds of
    both calls. With the least solutions and `nr := used_never_returns`
    (50 and 51), `calls_unboxing_flow3_nr` refuses the unit and
    `calls_unboxing_never_nr` accepts it. The change of calling conventions
    alone keeps the handlers 20 and 24 (`Invalid`, their parameters having
    no source) and the calls returning to them; the rebuild in the mode of
    the fix makes both calls return to the handler `Invalid` 62 and drops
    20 and 24. The rebuilt unit refines the unit up to observation (both
    raise 5 to the unit's exception continuation).
  - Departure from the plan of the earlier entries: no run premise carried
    from the run of the unit to that of the first rebuilt unit, and no
    change to `related_call`. `entered_used_never_returns` and
    `indirect_dead_frame_traversal` are not used: they speak of the run of
    the unit and its analysis, the relation of the runs of the two rebuilt
    units, and the code taken to never return (`NR`) is that of the analysis
    of the unit (its bodies are related at `code_K_never` by the check of
    `live_conts`, read off that analysis). The link is a check, in the form
    of the reach checks since FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReachSpec.v: the
    code that the flow of the first rebuilt unit brings to the callee is of
    `nr`. It is the "cheaper route" of the earlier entry with the reach of
    the callee in place of the arities of the call. With `nr :=
    used_never_returns` it should hold of the compiler's units: where
    `apply_never_returns` holds of a call of the unit with a least solution,
    each code that the flow of the unit brings to the callee has a result
    of the rank found with no source and is used (as
    `entered_used_never_returns` proves of the code entered at a call of
    known arity), and the flow of the first rebuilt unit brings no more
    code there (not proved).
- Nothing found false; no counterexample.
- Left:
  - The check of `never_code_ok` derived rather than checked: that the
    flow of the first rebuilt unit brings to a callee only code that that
    of the unit brings there (with the least solutions), and then
    `apply_never_returns` with `used_never_returns`.
  - Calls of unknown arity of a partial application of code with more
    parameters than the call has groups (`f x`, `f` a partial application
    of code of two parameters, which the call completes): refused by the
    check, which reads the code and not how many groups the partial
    application holds. Over-applications whose code returns and whose
    remainder never returns: refused (the code over-applied must be of
    `nr`).
  - The derived forms (`nr := used_never_returns` and the check of
    `live_conts` derived, FlambdaReaperNeverReturns{Spec,Proofs}.v) for the
    new fragment; and the closures fragment (`rr`, `repr_call`).
- `make all` (with `claims`; 507 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 5 new) and
  `make assumptions` pass; the statements of Flambda 2 depend on no axiom.
- The checks of the reach fragments read off the unit's own analysis
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesUnitFlow{Spec,Proofs}.v, new). The
  fragments of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach{,2,3}Spec.v and
  FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesNeverSpec.v read the flow off the first
  rebuilt unit, analysed again (`F1`, `T1`). The aim was to read it off the
  unit's own analysis (`F`, `T`). The open question was whether the first
  rebuilt unit, analysed again, brings to each callee no more code than
  the unit's own analysis does. It is not proved; the reach of `F1`, `T1`
  is checked instead, at one place, against that of `F`, `T`.
  - Found false: the inclusion at every variable
    (`poison_reach_statement`). A unit calls the closure of 50 or of 51
    (chosen by a `Switch`) at known arity with the closure `x61` of 51 as
    argument; neither code uses its parameter. The first rebuild gives the
    call poison. In the analysis of the first rebuilt unit a constant has
    any source (`Node_all_constants`), so the parameter `x40` of 50 may
    hold the closure of any code there, where the unit's own analysis
    brings it the closure of 51 only. `x40` has no use. At the callee `x31`,
    which has one, the two agree. Not a fault: no check reads the reach at
    a variable with no use. The inclusion is to be stated at the variables
    with a use only.
  - The check that links the two analyses, `used_reach_covered F T F1 T1
    e`: at every variable of `e` with a use in `T` and every code id of
    `e`, `flow_reach2` of `F1`, `T1` is within that of `F`, `T` (both
    values of the flag of known arity); and so are `over_reach` and
    `rem_reach` at every code id of `e`.
  - The fragments, to review: `calls_unboxing_unit_flow3_nr` and
    `calls_unboxing_unit_never_nr` are `calls_unboxing_reach_rem_nr` and
    `calls_unboxing_reach_never_nr` with `flow_reach2 F T`,
    `over_reach F T` and `rem_reach F T`.
  - New statements, all proved:
    - `rebuild_unbox_unit_flow3_refines_cc`, `_observably`,
      `_observably_local`: the statements of the `_flow3` fragment, with
      `calls_unboxing_unit_flow3_nr` and `used_reach_covered` in place of
      `calls_unboxing_flow3_nr`.
    - `rebuild_unbox_unit_never_refines_cc`, `_observably`,
      `_observably_local`: the same for the `_nr_ind` statements. The code
      taken to never return is fixed to `used_never_returns` of the unit's
      own analysis, as in the derived statements of
      FlambdaReaperNeverReturnsSpec.v. The check of `live_conts` stays in
      the fragment.
    - `unit_flow_examples`: the units of `flow_example`, `flow2_example`,
      `flow3_example` and `never_ind_example` are of the new fragments
      (the last with `used_never_returns`), and `used_reach_covered`
      holds of each, with the least solutions.
    - `unit_flow3_example`, `unit_never_example`: the rebuilt units of
      `flow3_example` and `never_ind_example` refine their units up to
      observation, by the new statements.
    - `poison_reach`: the counterexample above.
  - The proof (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesUnitFlowProofs.v). The checks
    are monotone in what they read. Each code that the reach gives a
    callee, or that `ovr` or `rem` gives, is asked something, and the rest
    nothing; so a check with larger `reach`, `ovr` and `rem` implies the
    check with smaller ones. This needs the comparison only at the
    callees of calls whose code is not known (used, by `ind_call_ok_f3` and
    `ind_never_ok`; variables of the term) and at the code of the unit
    (section `Mono`: `ind_call_mono_f3`, `ind_never_mono`, `call_mono_f3`,
    `call_mono_n`, `calls_mono_f3`, `calls_mono_n`).
    `ind_code_ok_o1_or` handles a smaller `ovr`. `covered_parts` reads
    the comparison off `used_reach_covered`. With it,
    `unit_flow3_covered` and `unit_never_covered` put the new fragments
    inside the old ones, whose statements give the new ones.
  - Evidence for the inclusion at used variables, outside the statements
    (`vm_compute` in a scratch file). On 32 units of the examples of the
    reaper files (`FlambdaReaperUnboxing.Examples`, the Calls, Refines,
    Closures, Wrapper, DeadFrames, Heap and Over examples), with the least
    solutions, `used_reach_covered` holds of every one. Even the inclusion
    at every variable holds of every one; only the unit of
    `poison_reach_statement` separates the two. Where the two analyses
    differ, the unit's own brings more code: parameters that the first
    rebuild deletes, handlers that it drops, and code over-applied in
    `never_ind_example`.
  - What a proof of the inclusion needs, not done:
    - A relation between the facts of the traversal of the first rebuilt
      unit and those of the unit, up to the names that the rebuild makes:
      wrappers and their parameters; parameters and results renumbered by
      the change of calling conventions; calls made direct, or of known
      arity with a list of code ids; poison at nodes with no use; value
      slots, code (made `Bind_deleted_code`, whose closures get a witness
      with any source in `add_closure_dep`) and handlers dropped.
    - Monotonicity of least solutions under that relation, at nodes with
      a use. The rules are not monotone at the level of the tables: the
      sources, usages and some alias rules have negative guards
      (`s_any_source S base = false`, `s_any_usage S base = false`), so
      only "any source, or these sources" is monotone, and that has to be
      carried through every rule.
    - A run-based route instead would carry the flow of the run of the
      unit to that of the first rebuilt unit through the simulation of
      FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v. It gives nothing past a point where
      the run of the unit is undefined and the first rebuilt unit's is not
      (a dropped `Let`), so it would serve the forms up to observation
      only, by a simulation of three runs.
  - Left of the premises on the first rebuilt unit: `in_heap_fragment` of
    it, `check_closed` and `check_local_reads_used` of `T1`, and
    `used_reach_covered`. `in_heap_fragment (first_rebuild ..)` should
    follow from `in_flow_fragment` of the unit; that is not proved
    (`conts_counted` of code whose results the change of calling
    conventions deletes, and of the wrappers).
- No existing file changed but the Makefile, which lists the two new files.
- `make -k all` (with `claims`; 520 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 10 new)
  and `make assumptions` pass; the statements of Flambda 2 depend on no
  axiom.
- The test tool with types. Each of its three modes compares the
  compiler's output, with types, with the model's rebuild with types:
  `rebuild_unit_with_types_from_tables`,
  `rebuild_unit_cc_with_types_from_tables` and
  `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_with_types` (FlambdaReaperTypes.v), with no
  set of closures in the context. On the 824 units of the corpus of
  `tests/semantics`, the terms that are the same up to bound names went
  from 276, 277 and 277 (plain, with changes of calling convention,
  with unboxing) to 356, 358 and 775; the same up to bound names and
  subkinds only, from 499, 501 and 499 to 419, 420 and 1. The terms
  that differ are the 9, 6 and 6 of before. The one unit of the
  unboxing that differs in subkinds
  (`ocaml/iarray_literal_measured_one_call_inlined.fl`) has a
  parameter of subkind `Immediate_array`, which the conversion makes
  `Anything` that is not null and the model's rule then erases; the
  model has no subkind of arrays. The plain and calling-convention
  rebuilds with types rewrite code at its parameter variables and state
  nothing of results or of wrappers, where the compiler (and the
  unboxing rebuild of the model) rewrites at the nodes of the code's
  dependencies (`cd_params`, `cd_return`), results included: the 419
  and 420. One unit's model rebuild is now undefined where it returned
  (`subkind_return_not_immediate.fl`): the input states `imm tagged` of
  a handler parameter given a block, and is undefined itself; the
  rebuild with types keeps that subkind, as the compiler's output does.
  Nine examples of `tests/semantics/reaper/` are now the same up to
  bound names.
- Rebased on the core with the code age relation and its second machine
  (C3: `FlambdaCodeAgeRelation*.v`, `FlambdaCodeAgeCalls*.v`,
  `step_aged`). No conflict, and nothing of the reaper changed: the
  machine of the tree, `step`, is unchanged, and the reaper's statements
  are of it. `make -k all` (520 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`) and
  `make assumptions` pass.
- The reaper and the code age relation, on the second machine of the
  core (`FlambdaCodeAgeCallsSpec.step_aged`), where a direct call may
  have for callee a closure of a newer version of the code that it
  names and goes by the code named: `FlambdaReaperCodeAgeSpec.v` and
  `FlambdaReaperCodeAgeProofs.v`, new, 22 statements. The relation is
  the parameter `age_of` of the core, one and the same for a unit and
  for its rebuild: the terms of the model do not carry
  `newer_version_of`, and the compiler's reaper copies the relation
  unchanged into the typing environment that it exports
  (`types/traversals.ml`) while it changes the calling convention of
  each code on its own (`unboxing_analysis.ml`). `unit_statics` is the
  static environment that the first step of a unit binds, what a client
  of the unit runs with (`unit_statics_step_statement`,
  `unit_statics_codes_statement`); `sound_conventions_for` is the part
  of `sound_for` that is of the codes alone, the same arities and
  `is_tupled` along the links above the code of a closure
  (`sound_for_conventions_statement`), and `conventions_agree` the same
  of every link between codes that a unit binds
  (`conventions_agree_statics_statement`).
  - Bug 12 as a counterexample (`conventions_counterexample_statement`,
    `conventions_client_statement`): the reproducer of the report
    (`relation_mixed_conventions.ml`) in the model. `f51` is a newer
    version of `f50`, the two are one code of two parameters; the
    closure of `f50` escapes, the static closure `s80` of `f51` is
    called directly only. The unit meets the premises of
    `least_rebuild_cc_auto_refines_observably_statement`, and the
    rebuild refines it, on `step` and on the machine. In the least
    table is `f50` and not `f51`, which loses its unused parameter. The
    relation is sound for `s80` in the unit (`sound_for`, with the
    refinement of the codes proved); in the rebuild, with the same
    relation, the conventions of `f50` and `f51` differ, and it is not.
    A client that holds `s80` and calls it directly naming `f50`, with
    two arguments, exits with 5 under the code of the unit and under
    that of the rebuild (on `step` that call is undefined); the same
    call naming `f51`, as `direct_call_newer_version_refines_statement`
    allows and as Simplify makes it where the type of the callee names
    `f51`, exits with 5 under the code of the unit and is undefined
    under that of the rebuild: it does not refine the call naming
    `f50`.
  - A direct call of a closure of a newer version within the unit
    (`aged_call_counterexample_statement`,
    `aged_call_sound_example_statement`). With `Auto` the traversal
    reads the witness of a direct call off its callee and links the call
    to the code that it names only where the callee has any source: the
    graph takes the call for a call of the code of the closure called.
    The rebuild names the code that the flow finds (`rewrite_call_kind`).
    So where a direct call names `f50` and its callee is a closure of
    `f51`, the unit runs `f50` with that closure and the rebuilt unit
    runs `f51`, the replacement of
    `direct_call_newer_version_refines_statement`, made whatever the
    relation. With `f50` returning 0 and `f51` returning 1 (the relation
    not sound), the unit meets the premises of
    `least_rebuild_refines_observably_statement`, goes wrong on `step`
    (so the statement holds of it and says nothing), exits with 0 on the
    machine, and its rebuilds, plain and with changes of calling
    convention (with the table of the fix too), exit with 1: the
    refinement of the rebuild does not hold on the machine of every
    relation. With both returning their argument, the relation is sound
    and both rebuilds refine the unit on the machine; but the solution
    gives `f50` no use and its closure no source, while the run of the
    machine enters the body of `f50` with its closure bound to `s80`:
    the invariant of the flow (`FlambdaReaperFlowSpec.v`), which is of
    the runs of `step`, does not hold of the runs of the machine, and
    the rebuild deletes `f50`.
  - What holds of every unit on the machine: a refinement of `step`
    whose source does not go wrong on `step` holds on the machine,
    whatever the relation (`refines_aged_of_defined_statement`,
    `refines_observably_aged_of_defined_statement`, by `step_aged`
    extending `step`): such a unit makes no direct call of a closure of
    another code, and its runs and those of its target are those of
    `step`. So `least_rebuild_refines_aged_statement` and
    `least_rebuild_cc_auto_refines_aged_statement`.
  - The two fixes of the report. `age_fixed_table` adds every code of
    the relation to the table of `cannot_change_calling_convention`; it
    passes `check_cc_closed` where the table does
    (`age_fixed_table_check_statement`), so the refinements hold with
    it (`rebuild_cc_fixed_refines_observably_statement`,
    `least_rebuild_cc_fixed_refines_observably_statement`, and on the
    machine for a unit that does not go wrong on `step`,
    `least_rebuild_cc_fixed_refines_aged_statement`). Where every code
    that a link relates is in the table
    (`age_fixed_table_links_statement`), the rebuild with changes of
    calling convention, with or without types, keeps the conventions of
    the linked codes that it binds
    (`rebuild_cc_keeps_conventions_statement`: each code of the rebuilt
    unit is the rebuild of a code of the unit by the same code id, and
    code of the table keeps its arities and `is_tupled`); so the statics
    of the rebuilt unit meet `sound_conventions_for` for every closure
    where the conventions of the unit agree
    (`rebuild_cc_fixed_sound_conventions_statement`). `cut_relation`
    keeps the links between codes of the table, and the conventions
    agree for it in the rebuild whatever the table
    (`rebuild_cc_cut_keeps_conventions_statement`). Both repair the unit
    of the bug (`conventions_fixed_statement`: with the table of the fix
    no code changes, the relation is sound for `s80` in the rebuild and
    the client's call naming `f51` refines the call naming `f50`; the
    cut relation is empty).
  - What the fixes do not keep (`sound_for_not_kept_statement`): the
    refinement half of `sound_for`. A unit in which no code changes its
    calling convention (both codes in the least table, which is then
    the table of the fix) and the conventions agree, before and after:
    `f50` reads into a variable of no use a value slot that `s80` has
    not, so `f50` with `s80` for its closure is undefined and `f51`,
    which returns 1, refines it; the relation is sound for `s80`. The
    rebuild removes the read, and `f50` then returns 0 with `s80`, which
    `f51` does not refine. The rebuild refines each code for the runs of
    the unit, not for every call of `sound_for`.
  - Not reached: a statement of the rebuild on the machine for a unit
    that makes a direct call of a closure of a newer version of the code
    named, under a relation sound for each closure so called. It would
    need the invariant of the flow for the run in which each such call
    goes by the code of its closure, and that this run refines the run
    of the machine, from the soundness at each call; the refinements
    compose by behaviours and not by steps, so the second is not a
    corollary of `direct_call_newer_version_refines_statement`. The
    changes of representation of closures (`FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v`)
    on the machine are not looked at.
- No existing file changed but the Makefile, which lists the two new
  files.
- `make -k all` (with `claims`; 542 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 22
  new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The plain rebuild with types rewrites code where the compiler does.
  `compute_code_changes` (unboxing_analysis.ml) rewrites the parameters
  and the results of code that keeps its calling convention at the
  nodes of its code dep (`code_dep.params`, `code_dep.return`), which
  the traversal aliases the parameter variables to; the plain rebuild
  and the rebuild with changes of calling convention rewrote at the
  variables, and stated nothing of the results.
  - The functions (FlambdaReaperRebuild.v). `subkinds_rewriting` is now
    `option subkinds_types`, a record of the rewriting (`st_rewrite`, of
    type `subkinds_rewrite`, a function of a node and of what is
    stated), the code deps of the traversal (`st_code_deps`) and the
    parameters of the continuations of the unit (`st_conts`, for the
    wrappers; not read yet). `kept_code_metadata` takes the code id: with
    `Some`, each parameter and each result states what is stated of it
    rewritten at its node in the code dep (`rewritten_at`, with
    `with_code_subkinds` and `nodes_with_subkinds`, moved from
    FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v), as the unboxing does; code with no code
    dep states nothing. `rebuild_code` takes the code id. Handlers are
    rewritten at their variables as before (`should_keep_param`). The
    rebuild with changes of calling convention shares
    `kept_code_metadata` in its not-changing branch; code that changes
    is rewritten at its variables as before
    (`changed_code_metadata_with`). With `None` every function is as
    before. The unboxing takes `option subkinds_rewrite`, as before.
  - The instance (FlambdaReaperTypes.v). `rewrite_of_context` is
    `rewrite_kind_with_subkind`; `subkinds_of_context ctx code_deps e`
    gives it with the code deps and `conts_params code_deps e`.
    `rebuild_unit_with_types` and `rebuild_unit_with_types_from_tables`
    take the code deps after the solution (after the sets of closures),
    `rebuild_unit_cc_with_types` passes its own to the rewriting.
  - The proofs (FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v). `types_sound` is stated of
    any node: what is stated, rewritten at `n`, holds of a value of the
    second run related at `n` to one of the first, where `n` has a use.
    Entering code that keeps its calling convention, the arguments are
    related at the parameters of the call witness, which are the
    `cd_params` of the callee (`known_witness_edges`, and `code_chain`
    for the calls of unknown arity and the tupled ones), before they are
    aliased to the parameter variables: `rebuilt_code_subkinds_sound`
    and `rebuilt_subkinds_of_bind` take that relation (`Hparams_q` in
    `closure_called` and `tupled_called`, the relation of the direct
    call in `call_rebuilt_code`). The section `Run` takes that the code
    deps of the rewriting are those of the traversal (`Htypes_deps`),
    and the four refinements with types
    (`rebuild_with_refines_checked_local`,
    `rebuild_cc_with_refines_checked_local`,
    `rebuild_with_refines_local`,
    `rebuild_with_refines_up_to_heap_local`) take it of
    `unit_code_deps flag u e` (`no_types_deps` without types).
    FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v: `subkinds_of_context_sound` is stated of
    any node (its proof did not read that the node is a variable), and
    `subkinds_of_context_sound_all` of any code deps and term;
    `subkinds_of_context_deps`. What code states of its results is not
    tested by the machine.
  - Statements. `rebuild_with_types_refines_local_statement` and
    `rebuild_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement` give the
    rebuild the code deps of the traversal
    (`FlambdaReaperCallingConventions.unit_code_deps flag u e`), as the
    statement with changes of calling convention does; their premises
    are unchanged. `rebuild_cc_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement`
    is unchanged. No statement without types changed. Proved.
  - Extracted signatures changed (tests/semantics/extracted,
    regenerated): FlambdaReaperRebuild: `subkinds_rewriting` (now
    `subkinds_types option`), new `subkinds_rewrite`, `subkinds_types`,
    `nodes_with_subkinds`, `with_code_subkinds`, `rewritten_at`;
    `kept_code_metadata` takes a code id in place of the parameters.
    FlambdaReaperUnboxing: `nodes_with_subkinds` and `with_code_subkinds`
    moved out; the rebuilds take `subkinds_rewrite option`.
    FlambdaReaperTypes: new `rewrite_of_context`; `subkinds_of_context`
    takes the code deps and the term; `rebuild_unit_with_types` and
    `rebuild_unit_with_types_from_tables` take the code deps before
    `preserve_direct_calls`. The rebuilds of FlambdaReaperRebuild and
    FlambdaReaperCallingConventions keep their signatures.
  - The test tool passes the code deps in its plain mode
    (`Cc.unit_code_deps`, as the calling-convention mode does). On the
    824 units of the corpus, the plain rebuild and the compiler's output
    are now the same up to bound names in 770 (356 before), the same up
    to subkinds only in 5 (419 before), different in 9 (unchanged, the
    known ones). Of the 5, four (the `cc_*` tests of the reaper) differ
    at the wrapper of a call, which states nothing, and one
    (`iarray_literal_measured_one_call_inlined`) is the immediate array
    that the unboxing mode also keeps. With changes of calling
    convention: 567 the same (358 before), 211 up to subkinds (420), 6
    different: code that changes its convention is still rewritten at
    its variables. The unboxing mode is unchanged (775, 1, 6). Three
    tests of the reaper's semantics tests (`escaping_parameter_argument`,
    `mixed_mutable_field`, `unbox_kept_result_gadt`) are now the same in
    the plain mode (promoted).
  - Left: the wrappers. The compiler gives a wrapper of a call of code
    that keeps its calling convention what the continuation's kept
    parameters state, rewritten (`make_apply_wrapper`, the return arity
    of `rebuild_apply` from `cont_params_to_keep`); the plain rebuild's
    wrappers still state nothing. The table is in the record
    (`st_conts`); what the proof needs is that the entry of a
    continuation in the table is the handler that the first run binds
    to it, which a global table keyed by continuations gives only if
    each is bound once (`well_formed_unit` allows a name to be bound
    again), and an invariant of the simulation that ties the table to
    the handlers in scope (`kinds_agree`, established at `Let_cont`
    from a check of the term).
- The rebuilds with types rewrite what the compiler rewrites at the code
  that changes its calling convention and at the wrappers of calls.
  - Code that changes its calling convention
    (FlambdaReaperCallingConventions.v). `changed_code_metadata_with`
    takes the code id in place of the parameters: with `Some`, each
    parameter and each result that the code keeps states what is stated
    of it, rewritten at its node in the code dep (`cd_params`,
    `cd_return`), as `compute_code_changes` rewrites the kind of a
    `Keep` at `code_dep.params` and `code_dep.return`; code with no code
    dep states nothing. It rewrote the parameters at their variables and
    stated nothing of the results. The proof
    (FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v): `rebuilt_code_subkinds_sound_cc`
    takes the code dep and the relation of the arguments at its
    parameters, which a direct call of code that changes gives as one of
    code that keeps its convention does (`known_mask_used`,
    `rel_argument`); no new invariant.
  - The wrappers (FlambdaReaperRebuild.v). `make_apply_wrapper` gives the
    parameters of the wrapper of a call `wrapper_metadata`: with
    `Some t`, each result that the continuation of the call keeps states
    what the parameter of the continuation states, rewritten at its node
    (`wrapper_subkinds`, from the entry of the continuation in
    `st_conts t`, `cont_params_of`), and each other nothing
    (`erase_subkind`): the return arity of `rebuild_apply` from
    `cont_params_to_keep`. Without types, nothing, as before.
    `make_apply_wrapper` and `rebuild_apply` take `types`. The table is
    `handlers_params e`: each continuation that the unit binds, with the
    parameters of its handler at their variables and what they state
    (`handler_entry`), and the return and exception continuations of
    code with no parameter; its keys are
    FlambdaStaticsInScopeSpec.bound_continuations, in order
    (`handlers_params_keys`). FlambdaReaperTypes.subkinds_of_context
    gives it as `st_conts` (it gave FlambdaReaperUnboxing.conts_params,
    which the unboxing still reads).
  - What the model still states nothing of, where the compiler states
    what code states of its results: the wrapper of a call of code that
    changes its calling convention (`make_apply_wrapper_cc`; the
    compiler's are the results that the callee keeps, rewritten at
    `code_dep.return`: `get_parameters` of the `return_decisions`), and
    the wrapper of a call, in code that changes, to the return
    continuation of that code (the compiler's are its results in the
    code dep). The machine does not test what code states of its results
    (`call_code` tests the parameters, `apply_cont_value` those of a
    handler): a unit whose code states of a result what the result is
    not runs, and a wrapper stating it would stop the rebuilt unit there,
    so no refinement holds with these without a premise on the runs of
    the unit. Not stated as a theorem.
  - The proof (FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v). `fresh_kinded` and
    `handler_fresh_kinded` have a fourth conjunct, `conts_tabled`: with
    `Some t`, the table has, for each handler in scope but the return
    continuations `rets`, the parameters of the handler of the first run
    (`cont_entry`), no parameter for each of `rets`, and the entries of
    the expression; it is established at each `Let_cont` from the
    entries of the expression, and in the body of code from the section
    hypothesis `Hcode_tabled` (`code_tabled`: no parameter for its
    return continuation, and the entries of its body). `wrapper` takes
    the parameters of the continuation; `way_out` takes the wrapper over
    a handler with those of the handler (`cont_entry`), so that at the
    return the second run passes the test of the wrapper where the first
    passes that of the handler (`wrapper_subkinds_sound`,
    `apply_cont_closure_step`); the wrapper over the return continuation
    of code that changes states nothing (`wrapper_params_ret`,
    `wrapper_as_cc`). `code_entered`, `code_entered_cc`,
    `code_entered_ret`, `call_entered` and `call_entered_cc` take what
    they need of it. The four refinements with types
    (`rebuild_with_refines_checked_local`,
    `rebuild_cc_with_refines_checked_local`,
    `rebuild_with_refines_local`,
    `rebuild_with_refines_up_to_heap_local`) take `conts_table_ok types
    e`: the table is `handlers_params e`, and each continuation of the
    unit is bound once (`conts_table_top`, `conts_table_codes`,
    `unit_codes_handlers_params`); `no_types_conts` without types.
    FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v: `subkinds_of_context_conts`.
  - Statements. `rebuild_with_types_refines_local_statement`,
    `rebuild_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement` and
    `rebuild_cc_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement` take a new
    premise, `distinct (FlambdaStaticsInScopeSpec.bound_continuations e)
    = true`: each continuation of the unit is bound once. Why: the
    wrappers read a table keyed by the names of the continuations, as
    the compiler's `cont_params_to_keep` is (a map of the
    `continuation_info` of the traversal), whose terms bind fresh names;
    `well_formed_unit` asks only that the names of one binder be
    distinct (`binders_distinct`), and in a unit that binds a name again
    with a handler that states more of its parameter, the wrapper of a
    call to the other handler would state what that one states, which a
    value it is given need not be. The compiler's terms meet it. No other
    premise changed; no statement without types changed. Proved.
  - Extracted signatures changed (tests/semantics/extracted,
    regenerated): FlambdaReaperRebuild: new `handler_entry`,
    `handlers_params` (with `handler_handlers_params`,
    `binding_handlers_params`, `code_handlers_params`),
    `wrapper_subkinds`, `wrapper_metadata`, `cont_params_of`;
    `make_apply_wrapper` and `rebuild_apply` take the rewriting after
    the first fresh name. FlambdaReaperCallingConventions:
    `changed_code_metadata_with` takes the code id in place of the
    parameters. The rebuilds and FlambdaReaperTypes keep their
    signatures; the test tool reads none of these and is unchanged.
  - On the 824 units of the corpus (plain / with changes of calling
    convention / with unboxing), the rebuild and the compiler's output
    are the same up to bound names in 774 / 776 / 775 (770 / 567 / 775
    before), the same up to subkinds only in 1 / 2 / 1 (5 / 211 / 1),
    different in 9 / 6 / 6 (unchanged). The 1 of each mode is the
    immediate array of `iarray_literal_measured_one_call_inlined`; the
    other of the calling-convention mode is the reaper's test
    `cc_wrapper`, at the wrapper of a call of code that changes its
    calling convention, which states nothing (above). Six of the
    reaper's semantics tests are now the same (promoted): the four
    `cc_*`, `mixed_mutable_field`, `unbox_kept_result_gadt`.
  - `FlambdaReaperRebuild.with_code_params_subkinds` is no longer used
    (it served the parameters of code that changes).
  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 542 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, as
    before; no statement added) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The unboxing with types, made consistent with the plain and
  calling-convention rebuilds with types, and its refinements where the
  unboxing is local.
  - The functions (FlambdaReaperUnboxing.v). The rebuild with unboxing
    takes the `types` of the plain rebuild
    (FlambdaReaperRebuild.subkinds_rewriting: the rewriting, the code deps
    and the table of the continuations) in place of an
    `option subkinds_rewrite` and a table of its own (`cont_params`).
    Code that keeps its calling convention states what
    `kept_code_metadata types id` gives, the function of the plain
    rebuild; code that changes and handlers state what they stated, read
    off the record (`st_rewrite`, `st_code_deps`). The wrapper of a call of
    code that keeps its calling convention states what the continuation of
    the call keeps of its parameters (`cont_kept_subkinds`, as before),
    read off `cont_params_of types`: the table is now
    FlambdaReaperRebuild.handlers_params, where the return and exception
    continuations of code have no parameter, so the wrapper of a call to
    the return continuation of code states nothing (it stated the results
    of the code at its code dep, from `conts_params`). The wrapper of a
    call of code that changes its calling convention states nothing (it
    stated the results of the callee at its code dep,
    `callee_results_u`). Both are what the plain and calling-convention
    rebuilds do, by the finding of the round before: the machine does not
    test what code states of its results. `conts_params` and
    `callee_results_u` are removed. New `rebuild_unit_unbox_with`, the
    rebuild with no change of representation for any `types`. With `None`
    every function is as before: the proofs pass `None` where they passed
    `None []`, and so does one statement,
    FlambdaReaperWrapperDerivedSpec.check_never_returns_auto_statement,
    whose text names `rebuild_code_u` with its arguments (the same
    statement; the function takes one argument fewer).
  - FlambdaReaperTypes.v: `rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_mode_with_types` gives
    the rebuild `subkinds_of_context (plain_context is_local F T [])
    code_deps e`, the record of the plain rebuild with types (no set of
    closures); new `rebuild_unit_unbox_with_types`, with no change of
    representation.
  - The proofs. FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesProofs.v: `ub` and `cont_rel`
    asked that the handlers of the rebuild with unboxing state nothing;
    they now ask that what such a handler states hold of the values that
    it is given wherever what the handler of the plain rebuild states
    holds of the values of the plain run (`sks_follow`, over the values
    of the two runs as the shapes of the parameters relate them,
    `vals_follow`); `bind_args_follow` gives that relation of the values
    at an `Apply_cont` (`bind_args_rel` is its corollary). The section
    `Rebuilds` takes `types`, the same for the two rebuilds, and
    `rebuild_ub` holds for any: a handler of the rebuild with unboxing
    states of a parameter that it keeps and does not unbox what the
    handler of the plain rebuild states, rewritten at the variable, and
    nothing of the fields of one that it unboxes (`sks_rel`,
    `handler_sks_follow`). `rebuild_unbox_with_refines_rebuild` is the
    refinement for any `types`, with no premise on the rewriting;
    `rebuild_unbox_refines_rebuild` is its `None` instance. The same in
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesHeapProofs.v (`ubh`, `cont_relh`,
    `rebuild_unbox_with_refines_rebuild_heap`).
  - New statements (FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v), proved
    (FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v):
    - `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_rebuild_statement` and
      `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_rebuild_heap_statement`: the unit
      rebuilt with unboxing by the reaper with types
      (`rebuild_unit_unbox_with_types`) refines the unit rebuilt by the
      reaper with types without unboxing, with no set of closures, with
      the premises of
      FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesSpec.rebuild_unbox_refines_rebuild_statement
      and of its heap form, for any code deps.
    - `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_observably_local_statement`,
      `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_observably_statement`,
      `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local_statement` and
      `rebuild_unbox_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_statement`: the
      refinements of the unit that the rebuild with unboxing without types
      has, with the premises of the statements without types and, as for
      the rebuild with types without unboxing, the least solution and
      each continuation of the unit bound once
      (`distinct (FlambdaStaticsInScopeSpec.bound_continuations e)`).
      They compose the statements above with the refinements of the
      rebuild with types without unboxing
      (`rebuild_with_types_refines_up_to_heap_local`, and
      `rebuild_with_types_refines_observably_local`, a new lemma, the
      refinement up to observation of the rebuild with types with the
      premises of FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.rebuild_refines_observably_local).
    No premise added beyond those of the statements without types and of
    the rebuild with types; no statement without types changed in
    meaning. No fault found.
  - Not done: the unboxing with types of units with calls or code. The
    simulations of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCalls{Sim,}Proofs.v (`uc`)
    and FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosures{Sim,}Proofs.v (`rr`), and the
    statements built on them (the `_fixed`, `_over*`, `_flow*`, `_nr_ind`,
    `_unit_*` and `_repr_*` ones, the last those of the test tool's mode),
    still run with `None`; they ask that the code, the handlers and the
    wrappers of the second run state nothing (`code_frame`, `wrap_u_ok`,
    `wrap_both_ok`, `uc_let_cont`). With types they would need: what code
    of the second run states of its parameters to hold of the values that
    it is given where that of the first run holds (code that keeps its
    calling convention states the same, `kept_code_metadata`; code that
    changes states the same of a parameter kept and nothing of the fields
    of one unboxed or of an unboxed `my_closure`: `sks_rel` at the shapes
    of its parameters); the same of the wrappers of calls of code that
    keeps its calling convention (the second run's states nothing where
    the continuation unboxes); that the rewriting state nothing of a
    parameter with no use, where the second run may give poison (`Psh_dead`:
    `Keep_unused` and the arguments of the mode of the fix), as
    `rewrite_kind_with_subkind` does (the metadata of a node with no use
    erases); and the test of a subkind on the same value under the two
    static environments of the runs, which differ there.

    FlambdaReaperUnboxing: the rebuilds (`rebuild_expr_u`,
    `rebuild_handler_u`, `rebuild_binding_u`, `rebuild_code_u`,
    `rebuild_apply_u`, `make_apply_wrapper_u`, `rebuild_fails_u` and the

    new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The rebuild on the second machine of the core for a unit that makes a
  direct call of a closure of a newer version of the code that it names
  (an aged call), under a relation sound for each closure so called:
  found false. `aged_call_sound_counterexample_statement`
  (FlambdaReaperCodeAgeSpec.v, proved in FlambdaReaperCodeAgeProofs.v).
  - The unit (`RecursiveAgedExample`). `f51` is a newer version of
    `f50`; `f50` returns its argument; `f51` calls its own closure
    directly, naming `f50`, with its argument, in tail position; the unit
    calls the static closure `s80` of `f51` directly, naming `f50`, with
    9. The relation is well-formed and sound (`sound_for`) for every
    closure under the statics of the unit, which every state of its run
    has after the first: `f51`, with a closure of `f51`, calls `f50`
    with that closure and its argument, and its call comes in one step to
    the state that the call of `f50` comes to (`recursive_codes_refine`).
    The unit meets the premises of
    `least_rebuild_refines_observably_statement` and
    `least_rebuild_cc_auto_refines_observably_statement` for the least
    solution, and goes wrong on `step`, so those statements hold of it and
    say nothing.
  - On the machine the unit exits with 9: its call runs `f50` with `s80`.
    The flow finds `f51` at both calls (only `s80` reaches the
    `my_closure` of `f51`), and the rebuild names `f51` at both
    (`rewrite_call_kind`): the rebuilt unit enters `f51`, which calls
    itself without end. So do the rebuild with changes of calling
    convention (where `f51` loses its parameter) and the rebuild with the
    table of the fix of bug 12 (`age_fixed_table`). None of the three
    refines the unit on the machine: the unit exits and the rebuild
    diverges.
  - One replacement alone refines the unit: with its call naming `f51`
    and the call in `f51` still naming `f50`, the unit exits with 9, as
    `direct_call_newer_version_refines_statement` says of the call. With
    both calls naming `f51` it runs without end. Soundness does not
    compose: `sound_for` compares a call of the newer code, whose own
    direct calls go by the code that they name, with a call of the older;
    the rebuild makes every aged call go by the code of its closure, the
    calls inside the newer code included.
  - Not claimed to be a fault of the compiler: whether Simplify makes such
    a unit is not shown (it meets the code id of a call with the type of
    the callee, which for `my_closure` in the newer code names the newer
    code). The finding is that a relation sound for each closure so
    called does not give the refinement of the rebuild on the machine,
    with or without changes of calling convention, with or without the
    fix.
  - Not reached: a premise under which the statement holds, and its
    proof. The premise would have to speak of the runs in which every
    aged call goes by the code of the closure, as the rebuild makes it
    go: that on those runs too the newer code refines the older for the
    closure. By an argument not checked by Rocq, with that and
    `sound_for` at every aged call that the runs come to, such a run
    refines the run of the machine (an exit of the machine's run gives
    the same exit of the other by induction on the length of the first,
    with the premise on the runs of the closure's code; an exit or an
    undefined step of the other gives the same or an undefined step of
    the machine's run by induction on the length of the second, with
    `sound_for`). The rebuild would then need the invariant of the flow
    for those runs: proved again for them, or through the unit whose aged
    calls name the code of their closure, which needs a simulation of the
    two runs (as FlambdaCopyCodeProofs.v does for copies of code) and the
    least solution of that unit related to the unit's (with `Auto` they
    differ at the nodes that `add_apply` makes for the code named).
  - Tools (FlambdaReaperCodeAgeProofs.v): `refines_by_of_step`,
    `run_by_loop`, `behaves_by_loop` and `not_exits_of_diverges`, of any
    machine; `not_refines_observably_aged_of_diverges`, `call_code_runs`
    and the tactic `loops`.
  - The head of FlambdaReaperCodeAgeSpec.v says so (its part 2, and its
    last paragraph: "Found false", "Not reached").
  - No other file changed. `make -k all` (with `claims`; 549 lines

- The closure types of a set whose representation changes, without
  `changed_unstored`: for every value, wherever the closures that change

  - What was in the way. The third run of the statements was the second
    with only the closure at the node changed (`changed_at`,
    `changed_closure_rebuilt`): what a field or a value slot held was the
    second run's value, so a closure that changes could not be held
    there (`changed_unstored`). The rewriting itself was not the
    obstacle: it rewrites the type of a field under the metadata of the
    field, and where that metadata keeps one set, which changes, it gives
    the new representation (`changed_rule`), as types_rewriter.ml does
    (`get_set_of_closures_changed_representation` in the closure case of
    `rewrite`, at any depth).

    FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v (to review): `rebuilt_changed G sol reprs v n
    v'`, the largest relation that holds one level down of what it
    relates (`rebuilt_changed_closed`) by `rebuilt_changed_step`, which is
    `rebuilt_value`'s level with the relation for what a block or a value
    slot holds, but that a closure whose allocation changes
    (`allocation_repr`: an allocation with a source that `reprs` gives a
    representation) is, in the third run, the closure with the related
    value slots in the representation of its allocation
    (`changed_closure_rebuilt`), and that a symbol has a use and does not
    change. A level relates only what the value holds, so it is the least
    such relation as well. The static environment of the third run:
    `statics_rebuilt_changed` (`static_rebuilt_with`, `static_rebuilt`
    for any relation) and `static_sets_whole_unchanged`, of the symbols
    that do not change. The static sets whose representation changes are
    left out, as before (they were never covered: the old third run had
    the second's static environment).

      `stored_type_sound_traversal`; full
      (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalUnaliasedSpec.v):
      `stored_type_sound_full`, which asks nothing of the graph; with
      `_landed`.
    - Heap (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalHeapSpec.v, new
      `rebuilt_changed_heap_step`, `rebuilt_changed_heap`,
      `statics_rebuilt_changed_heap`: a location rebuilt as `β` gives
      it): `rewrite_stored_type_sound_slots_heap`,
      `rewrite_stored_type_sound_traversal_heap`,
      `stored_type_sound_traversal_heap`, `stored_type_sound_full_heap`,
      with `_landed`.
    - `stored_example` and `stored_example_landed`
      (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec.v, `StoredExampleRuns`): the unit
      of `changed_unstored_counterexample` is of the new statements. The
      block `x10` holds the closure `x60` (slot 70 of the code 53, 7 in
      slot 80), read back into `x11`; its set changes (slot 82, value
      slot 81). The statement gives `sets_of_graph`, `check_dominated`,
      `check_unboxed_uses`, the fragment of the types, `rebuilt_changed`
      of the block at `x10` and of the closure at `x11` to those holding
      the closure in its new representation, the metadata of the names,
      the types of the first run, and the rewritten types, computed:
      the block type is the block of the closure type at 82 with `= 7`
      at 81, as is the closure type of `x11`, and they hold of the
      third run's block and closure. The other premises are met with the
      empty typing and static environments.
  - The old statements are corollaries, proved from the new ones:
    `rewrite_changed_type_sound{,_slots}{,_landed}`,
    `rewrite_changed_type_sound_traversal{,_landed}`,
    `changed_type_sound_traversal{,_landed}`,
    `changed_type_sound_full{,_landed}` (section `Unstored` of
    FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v: with `changed_unstored`, what
    `rebuilt_value` relates at a node held in a field other than a
    function slot is related so by `rebuilt_changed` (`held_closed`), and
    so are the static environments (`statics_changed_of_unstored`,
    `sets_whole_of_unstored`) and the closure of `changed_at`
    (`rebuilt_changed_of_runs`)). The old heap statements keep their
    proofs, from the old graph statements, now corollaries.
  - The proof. Section `Changed_sound` is generalized in place, not
    copied: `Hunst` is gone, `Hst` and `Hwhole` are of the third run;
    `rel_u`'s `unchanged_ext` relates a value other than the closure of a
    set by `rebuilt_changed` at a node (`not_set_closure`), or a closure
    whose allocation does not change rebuilt with its set, its value
    slots related by `rebuilt_changed`; `changed_ext` likewise.
    `rel_of_rc` puts a value at a node in one of the two, so a field or a
    value slot is related by `rel_u` under the metadata that holds where
    it is held, changed or not (it was `unchanged_field`, from
    `changed_unstored`). The one-level lemmas are proved for
    `rebuilt_changed` (`rebuilt_changed_unfold`/`_fold`, `_kind`,
    `rc_block_inv`, `rc_boxed_inv`, `block_rebuilt_u`,
    `boxed_rebuilt_u`, `string_rebuilt_u`, `closure_rebuilt_view_u`).
    A value slot unboxed in turn: `leaves_sound_rc` (section
    `Leaves_changed`, a copy of `Leaves_with` for `rebuilt_changed`, not
    an edit of it). The heap forms by erasure
    (`rebuilt_changed_heap_erase`: the erased pairs are closed under the
    step, `erased_closed`).
  - Left: the static sets whose representation changes (the step asks a
    symbol not to change; the third run would bind it in the new
    representation, which `statics_rebuilt_changed` does not describe);
    the simulation of the changes of representation for closures held
    in fields, whose third run `rebuilt_changed` is to relate (the
    simulation of FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v is of

  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 609 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`,
    20 new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The closure types of the static sets whose representation changes: the
  third run binds the symbols of such a set to the closures of the set in
  the new representation, and the rewritten types hold of them
  (FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v and its proofs, with the statements of the
  traversal, of the full traversal and of the heap). No counterexample:
  the stronger statements hold.
  - What was in the way. `rebuilt_changed` asks a symbol not to change
    (`allocation_repr (Node_symbol s) = None`), and
    `statics_rebuilt_changed` says nothing of a symbol whose allocation
    changes. Both rebuilds bind such a set whole in the new
    representation, used or not (FlambdaReaperUnboxing.static_set_repr,
    `rebuild_changed_static_const`; rebuild.ml,
    `rebuild_let_expr_static_consts`): the closure at the new slot, the
    function declarations and the closure symbols at the new function
    slots, the new value slots. types_rewriter.ml rewrites the type of
    such a symbol as that of any closure whose set changes
    (`get_set_of_closures_changed_representation`), into the closure type
    of the new representation, under the metadata of the symbol
    (`metadata_for_name`: `Single_source` of it where it has a use).
  - New definitions, FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v (to review):
    `rename_closure_symbols` (the closure symbols at the new slots, as
    the rebuild has them); `rebuilt_static_changed_step`,
    `rebuilt_static_changed_closed`, `rebuilt_static_changed`
    (`rebuilt_changed_step`, but that a symbol may change: it is itself,

    r s allocated statics statics'` (`s` a symbol of a static set whose

    (FlambdaClosuresSpec.allocated_valid); and each symbol `s_g` of the

    `changed_closure_rebuilt` gives of `vs_mid`);
    `statics_rebuilt_static_changed` (every symbol with a use: one that
    does not change as `statics_rebuilt_changed` says, one that changes

    `rebuilt_static_changed_heap_step`, `rebuilt_static_changed_heap`,
    `statics_rebuilt_static_changed_heap` (a location rebuilt as `β`
    gives it).
  - New statements, all proved, each with `_landed`:
    - Graph: `rewrite_static_type_sound` (representations with no value
      slot) and `rewrite_static_type_sound_slots`: the premises of

      and `statics_rebuilt_changed` (`static_sets_whole_unchanged` kept;

    - Traversal (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec.v):
      `rewrite_static_type_sound_traversal`, `static_type_sound_traversal`;
      full (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalUnaliasedSpec.v):
      `static_type_sound_full`, which asks nothing of the graph.
    - Heap (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalHeapSpec.v):
      `rewrite_static_type_sound_slots_heap`,
      `rewrite_static_type_sound_traversal_heap`,
      `static_type_sound_traversal_heap`, `static_type_sound_full_heap`.
    - `static_example` (FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalSpec.v,
      `StaticExample`): the unit FlambdaReaperUnboxing.Examples.static_set,
      whose symbol 90 is the closure at the function slot 70 of a static

      binds 90 to that closure; the run of the unit rebuilt with unboxing
      in the mode of the fix, computed, binds it to the closure at 82 with

      formed, binding each variable once, `sets_of_graph`,
      `check_dominated`, `check_unboxed_uses`, `statics_allocated`,
      `statics_rebuilt_static_changed` and `static_sets_whole_unchanged` of

      metadata of the symbol, `Single_source` of it, under which its type

      holds of the symbol in the first run; rewritten by

      `= 7` at 81, which holds of the symbol in the third.
  - The old statements keep their proofs, from the same induction:
    `rewrite_stored_type_sound{,_slots}{,_landed}` and those derived from
    them. They are not corollaries of the new ones: their static
    environment says nothing of the symbols that change, which the new
    ones ask of it.
  - The proof (FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v). The sections
    `Leaves_changed` and `Changed_sound` are generalized in place, not
    copied, over a relation `RC` of the first run to the third that holds
    one level down of what it relates as `rebuilt_static_changed_step`
    says (`HRC`; section `Rebuilt_with`: `rw_kind`, `rw_block_inv`,
    `rw_boxed_inv`, ...), with the static environment of the third run as
    `statics_rebuilt_changed` says of the symbols that do not change
    (`Hst`) and as `static_set_changed_rebuilt` says of a symbol that `RC`
    relates whose allocation changes (`Hst_ch`). `rebuilt_changed` is
    such a relation (`rebuilt_changed_static_closed`), relating no symbol
    that changes (`statics_changed_none`): the old statements are that
    instance. `rebuilt_static_changed` is another
    (`rebuilt_static_changed_unfold`, `statics_static_none`,
    `statics_static_some`): the new ones (`static_type_sound_gen`). In
    `rel_u`, a closure that changes is a pair of the two runs
    (`changed_pair`): of a set made by a `Let`, as before; of a static
    set, the symbol, its set bound whole in the new representation.
    `rel_of_rc` puts a symbol that `RC` relates there when it changes and
    is bound to the closure of a set, and otherwise among the values that
    do not change (`not_changed_symbol`, a new conjunct of
    `unchanged_ext`; a symbol bound to anything else does not change,
    `symbol_not_set_unchanged`). `changed_pair_view` gives the two forms
    one view, which `changed_sound` uses: the closure in the new
    representation, and at the new slot of each function slot of the set
    the closure of the first run at that slot, in the representation of
    its allocation, the closure symbols renamed injectively
    (`lookup_rename_symbols`). The heap forms by erasure (section
    `Static_erase` of FlambdaReaperTypesTraversalHeapProofs.v:
    `erased_static_closed`, `statics_static_heap_erase`).
  - Left: the simulation of the changes of representation for closures
    held in fields and for static sets, whose third run
    `rebuilt_changed` and `rebuilt_static_changed` are to relate (that of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v is of a fragment with

  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 629 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`,
    20 new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The unboxing with changes of representation of sets of closures, on a
  wider fragment: beside the sets whose representation changes, code that
  keeps its calling convention and the direct calls of it, raising from
  code, parameters of handlers and results of code kept with no use, and
  extra arguments of the exception continuation of calls
  (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresCalls{Spec,Proofs}.v, new; the
  construction in FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v and the
  simulation in FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v
  extended). No counterexample: the wider statements hold, and the old
  ones are unchanged in name, type and meaning.
  - The route: `rr` extended, not merged into `uc`. `uc` is destructed by
    the proofs of the Over/Reach/Never files and its construction is used
    by FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v; adding the class of changed names to it
    would change its signature everywhere. In the cases added, the two
    rebuilds make the same term (no wrapper), so `rr` needs no new case
    but the frame of an exception continuation with extra arguments.
  - The fragment (`repr_calls_unboxing_nr`, `repr_expr_w`, to review) is
    that of `repr_unboxing_nr`, but:
    - The decisions of a continuation are read as the two rebuilds read
      them (`normed`): a parameter kept with no use (`Keep_unused`) is
      kept, as one with a use (`get_args`, `params_of_decisions` and
      `decisions_subkinds` treat the two alike). So a handler may keep a
      parameter with no use.
    - Code that keeps its calling convention (`repr_code_w`, case
      `Not_changing_u`): not tupled, with a code dep, its results not
      unboxed (the return continuation has no decision in the first
      rebuild and those of a continuation of fixed arity in the second,
      each read as kept), plain parameters, its `my_closure` not unboxed;
      code of `nr` with its return continuation not live.
    - Raising: an `Apply_cont` (or an arm) to the exception continuation
      of the code it is in, with plain arguments, if the exception of the
      code is not unboxed (`code_exn_ds`, from `code_exn_decision`).
    - Calls (`repr_call_w`) of code that keeps its calling convention:
      direct or of known arity, at the arities of the code, by a callee of
      the class of its `my_closure`, the arguments at the same mask in both
      rebuilds (`kept_args_ok` of the calls fragment), returning to a
      continuation that keeps every result (or out of the unit), or of
      code of `nr`. Calls of code that changes its calling convention as
      in `repr_call`.
    - A call may give extra arguments to its exception continuation if
      that is a handler of the term (`extra_args_ok`): plain, both
      rebuilds giving those that the handler keeps.
  - The simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v):
    `call_ok_c` and `rr_call` ask the extra arguments plain in place of
    none; new `cont_rel_extra` (the frame `Extra_args` of both runs, with
    the same values); `cont_answered` by induction on `cont_rel`;
    `call_entered` by `step_apply`, the frame of the exception
    continuation of the code being `with_extra_args` of the call's
    (`cont_rel_with_extra`).
  - The construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v,
    section `Rebuilds`, now on `repr_expr_w`): `cinv` describes a
    continuation that may be applied by its decisions in the second
    rebuild, `normed`, and in the first by their mask or, for the return
    continuation of code that keeps its calling convention, by no mask
    with every parameter kept; the exception continuation of code that may
    be raised to likewise. `call_rebuilt` and `code_rebuilt` have the case
    of code that keeps its calling convention (the first rebuild's
    `rebuild_apply` and the second's `kept_arguments_u` give the same
    arguments, `make_apply_wrapper_none`; the code the same parameters
    and `kept_code_metadata` in both); `extra_rebuilt` the extra
    arguments; the handler lemmas take `normed` decisions. New section
    `Within`: the old fragment is within the new one (`repr_within`,
    `repr_within_unit`). `rebuild_repr_modes_with_refines_cc` (used by
    FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.v) keeps its type and is now derived from
    `rebuild_repr_calls_modes_with_refines_cc`, the lemma on the new
    fragment for any `types`.
  - New statements (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresCallsSpec.v), all
    proved, with the premises of the statements they widen and the new
    fragment in place of `repr_unboxing_nr` (no premise added):
    - `repr_calls_holds_repr_statement`: `repr_unboxing_nr` implies
      `repr_calls_unboxing_nr`, for the same tables and `nr`.
    - `rebuild_repr_calls_refines_cc_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_calls_observably_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_calls_local_statement`: those of
      `rebuild_repr_fixed_{refines_cc,observably,local}_never_returns`.
    - `rebuild_repr_calls_with_types_refines_cc_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_calls_with_types_local_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_calls_with_types_observably_statement`: those of
      `rebuild_repr_fixed_with_types_{refines_cc,local,observably}_nr`
      (FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v), with types; code that keeps its calling
      convention states the same in both rebuilds.
    - `repr_calls_kept_code_example_statement` (`ClosuresCallsExample`,
      computed): the unit of `TwoFieldsExample` (the set of `x60` changes
      its representation, the block in its slot unboxed into two new
      slots) with the code 56, whose closure the unit returns in a block,
      so that it keeps its calling convention (`cannot_change` of the
      rules); 56 is called directly under a handler of its exception, to
      which the call gives an extra argument, and raises in an arm that is
      not taken. Least solution: of `repr_calls_unboxing_nr`, not of
      `repr_unboxing`; the rebuild in the mode of the fix is the term
      `rebuilt`; both return the block of 48 and the closure of 56; the
      rebuilt unit refines the unit up to observation.
  - Left: calls whose code is not known (a closure held by a plain name
    could be one whose code reads its `my_closure` in the new
    representation, which needs the flow, as `RCH` for `uc`), wrappers
    (the two rebuilds make different wrappers; their frames would need a
    pending relation, as `uc`'s `related_pending`), unboxed parameters
    (the shapes of `uc` for arguments and parameters), and extra
    arguments for an exception continuation that is not a handler.
  - Nothing found false.
  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 637 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 8
    new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The rebuilds with unboxing on the second machine of the core
  (`step_aged`).
  - The unboxing statements on the machine
    (`FlambdaReaperCodeAgeUnboxingSpec.v` and
    `FlambdaReaperCodeAgeUnboxingProofs.v`, new; built). Each statement of
    the rebuilds with unboxing, `FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefines*Spec.v` and
    the `_with_types` ones of `FlambdaReaperTypesSpec.v`, is stated again
    with `aged_` in the place of `rebuild_` and one premise more: the
    source of its refinement does not go wrong on `step`
    (`program_defined`). Its refinement then holds on `step_aged` for
    every `age_of` (`program_refines_aged`,
    `program_refines_observably_aged`, and `refines_up_to_heap_aged` for
    the comparison up to the heap). 51 liftings: local unboxing (3), with
    the heap (3), across calls in the mode of the fix with and without
    code taken to never return (6), `_nr_ind` (3), `_unit_flow3` and
    `_unit_never` (6), changes of representation (6), on the wider
    fragment with and without types (6), and the reaper with types (18);
    and `refines_up_to_heap_aged_of_defined_statement`. Each is the
    statement composed with `refines_aged_of_defined_statement`,
    `refines_observably_aged_of_defined_statement` or the new one; none
    needs more. The source of `_refines_cc` and `_refines_rebuild` is the
    unit rebuilt without unboxing, and the premise is of it. The `_tail_`
    names are the same statements and are not repeated. What the premise
    leaves out is a unit that runs a direct call of a closure of a newer
    version of the code named (an aged call).
  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 689 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`,
    52 new) and `make assumptions` pass.
- The unboxing with changes of representation of sets of closures, with
  calls whose code is not known: of known arity, of unknown arity with
  one group of arguments, returning or never returning, the code that
  the flow of the first rebuilt unit brings to the callee having its
  `my_closure` plain (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresReach{Spec,
  Proofs}.v, new; the construction in
  FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v and the simulation in
  FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v extended). No
  counterexample: the wider statements hold, and the old ones are
  unchanged in name, type and meaning.
  - What was in the way. A call whose code is not known calls the code of
    whatever closure its callee holds. In `rr` a plain name holds the same
    value in both runs, never a closure in the new representation (a
    changed name flows to no plain name), so the code called must read its
    `my_closure` as the first run does: its `my_closure` must not change.
    That is a fact of the run, which the flow gives, as `RCH` does for
    `uc`.
  - The route: `rr` extended again, not `uc`. `rr` takes the flow `RCH b f`
    (`b`: of known arity, the code of a closure that `f` holds; otherwise
    that of the closure that a call of it calls, partial applications
    included), and has one more case, `rr_call_ind`: a call of a plain
    variable `f`, not direct, the same in both terms, plain arguments and
    extra arguments, with `ind_targets` (known arity: every code of
    `RCH true f` that the unit binds has its `my_closure` plain; unknown
    arity: one group of arguments, every code of `RCH false f` has its
    `my_closure` plain and a parameter). The three cases of calls of code
    that never returns now take `never_ok`: a direct call of such code as
    before, or a call of a plain variable whose code is not known, every
    code of `RCH` being such code with its `my_closure` plain and, for
    unknown arity, one parameter (`ind_never`), so that the call is a full
    call.
  - The simulation (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSimProofs.v):
    `flow_ok` of each state of the first run at a call of `f` (a partial
    application that `f` holds is bounded as the machine makes it,
    `PGP.pa_bound`; the code of its closures in `RCH`), `flow_run`,
    `rr_refines_flow` (the relation and `flow_run` of the first state),
    `rr_refines` where `RCH` holds everywhere (`flow_run_total`, from
    `PGP.pa_inv_run`). New lemmas: `call_code_entered` (the entry of the
    code of a full call, factored out of `call_entered`, which now uses
    it), `closure_shape_rel` (the same closure called in both runs: the
    same shape, of related code), `ind_closure_answered`,
    `ind_call_step`, `partial_one_no_over_b` (the lemma of the calls
    simulation on the bound of the partial application alone: the flow
    proofs and the partial groups proofs have two different `pa_ok`),
    `known_arity_full_shape`, `closure_call_full`,
    `never_closure_entered`, `never_entered` (in place of `call_entered`
    in the three never cases). `related_answered` takes `flow_ok`. Used
    from FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSimProofs.v: the lemmas
    `known_arity_no_over` and `closure_one_no_over` only.
  - The fragment (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresReachSpec.v, to
    review): `repr_reach_unboxing_nr` for any `reach : bool -> variable
    -> code_id -> bool`, and `repr_flow_unboxing_nr`, with `reach` read
    off the first rebuilt unit analysed again (`flow_reach2 F1 T1` of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2Spec.v). It is
    `repr_calls_unboxing_nr` (`repr_expr_r` and the functions with it are
    copies of `repr_expr_w` and its own) with `repr_call_r`, which is
    `repr_call_w` or `ind_call_r`: a call whose code is not known
    (`code_id_actually_called` gives none), not direct, of a variable `f`
    neither unboxed nor changed; plain arguments, read at the same mask
    by both rebuilds (`call_mask` of the solution and of the solution
    without the unboxed names); `extra_args_ok`; then, if the call
    `apply_never_returns`, every code of `reach` is code of `nr` (one
    parameter for unknown arity); otherwise its continuation keeps every
    result (`returns_ok`), so that neither rebuild makes a wrapper; and
    every code of `reach b f` has its `my_closure` plain
    (`plain_my_closure`), a parameter for unknown arity, which has one
    group of arguments.
  - The construction (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v):
    section `Rebuilds` on `repr_expr_r`, with `reach` and `RCHf`; new
    `rewrite_call_kind_ind` (both rebuilds keep the kind, the list of code
    ids of a call of known arity aside), `ind_call_rebuilt` (both
    rebuilds: `rebuild_apply` and `kept_arguments_u` give the arguments
    at the same mask; no wrapper, `kept_mask_none`; or the `Invalid`
    handler of the fix and any wrapper of the first, for a call that never
    returns), `call_rebuilt_r`; the never cases of `call_rebuilt` give
    `never_ok`. Section `Within`: `code_within_r`, `w_within_r`;
    `repr_calls_within_reach_unit`. New
    `rebuild_repr_reach_modes_with_refines_cc` (any `types`, any `reach`,
    with `flow_run` of the first rebuilt unit);
    `rebuild_repr_calls_modes_with_refines_cc` keeps its type and is now
    its instance where the flow may bring any code anywhere.
  - New statements (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresReachSpec.v), all
    proved (...ClosuresReachProofs.v):
    - `repr_reach_holds_calls_statement`: `repr_calls_unboxing_nr`
      implies `repr_reach_unboxing_nr`, for any `reach`.
    - `rebuild_repr_reach_refines_cc_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_reach_observably_statement`,
      `rebuild_repr_reach_local_statement`: those of
      `rebuild_repr_calls_{refines_cc,observably,local}` with
      `repr_flow_unboxing_nr` and the premises of
      FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesReach2Spec.v on the first rebuilt unit

      its tables `T1`).
    - `rebuild_repr_reach_with_types_refines_cc_statement`,

    - `repr_reach_ind_call_example_statement` (`ClosuresReachExample`,

    - The flow of the first rebuilt unit (`first_flow`, with or without

  - Premises added: those of the Reach2 statements on the first rebuilt

  - Seen while making the example: when the two closures do not escape,

    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresReachSpec.v depend on no axiom

- The types applied end to end to the runs of the unboxing across calls
  and of the changes of representation, and the result types of code with
  no premise on the runs (FlambdaReaperTypesCalls{Spec,Proofs}.v,
  FlambdaReaperTypesCallsExitProofs.v and
  FlambdaReaperTypesReprExitProofs.v, new). No counterexample: the
  statements hold.
  - What was in the way. The statements of result types
    (`rewrite_result_type_sound`, `rewrite_unboxed_result_type_sound` and
    their forms) took the relation of the values of the runs at the
    variables of code as a premise; end to end, the types were applied
    only to the runs of the plain rebuild and, with the heap, of the local
    unboxing. The simulations of the rebuild with changes of calling
    convention (FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.related), of the unboxing
    across calls (`uc`, `related2`) and of the changes of representation
    (`rr`) relate the states of the runs; what they relate is now read off
    them, for any state that the run of the unit comes to, not only its
    exit.
  - The states (FlambdaReaperTypesCallsExitProofs.v,
    FlambdaReaperTypesReprExitProofs.v): `shrinking_reaches` (in a
    shrinking simulation, each state that the first run comes to and
    from which it steps is related to one that the second comes to) and
    `reach_of_located` (the same for the relation of
    FlambdaReaperRebuildProofs.v under the allocations of the locations).
    `cc_reach`: the unit and its rebuild with changes of calling
    convention, for any table closed under the rules, the closures made
    where the graph has only the facts of their sets; the hypotheses of
    the section `Run` are discharged as
    `rebuild_cc_with_refines_checked_local` does (its proof up to its
    last step, copied). `rebuild_unbox_fixed_uc`: the two rebuilt terms
    related by `uc` (the proof of
    `rebuild_unbox_modes_with_refines_cc_never` up to its last step,
    copied, about 200 lines, so as not to edit that file; splitting the
    lemma there would remove the copy); `uc_of_calls` (the fragment of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesCallsSpec.v, with the invariants of the
    first run that `uc` takes), `uc_reach`, `uc_exit`. `repr_calls_rr`:
    the two rebuilt terms of the fragment of
    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresCallsSpec.v related by `rr`,
    whose flow is any (`flow_run_total`); `rr_reach`, `rr_exit`, with the
    invariant of the flow of the first run that `rr` takes (`rr_rel`).
  - A lemma split (FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresProofs.v):
    `rebuild_repr_reach_modes_with_rr` is the proof of
    `rebuild_repr_reach_modes_with_refines_cc` up to its last step: the two
    rebuilt terms of the fragment `repr_reach_unboxing_nr` are related by
    `rr`, whose flow is as `reach` says. The old lemma keeps its name and
    type and is now derived from it by `rr_refines_flow`;
    FlambdaReaperTypesReprExitProofs.v uses it, in place of a copy of
    that proof (about 170 lines, removed).
  - What the proofs read off them (FlambdaReaperTypesCallsProofs.v):
    `related_facts` (`statics_rebuilt` by
    FlambdaReaperTypesProofs.statics_rel_rebuilt, `statics_allocated`
    from the invariant of the flow, a variable with a use bound in the
    first run bound in the second to what `rebuilt_value` relates to it,
    by `rel_rebuilt_value`, the expression of the second the rebuild of
    the first's), `cc_states`, `calls_states`, `calls_exit`,
    `repr_exit`. The second and third runs bind the same static values
    and other code; a type satisfied under the static values alone
    (`values_only`) is satisfied under any environment that binds them
    (`type_satisfied_values_only`, by `type_satisfied_statics_extend`),
    and `statics_rebuilt` reads only those (`statics_rebuilt_values`).
  - New statements (FlambdaReaperTypesCallsSpec.v), all proved, each with
    `_landed`, `_local` and `_local_landed`:
    - `unbox_calls_types_end_to_end`: the statement of
      `types_end_to_end_term` of the unit rebuilt with unboxing across
      calls (`rebuild_unit_unbox_fixed`, the fragment `calls_unboxing_nr`
      for any `nr`, a table that passes `check_cc_closed` and
      `check_apply_widgets`): where the unit exits, the rebuilt unit exits

    - `repr_calls_types_end_to_end`: the same of the unit rebuilt with
      unboxing and changes of representation
      (`rebuild_unit_unbox_repr_fixed`, the fragment
      `repr_calls_unboxing_nr`): `rr` binds the same static values in the

    - `result_type_end_to_end` (plain rebuild) and
      `cc_result_type_end_to_end` (rebuild with changes of calling
      convention): `rewrite_result_type_sound` with no premise on the

    - `unbox_calls_result_type_end_to_end`: the same of the unit rebuilt

    - `unbox_calls_example` (`UnboxCallsExample`, computed): the unit of
      FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesSpec.CallExample, whose code 50 reads
      field 1 of its parameter `x40`, called with the pair `[7; 8]`, with
      its tables (the least solution, `check_least`), the set of `x60`,
      result types giving the variable 90 the block of tag 0 whose field 1
      is `= 8`: it meets the premises of
      `calls_unboxed_result_type_end_to_end` at the `Let` of `x43` in the
      code (the unit exits, so it does not go wrong), the rules unbox

    Two names shortened: `unbox_calls_result_end_to_end_local_landed`,
    `calls_unboxed_result_end_to_end_local_landed`.
  - Premises added, and why. Of a variable of the result types: that it
    has a use (the rebuild keeps no other; the change of calling
    conventions deletes a parameter with no use, whose type it drops) and
    that the run of the unit steps from the state. For the third run: the
    unit does not go wrong (so that the run of its first rebuild, which
    refines it observably, does not either, and `uc` reaches a state
    related to that of the first rebuild), the state is at a `Let` of a
    variable with a use, and the variable is a variable of the unit (not
    one of the fields). `uc` relates the states within a wrapper of both
    runs, about to apply the continuation of a call (`related_pending`),
    with no environment; the rebuild keeps a `Let` of a variable with a
    use (`rebuild_let_used`), so the state of the second run there is a
    `Let` (or `Invalid`, which goes wrong), which `uc` relates with the
    environments (`uc_let`).
  - Left (the header of FlambdaReaperTypesCallsSpec.v says so):
    - The results of code: the model's traversal gives them `Node_made`

      and `rewrite_result_type` reads the metadata of a variable; the

      of code among them).
    - The closure types of the sets whose representation changes, of the

    - The fragment `loose`: `static_sets_whole` of the rebuild with

    - The states within a wrapper of both runs, for the third run; the

  - Nothing found false. The model files and the existing statements are
    unchanged; rocq/Makefile lists the four new files, and

  - `make -k all` (with `claims`: 723 lines `proves FlambdaReaper`, 26

    FlambdaReaperUnboxingRefinesClosuresSpec.v,
