## Choices to review

- The flag `-reaper-preserve-direct-calls` is to be removed from the
  compiler, keeping its mode `auto` (a change proposed for the reaper):
  only `Auto` is stated from now on. The statements that hold of every
  flag keep it; `check_apply_widgets`, which only the modes `always` and
  `never` need (`widgets_counterexample`), and the counterexamples under
  those modes stay as they are, not extended.
- Resolved: the call kinds. The model had one indirect call kind, read
  as one of unknown arity, so that the graph of the compiler's call of
  known arity was not the model's. The core now has the compiler's
  kinds, the transcriptions follow the compiler on each, and the
  statements cover calls of known arity (see "Done").
- A direct call is read as one of known arity. The statements are
  for units whose direct calls are full and call code that is not
  tupled (a check of the term), as the compiler makes them: the machine
  gives tupled code a tuple at every call, and the compiler's direct
  call of tupled code, which gives it the fields, is not in the model.

## Asked of other streams

Not yet written as requests; for the human to say whether they are.

- Of C0, a row of `well_formed_unit` (rule 5 of
  `requests/C0-well-formed.md`: a condition the compiler's units always
  meet is a row, not a premise). Each continuation of the unit is given
  one value: an `Apply_cont` to one gives one argument, a call that
  returns to one has a return arity of one value, and a call whose
  exception goes to one gives no extra argument. The compiler builds
  the unit's return continuation with one parameter, the module block,
  and checks nothing more. S7 keeps it as its own premise,
  `exits_one_value`, until the row exists; two counterexamples show it
  needed.
- Of C0, a decision for the human. `results_kinded` is a condition on
  kinds, which rule 5 says is never a premise; but without it the
  rebuild is not a refinement: the wrapper it gives a call tests the
  kinds of what the code returns, and the machine does not. The two
  ways: the machine makes a return of values of other kinds than the
  call's return arity undefined (a change to the core, after which
  `results_kinded` goes and `wrapper_kinds_counterexample` is a unit
  with no behaviour to refine), or S7 keeps the premise as an exception
  to the rule. The first is recommended: it is what the compiler
  does, which never tests the kinds of returned values. The proof for
  calls that return to a handler uses only the part of
  `results_kinded` that compares the return arity of a call with the
  parameters of its handler.
- Of the core, a call that may raise on its own, as the runtime makes
  one raise `Sys.Break` or `Out_of_memory`: an exception that the
  machine may give any call's exception continuation, which the
  statements of the rebuild would then cover; the traversal already
  gives the exception parameter of every handler a source.
- Of V2, whose file `flsemantics.ml` is, two additions that the tool
  of S7 uses: `Numbering.set`, which gives a name a number of the
  model's, and `poison_missing_callee`, off by default, under which a
  call with no callee, as the reaper makes of a call whose callee it
  unboxes, is converted with a poison callee rather than refused.
- Of the core, a direct call with no callee, as the reaper makes of a
  call whose callee it unboxes: the machine takes the closure from the
  callee and finds such a call undefined, where the compiler calls the
  code with no closure; the rebuild with unboxing of closures is
  undefined for that reason alone on units that the compiler
  compiles.
- Of the core, a binding of every closure of a set at once, as the
  compiler's `Set_of_closures` pattern: the model binds the first and
  projects the others from it, and the traversal then adds, for each,
  an accessor fact that the compiler's graph has not.
- Of the core, the code age relation: `newer_version_of` in the
  metadata of code; a direct call that runs the code it names or any
  newer version of it (the expected semantics, from the user); the list
  of a call of known arity read up to newer versions (in place of
  decision D59); a function type that means its code or a newer version.
  The reaper changes the calling convention of each code on its own and
  keeps the relation, so the export can relate two versions with
  different conventions. S7 waits for the core rather than model it.
- Of the core, regions in the base machine, or the statements of S7
  carried to the machine with regions: units with regions are covered
  only vacuously.
- Of C4, a meaning of `Mutable_block` types that reads the cell of a
  location: the statements of types with the heap say nothing of
  environments that use it.
- Of the core, the two call kinds `Indirect_known_arity` and
  `Indirect_unknown_arity`: satisfied (`FlambdaModel.call_kind`), and
  taken up by S7 (see "Done").
